\underset{Z\in\operatorname{Sch}_{/{\mathcal{Y}}}}{\operatorname{colim}}\,\operatorname{Shv}(Z\underset{B}{\times}X)\simeq\underset{Z^{\prime}\in\operatorname{Sch}_{/{\mathcal{Y}}\underset{B}{\times}X}}{\operatorname{colim}}\,\operatorname{Shv}(Z^{\prime}).

This follows from the fact that the functor

\operatorname{Sch}_{/{\mathcal{Y}}}\to\operatorname{Sch}_{/{\mathcal{Y}}\underset{B}{\times}X},\quad Z\mapsto Z\underset{B}{\times}X

is cofinal. Indeed, it has a left adjoint given by

(Z^{\prime}\to{\mathcal{Y}}\underset{B}{\times}Y)\mapsto(Z^{\prime}\to{\mathcal{Y}}).

∎

3.2.7.

As above, for any
f:{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\to{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}
, we have a map

f_{\operatorname{co},*}:\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})_{\operatorname{co}}\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2})_{\operatorname{co}},

and when
f
is schematic also a map

f_{\operatorname{co}}^{!}:\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2})_{\operatorname{co}}\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})_{\operatorname{co}}.

When we evaluate the above functors on
X\in\operatorname{Sch}
, in terms of the identification of Lemma
3.2.6
,
we recover the corresponding functors from Sect.
3.2.2
for

{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\underset{B}{\times}X\overset{f\times\operatorname{id}}{\to}{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}\underset{B}{\times}X.

3.2.8.

We define the functor

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(-)_{\operatorname{co}}:\operatorname{Corr}_{\operatorname{all,sch}}(\operatorname{PreStk}_{/B})\to\operatorname{AGCat}_{/B},

to be the left Kan extension of

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(-):\operatorname{Corr}(\operatorname{Sch}_{/B})\to\operatorname{AGCat}_{/B}

along

\operatorname{Corr}(\operatorname{Sch}_{/B})\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Corr}_{\operatorname{all,sch}}(\operatorname{PreStk}_{/B}).

As above, the value of the above left Kan extension on
{\mathcal{Y}}
recovers
\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}}
.

3.3.
The
right-lax
symmetric monoidal structure(s)

3.3.1.

By
[
AGKRRV1
, Theorem B.1.3]
(or a special case of
[
GR1
, Chapter 9, Proposition 3.2.4]
),
the functor
Equation

3.2
is naturally right-lax symmetric monoidal.

Concretely, this means that for a pair of prestacks
{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}
over
B
, we have a canonically defined map

(3.3)

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})\otimes\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2})\overset{\boxtimes}{\to}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\underset{B}{\times}{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}).

3.3.2.

Explicitly, the map (
3.3
) is given by

(3.4)

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})\otimes\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2})\simeq\left(\underset{X_{1}\in(\operatorname{Sch}_{/{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}})^{\operatorname{op}}}{\operatorname{lim}}\,\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(X_{1})\right)\otimes\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2})\overset{\rm{(A)}}{\to}\\
\to\underset{X_{1}\in(\operatorname{Sch}_{/{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}})^{\operatorname{op}}}{\operatorname{lim}}\,\left(\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(X_{1})\otimes\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2})\right)\simeq\underset{X_{1}\in(\operatorname{Sch}_{/{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}})^{\operatorname{op}}}{\operatorname{lim}}\,\left(\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(X_{1})\otimes\left(\underset{X_{2}\in(\operatorname{Sch}_{/{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}})^{\operatorname{op}}}{\operatorname{lim}}\,\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(X_{2})\right)\right)\overset{\rm{(B)}}{\to}\\
\to\underset{X_{1}\in(\operatorname{Sch}_{/{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}})^{\operatorname{op}}}{\operatorname{lim}}\,\left(\underset{X_{2}\in(\operatorname{Sch}_{/{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}})^{\operatorname{op}}}{\operatorname{lim}}\,\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(X_{1})\otimes\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(X_{2})\right)\simeq\\
\simeq\underset{X_{1}\in(\operatorname{Sch}_{/{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}})^{\operatorname{op}},X_{2}\in(\operatorname{Sch}_{/{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}})^{\operatorname{op}}}{\operatorname{lim}}\,\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(X_{1})\otimes\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(X_{2})\simeq\underset{X_{1}\in(\operatorname{Sch}_{/{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}})^{\operatorname{op}},X_{2}\in(\operatorname{Sch}_{/{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}})^{\operatorname{op}}}{\operatorname{lim}}\,\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(X_{1}\underset{B}{\times}X_{2})\simeq\\
\simeq\underset{X\in(\operatorname{Sch}_{/{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\underset{B}{\times}{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}})^{\operatorname{op}}}{\operatorname{lim}}\,\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(X)=\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\underset{B}{\times}{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}),

where the last isomorphism is due to the fact that the functor

(3.5)

\operatorname{Sch}_{/{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}}\times\operatorname{Sch}_{/{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}}\to\operatorname{Sch}_{/({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\underset{B}{\times}{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})},\quad((X_{1}\to{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}),(X_{2}\to{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}))\mapsto(X_{1}\underset{B}{\times}X_{2}\to{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\underset{B}{\times}{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})

is cofinal.

3.3.3.

When
{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}
are both schemes, the map (
3.3
) is an isomorphism; this
was the main point of introducing
\operatorname{AGCat}_{/B}
.

However, it is not necessarily an isomorphism for
general prestacks
{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}
: this is due to the fact that the map (A) in (
3.4
)
is not necessarily an isomorphism (we will see, however, that the map (B)
is
always an isomorphism).

However, in the course of this section we will distinguish a class of prestacks
{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}
, namely,

AG tame
prestacks, for which the map (
3.3
) is
an isomorphism against any
{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}
, see Proposition
3.6.7
.

3.3.4.

By contrast, the functor

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(-)_{\operatorname{co}}:\operatorname{Corr}_{\operatorname{all,sch}}(\operatorname{PreStk}_{/B})\to\operatorname{AGCat}_{/B}

is
strictly
symmetric monoidal.

Namely, for a pair of prestacks
{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}
, we have

(3.6)

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})_{\operatorname{co}}\otimes\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2})_{\operatorname{co}}\simeq\left(\underset{X_{1}\in\operatorname{Sch}_{/{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}}}{\operatorname{colim}}\,\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(X_{1})\right)\otimes\left(\underset{X_{2}\in\operatorname{Sch}_{/{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}}}{\operatorname{colim}}\,\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(X_{2})\right)\simeq\\
\simeq\underset{X_{1}\in\operatorname{Sch}_{/{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}},X_{2}\in\operatorname{Sch}_{/{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}}}{\operatorname{colim}}\,\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(X_{1})\otimes\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(X_{2})\simeq\underset{X_{1}\in\operatorname{Sch}_{/{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}},X_{2}\in\operatorname{Sch}_{/{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}}}{\operatorname{colim}}\,\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(X_{1}\underset{B}{\times}X_{2})\simeq\\
\simeq\underset{X\in\operatorname{Sch}_{/{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\underset{B}{\times}{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}}}{\operatorname{colim}}\,\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(X)=\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\underset{B}{\times}{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})_{\operatorname{co}},

where the last isomorphism is due to the cofinality of (
3.5
).

3.4.
Tame prestacks

In this subsection we let
{\mathcal{Y}}
be a prestack with a schematic diagonal.

3.4.1.

Due to the assumption on
{\mathcal{Y}}
, for any a map
f:X\to{\mathcal{Y}}
, where
X
is a scheme, is schematic.
Hence, we have a well-defined funcor

f_{*}:\operatorname{Shv}(X)\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}).

These functors assemble to a functor

(3.7)

\Omega_{\mathcal{Y}}:\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}}\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}).

3.4.2.

Note that for a schematic map between prestacks
f:{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\to{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}
, we have the commutative diagrams

(3.8)

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})_{\operatorname{co}}@>{\Omega_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}}}>{}>\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})\\
@V{f_{\operatorname{co},*}}V{}V@V{}V{f_{*}}V\\
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2})_{\operatorname{co}}@>{\Omega_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}}}>{}>\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2})\end{CD}

and

(3.9)

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})_{\operatorname{co}}@>{\Omega_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}}}>{}>\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})\\
@A{f^{!}_{\operatorname{co}}}A{}A@A{}A{f^{!}}A\\
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2})_{\operatorname{co}}@>{\Omega_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}}}>{}>\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2})\end{CD}

3.4.3.

We shall say that
{\mathcal{Y}}
is
tame
if
\Omega_{\mathcal{Y}}
is an equivalence.

Not all prestacks are tame. However, in Sect.
4.6.3
we will
single out a class of algebraic stacks, for which it is. Conjecturally, this includes all quasi-compact algebraic
stacks.

3.4.4.

Recall that
{\mathcal{Y}}
is a
pseudo-scheme
if (up to sheafification in the h-topology)
it can be rewritten as a (not necessarily filtered) colimit of schemes with transition maps that are proper. For example,
and ind-scheme is such.

We claim:

Lemma 3.4.5
.

Any pseudo-scheme is tame.

Proof.

Write (up to h-sheafification)

{\mathcal{Y}}=\underset{i\in I}{\operatorname{colim}}\,Y_{i},

where
Y_{i}
are schemes are the transition maps are proper. Then

(3.10)

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\simeq\underset{i\in I^{\operatorname{op}},(-)^{!}}{\operatorname{lim}}\,\operatorname{Shv}(Y_{i}),

and

(3.11)

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}}\simeq\underset{i\in I,(-)_{*}}{\operatorname{colim}}\,\operatorname{Shv}(Y_{i}).

Note, however, that since the transition functors in (
3.10
) are the right adjoints
of those in (
3.11
), we obtain that the functor

(3.12)

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}}\simeq\underset{i\in I,(-)_{*}}{\operatorname{colim}}\,\operatorname{Shv}(Y_{i})\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})

assembled from the left adjoints of the evaluation functors

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\to\operatorname{Shv}(Y_{i}),

is an equivalence, see
[
GR1
, Chapter 1, Proposition 2.5.7]
.

Now, by construction, the functor (
3.12
) is the functor
\Omega_{\mathcal{Y}}
.

∎

3.4.6.

As in Sect.
3.4.1
, the maps

f_{*}:\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(X)\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}),\quad(X,f)\in\operatorname{Sch}_{/{\mathcal{Y}}}

assemble into a map

(3.13)

\underline{\Omega}_{\mathcal{Y}}:\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}}\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}).

In terms of the identification given by Lemma
3.2.6
, the values of the map
(
3.13
) are given by the functors (
3.7
) for
{\mathcal{Y}}\underset{B}{\times}X
.

The commutative diagrams (
3.8
) and (
3.9
) give rise to
commutative diagrams

(3.14)

\begin{CD}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})_{\operatorname{co}}@>{\underline{\Omega}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}}}>{}>\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})\\
@V{f_{\operatorname{co},*}}V{}V@V{}V{f_{*}}V\\
\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2})_{\operatorname{co}}@>{\underline{\Omega}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}}}>{}>\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2})\end{CD}

and

(3.15)

\begin{CD}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})_{\operatorname{co}}@>{\underline{\Omega}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}}}>{}>\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})\\
@A{f^{!}_{\operatorname{co}}}A{}A@A{}A{f^{!}}A\\
\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2})_{\operatorname{co}}@>{\underline{\Omega}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}}}>{}>\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2})\end{CD}

3.4.7.

We shall say that
{\mathcal{Y}}\in\operatorname{PreStk}_{/B}
is
universally tame
if the map (
3.13
) is an isomorphism
in
\operatorname{AGCat}_{/B}
. Explicitly, this means that for any
X\in\operatorname{Sch}
, the prestack

{\mathcal{Y}}\underset{B}{\times}X

is tame.

We shall say that
{\mathcal{Y}}\in\operatorname{PreStk}_{/B}
is
AG tame
of both
{\mathcal{Y}}
and
{\mathcal{Y}}\underset{B}{\times}{\mathcal{Y}}
are universally tame.

3.4.8.

Let
{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}
be a pair of prestacks over
B
, both with a schematic diagonal. Note that we have a commutative diagram

(3.16)

\begin{CD}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})_{\operatorname{co}}\otimes\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2})_{\operatorname{co}}@>{\text{\eqref{e:boxtimes co}}}>{\sim}>\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\underset{B}{\times}{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})_{\operatorname{co}}\\
@V{\underline{\Omega}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}}\otimes\underline{\Omega}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}}}V{}V@V{}V{\underline{\Omega}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\underset{B}{\times}{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}}}V\\
\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})\otimes\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2})@>{}>{\text{\eqref{e:ten prod prestack}}}>\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\underset{B}{\times}{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}).\end{CD}

Hence, we obtain:

Corollary 3.4.9
.

Suppose that
{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}
,
{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}
are universally tame. Then the following are equivalent:

(i) The prestack
{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\underset{B}{\times}{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}
is also universally tame;

(ii) The map

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})\otimes\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2})\overset{\boxtimes}{\to}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\underset{B}{\times}{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})

of (
3.3
) is an isomorphism.

In particular, we obtain:

Corollary 3.4.10
.

Let
{\mathcal{Y}}
be an AG tame prestack. Then the map

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})\overset{\boxtimes}{\to}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}\underset{B}{\times}{\mathcal{Y}})

is an isomorphism.

3.5.
Dualizability in
\operatorname{AGCat}

3.5.1.

Since the functor
Equation

2.7
is symmetric monoidal, by Sect.
2.1.2
,
for every scheme
X
, the object

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(X)\in\operatorname{AGCat}_{/B}

is canonically self-dual with the unit map given by

\mathbb{1}_{\operatorname{AGCat}_{/B}}=\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(B)\overset{(p_{X/B})^{!}}{\longrightarrow}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(X)\overset{(\Delta_{X/B})_{*}}{\longrightarrow}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(X\underset{B}{\times}X)\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(X)\otimes\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(X)

and the counit map given by

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(X)\otimes\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(X)\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(X\underset{B}{\times}X)\overset{(\Delta_{X/B})^{!}}{\longrightarrow}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(X)\overset{(p_{X/B})_{*}}{\longrightarrow}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(B)\simeq\mathbb{1}_{\operatorname{AGCat}_{/B}},

where
p_{X/B}:X\to B
is the projection map and
\Delta_{X/B}:X\to X\underset{B}{\times}X
is the diagonal.

3.5.2.

It follows formally that for a map of schemes
f:X_{1}\to X_{2}
, under the above identifications

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(X_{i})^{\vee}\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(X_{i}),

we have

(f^{!})^{\vee}\simeq f_{*}

as maps

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(X_{1})\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(X_{2}).

3.5.3.

We will be interested in more general dualizable objects in
\operatorname{AGCat}
. First, we claim:

Proposition 3.5.4
.

Let
{\mathcal{Y}}
be a prestack such that
\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})\in\operatorname{AGCat}_{/B}
is a dualizable object.
Then for any prestack
{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}
, the natural map
(
see (
3.3
)
)

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime})\otimes\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})\overset{\boxtimes}{\to}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}\underset{B}{\times}{\mathcal{Y}})

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

We need to show that both maps (A) and (B) in (
3.4
) are isomorphisms
(with
{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}={\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}
and
{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}={\mathcal{Y}}
).

The fact that (A) is an isomorphism follows from the assumption that
\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})

is dualizable.

The fact that (B) is an isomorphism follows from the fact that the object
\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(X)

for
X\in\operatorname{Sch}
is dualizable.

∎

3.5.5.

Next, we claim:

Proposition 3.5.6
.

For a pair of prestacks we have a canonical identifification

\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{AGCat}_{/B}}(\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})_{\operatorname{co}},\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2}))\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\underset{B}{\times}{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}).

Proof.

We have

\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{AGCat}_{/B}}(\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})_{\operatorname{co}},\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2}))\simeq\underset{X\in(\operatorname{Sch}^{\operatorname{aff}}_{/{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}})^{\operatorname{op}}}{\operatorname{lim}}\,\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{AGCat}_{/B}}(\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(X),\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2}))\overset{\text{Sect.~\ref{sss:self-dual AG sch}}}{\simeq}\\
\simeq\underset{X\in(\operatorname{Sch}^{\operatorname{aff}}_{/{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}})^{\operatorname{op}}}{\operatorname{lim}}\,\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(X)\otimes\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2})\overset{\text{Proposition~\ref{p:tensor with dualizable}}}{\simeq}\\
\simeq\underset{X\in(\operatorname{Sch}^{\operatorname{aff}}_{/{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}})^{\operatorname{op}}}{\operatorname{lim}}\,\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(X\underset{B}{\times}{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\underset{B}{\times}{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}),

where the last isomorphism follows from the cofinality of (
3.5
).
∎

In particular, we obtain:

Corollary 3.5.7
.

For a prestack
{\mathcal{Y}}
, we have

\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{AGCat}_{/B}}(\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}},\mathbb{1}_{\operatorname{AGCat}_{/B}})\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}).

3.5.8.

Using Sect.
3.5.2
, we obtain that for a map
f:{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\to{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}
between arbitrary prestacks,
under the identification of Corollary
3.5.7
, the map

(f_{\operatorname{co},*})^{\vee}:\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{AGCat}_{/B}}(\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2})_{\operatorname{co}},\mathbb{1}_{\operatorname{AGCat}_{/B}})\to\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{AGCat}_{/B}}(\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})_{\operatorname{co}},\mathbb{1}_{\operatorname{AGCat}_{/B}})

corresponds to
f^{!}
.

Similarly, we obtain that if
f
is schematic, the functor

(f^{!}_{\operatorname{co}})^{\vee}:\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{AGCat}_{/B}}(\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})_{\operatorname{co}},\mathbb{1}_{\operatorname{AGCat}_{/B}})\to\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{AGCat}_{/B}}(\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})_{\operatorname{co}},\mathbb{1}_{\operatorname{AGCat}_{/B}})

corresponds to
f_{*}
.

3.5.9.

As another consequence of Proposition
3.5.6
, we obtain:

Corollary 3.5.10
.

Let
{\mathcal{Y}}
be universally tame. There is a natural isomorphism

\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{AGCat}_{/B}}(\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}),\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}))\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}\underset{B}{\times}{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}).

3.5.11.

Thus, we obtain that
if

{\mathcal{Y}}
is a universally tame prestack such that

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})
is dualizable,
then

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})
is canonically self-dual as an object of
\operatorname{AGCat}_{/B}
.

In the next subsection we will see that this is the case for AG tame prestacks.

3.6.
Dualizability of AG tame prestacks

3.6.1.

We are going to prove:

Theorem 3.6.2
.

Let
{\mathcal{Y}}
be an AG tame prestack. Then the object
\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})
is dualizable in
\operatorname{AGCat}_{/B}
.

3.6.3.

The proof of Theorem
3.6.2
will rely on the following characterization of dualizability:

Recall that if
{\mathcal{A}}
is a monoidal category we have the notion of left and right dualizable objects (see
[
Lu2
, Sect. 4.6.1]
)
which agree in the case when
{\mathcal{A}}
is symmetric monoidal. Recall also that for two objects
a_{1},a_{2}\in{\mathcal{A}}

the internal hom
\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{{\mathcal{A}}}(a,b)

is the object representing the functor

a\mapsto\operatorname{Maps}_{\mathcal{A}}(a\otimes a_{1},a_{2}).

The proof of the following is identical to
[
Lu2
, Lemma 4.6.1.6]
.

Proposition 3.6.4
.

Let
{\mathcal{A}}
be a monoidal category. An object
a\in{\mathcal{A}}
is right dualizable if and only if

(i) There exist internal hom objects
\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathcal{A}}(a,\mathbb{1}_{\mathcal{A}})
and
\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathcal{A}}(a,a)
, and

(ii) The tautological map
\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathcal{A}}(a,\mathbb{1}_{\mathcal{A}})\otimes a\to\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathcal{A}}(a,a)

is an isomorphism.

3.6.5.
Proof of Theorem
3.6.2

By Proposition
3.6.4
we need to show that the canonical map

(3.17)

\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{AGCat}_{/B}}(\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}),\mathbb{1}_{\operatorname{AGCat}_{/B}})\otimes\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})\to\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{AGCat}_{/B}}(\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}),\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}))

is an isomorphism. We identify

\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{AGCat}_{/B}}(\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}),\mathbb{1}_{\operatorname{AGCat}_{/B}})\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})\text{ and }\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{AGCat}_{/B}}(\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}),\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}))\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}\underset{B}{\times}{\mathcal{Y}})

by Corollary
3.5.10
. Under these identifications, the map (
3.17
) is the map

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})\overset{\boxtimes}{\to}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}\underset{B}{\times}{\mathcal{Y}})

of (
3.4
). The latter map is an isomorphism by
3.4.10
.

∎[Theorem
3.6.2
]

3.6.6.

By
3.5.4
, we obtain:

Corollary 3.6.7
.

Let
{\mathcal{Y}}
be an AG tame prestack and
{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}
a prestack. Then the canonical map

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime})\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}\underset{B}{\times}{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime})

is an isomorphism.

3.6.8.

We now obtain a characterization of AG tame prestacks in terms of
\operatorname{AGCat}_{/B}
:

Proposition 3.6.9
.

Let
{\mathcal{Y}}
be a
universally
tame prestack. Then the following conditions are equivalent:

(i)
{\mathcal{Y}}
is AG tame;

(ii) The object
\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})\in\operatorname{AGCat}_{/B}
is dualizable;

(iii) The map
\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}\underset{B}{\times}{\mathcal{Y}})
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

First, (i) implies (ii) by
3.6.2
. Next, (ii) implies (iii) by Proposition
3.5.4
.
Finally, (iii) implies (i) by Corollary
3.4.9
.

∎

3.6.10.

We can now prove that the notion of being AG Verdier-compatible is stable under products:

Proposition 3.6.11
.

If
{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}
are AG tame, then so is
{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\underset{B}{\times}{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}
.

Proof.

We apply the characterization of AG tame prestacks from
3.6.9
.
By Corollary
3.6.7
,
we have:

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})\otimes\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2})\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\underset{B}{\times}{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}).

Hence, since
\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})
and
\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2})
are dualizable, so is
\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\underset{B}{\times}{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})
.

The fact that
{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\underset{B}{\times}{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}
is universally tame follows from
Corollary
3.4.9
using Proposition
3.5.4
.

∎

3.7.
Description of unit and counit

In this section we let
{\mathcal{Y}}
be an AG tame prestack. By Theorem
3.6.2
, we know that

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})
is dualizable as an object of
\operatorname{AGCat}_{/B}
. Moreover, by Sect.
3.5.11
, it is self-dual.

In this subsection we will describe explicitly the unit and counit maps for this self-duality.

3.7.1.

Note that since
{\mathcal{Y}}
was assumed to have a schematic diagonal, we have a well-defined map

(\Delta_{{\mathcal{Y}}/B})_{*}:\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}\underset{B}{\times}{\mathcal{Y}}).

First, we will prove:

Proposition 3.7.2
.

The unit of the self-duality on
\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})
is given by the map

(3.18)

\mathbb{1}_{\operatorname{AGCat}_{/B}}=\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(B)\overset{(p_{{\mathcal{Y}}/B})^{!}}{\to}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})\overset{(\Delta_{{\mathcal{Y}}/B})_{*}}{\to}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}\underset{B}{\times}{\mathcal{Y}})\overset{\text{Corollary~\ref{c:tensor product verd comp}}}{\simeq}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}).

Proof.

We will think of the left copy of
\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})
in the tensor product as
\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})^{\vee}
via

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})^{\vee}\overset{\text{Corollary~\ref{c:dual co ushv}}}{\simeq}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}}\overset{\underline{\Omega}_{\mathcal{Y}}}{\to}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}).

Since
\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})
is the limit of
\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(X)
over
X\overset{f}{\to}{\mathcal{Y}}
, using Sect.
3.5.1
,
we conclude that we need to show that for every
f:X\to{\mathcal{Y}}
, the composition of (
3.18
) with

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})\overset{\operatorname{id}\otimes f^{!}}{\longrightarrow}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(X)\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})^{\vee}\otimes\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(X)

identifies with

\mathbb{1}_{\operatorname{AGCat}_{/B}}=\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(B)\overset{(p_{X/B})^{!}}{\to}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(X)\overset{(\Delta_{X/B})_{*}}{\to}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(X\underset{B}{\times}X)\simeq\\
\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(X)\otimes\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(X)\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(X)^{\vee}\otimes\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(X)\overset{(f^{!})^{\vee}\otimes\operatorname{Id}}{\longrightarrow}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})^{\vee}\otimes\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(X),

functorially in
(X,f)
.

Using the commutative diagram

\begin{CD}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(X)@>{\boxtimes}>{}>\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}\underset{B}{\times}X)\\
@A{\operatorname{Id}\otimes f^{!}}A{}A@A{}A{(\operatorname{id}\times f)^{!}}A\\
\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})@>{\boxtimes}>{}>\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}\underset{B}{\times}{\mathcal{Y}}),\end{CD}

and base change, we obtain that composition of (
3.18
) with
\operatorname{id}\otimes f^{!}
is the map

\mathbb{1}_{\operatorname{AGCat}_{/B}}=\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(B)\overset{(p_{X/B})^{!}}{\to}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(X)\overset{(\Delta_{X/B})_{*}}{\to}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(X\underset{B}{\times}X)\simeq\\
\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(X)\otimes\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(X)\overset{f_{*}\otimes\operatorname{Id}}{\longrightarrow}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(X).

Hence, it remains to show that the functor

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(X)\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(X)^{\vee}\overset{(f^{!})^{\vee}}{\to}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})^{\vee}

identifies with

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(X)\overset{f_{*}}{\to}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})^{\vee}.

However, this follows by combining (
3.14
) and Sect.
3.5.8
.

∎

3.7.3.

For a (not necessarily) schematic map
f:{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\to{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}
between tame prestacks,
we introduce the functor

f_{\blacktriangle}:\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2})

to be the composition

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})\overset{\Omega_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}}}{\simeq}\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})_{\operatorname{co}}\overset{f_{\operatorname{co,*}}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2})_{\operatorname{co}}\overset{\Omega_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}}}{\simeq}\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2}).

Note that if
f
is schematic, it follows from (
3.8
) that we have

f_{\blacktriangle}\simeq f_{*}.

For future use, we note:

Lemma 3.7.4
.

Let

\begin{CD}{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}_{1}@>{g_{1}}>{}>{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\\
@V{f^{\prime}}V{}V@V{}V{f}V\\
{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}_{2}@>{g_{2}}>{}>{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}\end{CD}

be a Cartesian diagram of tame prestacks, where the horizontal maps are schematic. Then
there exists a canonical isomorphism

g_{2}^{!}\circ f_{\blacktriangle}\simeq f^{\prime}_{\blacktriangle}\circ g_{1}^{!}.

Proof.

Follows from (
3.9
).

∎

Corollary 3.7.5
.

Suppose that
{\mathcal{Y}}_{1},{\mathcal{Y}}_{2},{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2},{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}
are tame.
Then the functor
f_{\blacktriangle}
satisfies the projection formula:

f_{\blacktriangle}({\mathcal{F}}_{1})\overset{!}{\otimes}{\mathcal{F}}_{2}\simeq f_{\blacktriangle}({\mathcal{F}}_{1}\overset{!}{\otimes}f^{!}({\mathcal{F}}_{2})).

Proof.

We apply Lemma
3.7.4
to

\begin{CD}{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}@>{\operatorname{Graph}_{f}}>{}>{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}\\
@V{f}V{}V@V{}V{f\times\operatorname{id}}V\\
{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}@>{\Delta_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}}}>{}>{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}.\end{CD}

∎

3.7.6.

Similarly, for a map
f:{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\to{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}
between
universally
tame prestacks
we define the morphism

f_{\blacktriangle}:\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2})

to be the composition

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})\overset{\underline{\Omega}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}}}{\simeq}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})_{\operatorname{co}}\overset{f_{\operatorname{co,*}}}{\longrightarrow}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2})_{\operatorname{co}}\overset{\underline{\Omega}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}}}{\simeq}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2}).

The values of the latter map are given by
(f\times\operatorname{id})_{\blacktriangle}
for

f\times\operatorname{id}:{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\underset{B}{\times}X\to{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}\underset{B}{\times}X.

3.7.7.

Unwinding, from Proposition
3.7.2
and Sect.
3.5.8
we obtain:

Corollary 3.7.8
.

For a map
f:{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\to{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}
between AG tame prestacks, with respect to
the self-dualities
\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{i})\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{i})^{\vee}
, we have

(f^{!})^{\vee}\simeq f_{\blacktriangle}.

3.7.9.

As a formal consequence of Corollary
3.7.8
we obtain:

Corollary 3.7.10
.

The counit of the self-duality of
\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})
is given by

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})\overset{\boxtimes}{\to}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}\underset{B}{\times}{\mathcal{Y}})\overset{(\Delta_{{\mathcal{Y}}/B})^{!}}{\longrightarrow}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})\overset{(p_{{\mathcal{Y}}/B})_{\blacktriangle}}{\longrightarrow}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(B)=\mathbb{1}_{\operatorname{AGCat}_{/B}}.

4.
Duality in
\operatorname{DGCat}
and
\operatorname{AGCat}

In this section we study how monoidal dualilty in
\operatorname{DGCat}
interacts with Verdier duality
on schemes and algebraic stacks.

4.1.
Ambidexterity

4.1.1.

Recall the general setup in
Section

1.1
: we have a closed symmetric monoidal category
{\mathbf{O}}
and a right-lax
symmetric monoidal functor

F:{\mathbf{O}}\to{\mathcal{V}}.

Suppose now that
F
satisfies the following additional hypotheses:

(1)

F
is strictly unital; i.e. the natural map

\mathbb{1}_{{\mathcal{V}}}\to F(\mathbb{1}_{{\mathbf{O}}})

is an isomorphism.

(2)

Every object
{\mathbf{o}}\in{\mathbf{O}}
is dualizable.

(3)

For every
{\mathbf{o}}\in{\mathbf{O}}
, the pairing

F({\mathbf{o}})\otimes F({\mathbf{o}}^{\vee})\to F({\mathbf{o}}\otimes{\mathbf{o}}^{\vee})\to F(\mathbb{1}_{{\mathbf{O}}})\simeq\mathbb{1}_{{\mathcal{V}}}

is the counit of a duality pairing, where
{\mathbf{o}}^{\vee}
is the dual of
{\mathbf{o}}
and the right map is the image under
F
of the duality pairing of
{\mathbf{o}}
and
{\mathbf{o}}^{\vee}
.

4.1.2.

Recall that we have the adjunction
Equation

1.6

\textstyle{{\mathbf{i}}:{\mathcal{V}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\underline{{\mathcal{V}}}:{\mathbf{e}}}

of
{\mathcal{V}}
-module categories.

By
1.2.8
, condition (
1
) implies that the functor
{\mathbf{i}}
is fully faithful. Thus,
the unit natural transformation

(4.1)

\operatorname{Id}_{{\mathcal{V}}}\to{\mathbf{e}}\circ{\mathbf{i}}

is an isomorphism.

By Proposition
1.2.5
,
\underline{{\mathcal{V}}}
is a closed symmetric monoidal category. We have:

(4.2)

{\mathbf{e}}(\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\underline{{\mathcal{V}}}}(\underline{v}_{1},\underline{v}_{2}))\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\underline{{\mathcal{V}}},{\mathcal{V}}}(\underline{v}_{1},\underline{v}_{2})\in{\mathcal{V}}.

4.1.3.

Assuming conditions (
1
) and (
2
), condition (
3
) is equivalent to the following two conditions:

(3’) For every
{\mathbf{o}}\in{\mathbf{O}}
, the object
F({\mathbf{o}})\in{\mathcal{V}}
is dualizable, and

(3”) The map

\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\underline{{\mathcal{V}}},{\mathcal{V}}}(\underline{F}({\mathbf{o}}),\mathbb{1}_{\underline{{\mathcal{V}}}})\to\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\underline{{\mathcal{V}}},{\mathcal{V}}}({\mathbf{i}}\circ{\mathbf{e}}(\underline{F}({\mathbf{o}})),\mathbb{1}_{\underline{{\mathcal{V}}}})\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\underline{{\mathcal{V}}},{\mathcal{V}}}({\mathbf{i}}(F({\mathbf{o}})),\mathbb{1}_{\underline{{\mathcal{V}}}})\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{{\mathcal{V}}}(F({\mathbf{o}}),\mathbb{1}_{\mathcal{V}}),

given by restriction along the counit map
{\mathbf{i}}\circ{\mathbf{e}}(\underline{F}({\mathbf{o}}))\to\underline{F}({\mathbf{o}})
, is an isomorphism.

4.1.4.

We claim:

Proposition 4.1.5
.

The adjunction
({\mathbf{i}},{\mathbf{e}})
is ambidexterous, with the counit of the
({\mathbf{e}},{\mathbf{i}})
-adjunction given by the inverse
of
Equation

4.1
.

Proof.

We need to show that for every
v\in{\mathcal{V}}
and every
\underline{v}\in\underline{{\mathcal{V}}}
, the natural map

(4.3)

\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\underline{{\mathcal{V}}},{\mathcal{V}}}(\underline{v},{\mathbf{i}}(v))\to\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{{\mathcal{V}}}({\mathbf{e}}(\underline{v}),{\mathbf{e}}\circ{\mathbf{i}}(v))\overset{\sim}{\to}\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{{\mathcal{V}}}({\mathbf{e}}(\underline{v}),v)

is an isomorphism.

Since both sides take weighted colimits in the
\underline{v}
-variable to weighted limits, by

Equation

B.5
, it suffices to show that

Equation

4.3
is an isomorphism for
\underline{v}=\underline{F}({\mathbf{o}})
for
{\mathbf{o}}\in{\mathbf{O}}
.

By definition,
{\mathbf{i}}(v)\simeq\mathbb{1}_{\underline{{\mathcal{V}}}}\otimes v
. Hence, we have a commutative diagram

\textstyle{\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\underline{{\mathcal{V}}},{\mathcal{V}}}(\underline{F}({\mathbf{o}}),\mathbb{1}_{\underline{{\mathcal{V}}}})\otimes v\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\underline{{\mathcal{V}}},{\mathcal{V}}}(\underline{F}({\mathbf{o}}),{\mathbf{i}}(v))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\underline{{\mathcal{V}}},{\mathcal{V}}}({\mathbf{i}}\circ{\mathbf{e}}(\underline{F}({\mathbf{o}})),\mathbb{1}_{\underline{{\mathcal{V}}}})\otimes v\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\underline{{\mathcal{V}}},{\mathcal{V}}}({\mathbf{i}}\circ{\mathbf{e}}(\underline{F}({\mathbf{o}})),{\mathbf{i}}(v))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathcal{V}}({\mathbf{e}}(\underline{F}({\mathbf{o}})),v),}

where the composite map

\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\underline{{\mathcal{V}}},{\mathcal{V}}}(\underline{F}({\mathbf{o}}),{\mathbf{i}}(v))\to\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\underline{{\mathcal{V}}},{\mathcal{V}}}({\mathbf{i}}\circ{\mathbf{e}}(\underline{F}({\mathbf{o}})),{\mathbf{i}}(v))\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathcal{V}}({\mathbf{e}}(\underline{F}({\mathbf{o}})),v)

is the map (
4.3
) for
\underline{v}=\underline{F}({\mathbf{o}})
. Thus, we need to check that the right vertical map in this diagram is an isomorphism.

In the above diagram, the left vertical map is an isomorphism by condition (3”) above; the top horizontal map is an isomorphism since
\underline{F}({\mathbf{o}})
is dualizable;
and the bottom horizontal map is an isomorphism because
{\mathbf{i}}\circ{\mathbf{e}}(\underline{F}({\mathbf{o}}))\simeq{\mathbf{i}}(F({\mathbf{o}}))
is dualizable by condition (3’) above.
Hence, the right vertical map is an isomorphism, as desired.

∎

Remark 4.1.6
.

In the specific situation of
\operatorname{AGCat}
, we will show in
4.5.6
that more is true. Namely, in this case the counit map

{\mathbf{i}}\circ{\mathbf{e}}\to\operatorname{Id}

admits a right adjoint (which makes sense since
\operatorname{AGCat}
is naturally a 2-category). In this situation (i.e., when
{\mathbf{e}}\circ{\mathbf{i}}=\operatorname{Id}
), this right adjoint
is automatically the unit of an
({\mathbf{e}},{\mathbf{i}})
-adjunction, which is automatically the same as the
({\mathbf{e}},{\mathbf{i}})
-adjunction described above.

4.2.
Duality of the values

We keep the assumptions of
Section

4.1.1
. In this subsection, we will show that the functor

{\mathbf{e}}:\underline{{\mathcal{V}}}\to{\mathcal{V}}

maps dualizable objects to dualizable objects, and we will describe the duality data explicitly.

4.2.1.

We are going to prove:

Proposition 4.2.2
.

Suppose that
\underline{v}\in\underline{{\mathcal{V}}}
is a dualizable object. Then
{\mathbf{e}}(\underline{v})
is also dualizable, and the paring

(4.4)

{\mathbf{e}}(\underline{v})\otimes{\mathbf{e}}(\underline{v}^{\vee})\to{\mathbf{e}}(\underline{v}\otimes\underline{v}^{\vee})\to{\mathbf{e}}(\mathbb{1}_{{\mathcal{V}}})\simeq\mathbb{1}_{{\mathcal{V}}}

is the counit of a duality.

Proof.

First, we will show that
{\mathbf{e}}(\underline{v})
is dualizable. We need to show that for any
v\in{\mathcal{V}}
, the natural map

\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathcal{V}}({\mathbf{e}}(\underline{v}),\mathbb{1}_{\mathcal{V}})\otimes v\to\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathcal{V}}({\mathbf{e}}(\underline{v}),v)

is an isomorphism. We have a commutative diagram

(4.5)

\textstyle{\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\underline{{\mathcal{V}}},{\mathcal{V}}}(\underline{v},\mathbb{1}_{\underline{{\mathcal{V}}}})\otimes v\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\underline{{\mathcal{V}}},{\mathcal{V}}}(\underline{v},{\mathbf{i}}(v))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{{\mathcal{V}}}({\mathbf{e}}(\underline{v}),\mathbb{1}_{\mathcal{V}})\otimes v\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{{\mathcal{V}}}({\mathbf{e}}(\underline{v}),v)}

The vertical maps is
Equation

4.5
are isomorphisms by
4.1.5
.
Since
\underline{v}
is dualizable, the map

\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\underline{{\mathcal{V}}}}(\underline{v},\mathbb{1}_{\underline{{\mathcal{V}}}})\otimes v\to\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\underline{{\mathcal{V}}}}(\underline{v},{\mathbf{i}}(v))

is an isomorphism. Applying the functor
{\mathbf{e}}
and using (
4.2
),
we obtain that the top horizontal map in
Equation

4.5
is an isomorphism.
Hence the bottom horizontal map is also an isomorphism, as desired. Thus, the object
{\mathbf{e}}(\underline{v})
is dualizable.

It remains to show that
Equation

4.4
is the counit of a duality. We need to show that the map

(4.6)

{\mathbf{e}}(\underline{v}^{\vee})\to\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathcal{V}}({\mathbf{e}}(\underline{v}),\mathbb{1}_{{\mathcal{V}}})

induced by
Equation

4.4
is an isomorphism. Unraveling the definition, the map
Equation

4.6
is the composite

{\mathbf{e}}(\underline{v}^{\vee})\simeq{\mathbf{e}}(\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\underline{{\mathcal{V}}}}(\underline{v},\mathbb{1}_{\underline{{\mathcal{V}}}}))\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\underline{{\mathcal{V}}},{\mathcal{V}}}(\underline{v},\mathbb{1}_{\underline{{\mathcal{V}}}})\to\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathcal{V}}({\mathbf{e}}(\underline{v}),\mathbb{1}_{\mathcal{V}}),

which is an isomorphism by
4.1.5
.

∎

4.2.3.

As a consequence of
4.2.2
, we obtain that all values of a dualizable object are dualizable:

Corollary 4.2.4
.

Let
\underline{v}\in\underline{{\mathcal{V}}}
be a dualizable object and
{\mathbf{o}}\in{\mathbf{O}}
. Then
\underline{v}({\mathbf{o}})\in{\mathcal{V}}
is dualizable with
dual
\underline{v}^{\vee}({\mathbf{o}}^{\vee})
and the counit given by

\underline{v}({\mathbf{o}})\otimes\underline{v}^{\vee}({\mathbf{o}}^{\vee})\to(\underline{v}\otimes\underline{v}^{\vee})({\mathbf{o}}\otimes{\mathbf{o}}^{\vee})\to(\underline{v}\otimes\underline{v}^{\vee})(\mathbb{1}_{{\mathbf{O}}})\to\mathbb{1}_{\underline{{\mathcal{V}}}}(\mathbb{1}_{{\mathbf{O}}})\simeq\mathbb{1}_{{\mathcal{V}}}.

Proof.

We have

\underline{v}({\mathbf{o}})\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\underline{{\mathcal{V}}},{\mathcal{V}}}(\underline{F}({\mathbf{o}}),\underline{v})\simeq{\mathbf{e}}(\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\underline{{\mathcal{V}}}}(\underline{F}({\mathbf{o}}),\underline{v}))\simeq{\mathbf{e}}(\underline{F}({\mathbf{o}}^{\vee})\otimes\underline{v}).

Now,
\underline{F}({\mathbf{o}}^{\vee})\otimes\underline{v}\in\underline{{\mathcal{V}}}
is a dualizable object (since it’s a tensor product of two dualizable objects).
Applying
4.2.2
to this object, we obtain the desired result.
∎

4.3.
Recollections on Verdier duality on schemes

4.3.1.

Let
X
be a scheme. In this case, by definition, we have

\operatorname{Shv}(X)=\operatorname{Ind}(\operatorname{Shv}(X)^{\operatorname{constr}})

is the ind-category of constructible
\ell
-adic sheaves. Now, Verdier duality gives an equivalence

(4.7)

{{\symAMSb D}}_{X}:(\operatorname{Shv}(X)^{\operatorname{constr}})^{\operatorname{op}}\simeq(\operatorname{Shv}(X)^{\operatorname{constr}}).

4.3.2.

Recall that if
{\mathbf{C}}
is a compactly generated DG category, then it is dualizable and the dual is given by

(4.8)

{\mathbf{C}}^{\vee}\simeq\operatorname{Ind}(({\mathbf{C}}^{c})^{\operatorname{op}}),

where
{\mathbf{C}}^{c}\subset{\mathbf{C}}
is the full subcategory consisting of compact objects.

Moreover, suppose that

F:{\mathbf{C}}_{1}\to{\mathbf{C}}_{2}

is a functor between compactly generated categories that preserves compact objects; i.e.
F=\operatorname{Ind}(F^{c})

for a DG functor

F^{c}:{\mathbf{C}}_{1}^{c}\to{\mathbf{C}}_{2}^{c}.

Then the dual
F^{\vee}
of
F
identifies with

{\mathbf{C}}_{2}^{\vee}\simeq\operatorname{Ind}(({\mathbf{C}}_{2}^{c})^{\operatorname{op}})\overset{\operatorname{Ind}((F^{c})^{\operatorname{op}})^{R}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Ind}(({\mathbf{C}}_{1}^{c})^{\operatorname{op}})\simeq{\mathbf{C}}_{1}^{\vee},

i.e., the right adjoint of the ind-extension of
(F^{c})^{\operatorname{op}}:{\mathbf{C}}_{1}^{\operatorname{op}}\to{\mathbf{C}}_{2}^{\operatorname{op}}
.

4.3.3.

Applying this to the category
\operatorname{Shv}(X)
, we see that the Verdier duality functor gives rise to an identification

(4.9)

\operatorname{Shv}(X)^{\vee}\simeq\operatorname{Shv}(X).

4.3.4.

Recall also that for
{\mathcal{F}}_{1},{\mathcal{F}}_{2}\in\operatorname{Shv}(X)^{\operatorname{constr}}
, we have

{\mathcal{H}}\operatorname{om}_{\operatorname{Shv}(X)}({\mathcal{F}}_{1},{\mathcal{F}}_{2})\simeq\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(X,{{\symAMSb D}}_{X}({\mathcal{F}}_{1})\overset{!}{\otimes}{\mathcal{F}}_{2}).

This implies that the pairing

\operatorname{Shv}(X)\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(X)\to\operatorname{Vect}

corresponding to (
4.9
) is given by

\operatorname{Shv}(X)\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(X)\overset{\boxtimes}{\to}\operatorname{Shv}(X\times X)\overset{\Delta_{X}^{!}}{\to}\operatorname{Shv}(X)\overset{(p_{X})_{*}}{\to}\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{pt})\simeq\operatorname{Vect}.

4.3.5.

The above formula for the pairing implies that for a map of schemes
f:X_{1}\to X_{2}
, we have an identification of functors on the
subcategories of compact objects

f^{!}\circ{{\symAMSb D}}_{X_{2}}\simeq{{\symAMSb D}}_{X_{1}}\circ f^{*}

and therefore with respect to the self-duality
Equation

4.9
, we have

(4.10)

(f^{!})^{\vee}\simeq(f^{*})^{R}\simeq f_{*}.

4.3.6.

Let
{\mathcal{Y}}
be a prestack, and recall the category
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}}
. Since the transition functors
in the colimit preserve compactness, the category
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}}
is compactly generated, and
hence dualizable.

From (
4.10
) it follows that we have a canonical identification

(4.11)

(\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}})^{\vee}\simeq\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}).

Under this identification, for a map of prestacks
f:{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\to{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}
, we have

(4.12)

f^{!}\simeq(f_{\operatorname{co},*})^{\vee},

and if
f
is schematic

(4.13)

f_{*}\simeq(f^{!}_{\operatorname{co}})^{\vee}.

Remark 4.3.7
.

Note that formulas (
4.10
), (
4.12
) and (
4.13
) look
similar to those in Sects.
3.5.2
and
3.5.8
. However, for
now, these are “apples and oranges”: the former talk about duality in
\operatorname{DGCat}
and the
latter about duality in
\operatorname{AGCat}
. The two will be related in the next subsection, see Corollary
4.4.3
.

4.4.
Duality in
\operatorname{AGCat}

We now specialize the discussion in Sects.
4.1
and
4.2
to

\operatorname{Shv}:\operatorname{Corr}(\operatorname{Sch})\to\operatorname{DGCat},

i.e., to the setting of Sect.
2
with
B=\operatorname{pt}
.

The latter assumption is needed in order for
\operatorname{Vect}\to\operatorname{Shv}(B)
to be
an equivalence. As was mentioned already, in this case we write
\operatorname{AGCat}
instead of
\operatorname{AGCat}_{/B}
.

4.4.1.

We claim that conditions (1)-(3) in Sect.
4.1.1
are satisfied. Indeed, condition (1)
is satisfied since
B=\operatorname{pt}
.
Condition (2) is satisfied because every object in
\operatorname{Corr}(\operatorname{Sch})
is self-dual
(see Sect.
2.1.2
). Condition (3) is satisfied by Sect.
4.3.4
.

Thus, the contents of Sects.
4.1
-
4.2
are applicable.

4.4.2.

Applying
4.2.4
to
\operatorname{AGCat}
, we obtain:

Corollary 4.4.3
.

Let
\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}\in\operatorname{AGCat}
be a dualizable object. Then for any scheme
X
,

\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}(X)\in\operatorname{DGCat}

is dualizable, with dual
\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}^{\vee}(X)
and counit pairing

\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}(X)\otimes\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}^{\vee}(X)\to(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}\otimes\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}^{\vee})(X\times X)\overset{\Delta_{X}^{!}}{\to}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}\otimes\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}^{\vee})(X)\overset{(p_{X})_{*}}{\to}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}\otimes\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}^{\vee})(\operatorname{pt})\to\mathbb{1}_{\operatorname{AGCat}}(\operatorname{pt})\simeq\operatorname{Vect}.

In particular,
\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}(\operatorname{pt})
is dualizable with counit given by

\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}(\operatorname{pt})\otimes\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}^{\vee}(\operatorname{pt})\to(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}\otimes\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}^{\vee})(\operatorname{pt})\to\mathbb{1}_{\operatorname{AGCat}}(\operatorname{pt})\simeq\operatorname{Vect}.

4.4.4.

Let
{\mathcal{Y}}
be an AG tame prestack. From Corollaries
4.4.3
and
3.7.10
, we obtain:

Corollary 4.4.5
.

The category
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})
is self-dual with the counit given by

(4.14)

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\overset{\boxtimes}{\to}\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times{\mathcal{Y}})\overset{\Delta_{\mathcal{Y}}^{!}}{\to}\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\overset{(p_{\mathcal{Y}})_{\blacktriangle}}{\to}\operatorname{Vect}.

4.4.6.

Unwinding, we obtain that the self-duality

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})^{\vee}\simeq\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})

given by Corollary
4.4.5
equals

(4.15)

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})^{\vee}\overset{\Omega_{\mathcal{Y}}^{\vee}}{\simeq}(\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}})^{\vee}\overset{\text{\eqref{e:dual of co}}}{\simeq}\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}).

We will denote the resulting equivalence

(4.16)

(\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})^{c})^{\operatorname{op}}\simeq\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})^{c}

by
{{\symAMSb D}}_{\mathcal{Y}}
, see (
4.8
).

Note that by construction, when
{\mathcal{Y}}=X
is a scheme, this is the same functor as (
4.7
).

Remark 4.4.7
.

Note that the identification (
4.15
) and the equivalence (
4.16
) take
place for any tame prestack (it does not need to be AG or universally tame).

For future reference, we note that if
{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}
,
{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}
and
{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}
are tame, and
{\mathcal{F}}_{i}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{i})^{c}
, we have
a canonical identification

(4.17)

{{\symAMSb D}}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}}({\mathcal{F}}_{1})\boxtimes{{\symAMSb D}}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}}({\mathcal{F}}_{2})\simeq{{\symAMSb D}}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}}({\mathcal{F}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathcal{F}}_{2}).

4.5.
Ambidexterity in
\operatorname{AGCat}

4.5.1.

Recall the adjunction

(4.18)

\textstyle{{\mathbf{i}}:\operatorname{DGCat}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\operatorname{AGCat}:{\mathbf{e}}}

of (
2.8
).

The counit is a natural transformation

(4.19)

{\mathbf{i}}\circ{\mathbf{e}}\to\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{AGCat}}

of
\operatorname{DGCat}
-module functors
\operatorname{AGCat}\to\operatorname{AGCat}
.

4.5.2.

We wll prove:

Proposition 4.5.3
.

The counit natural transformation
Equation

4.19
admits a right adjoint in the 2-category

\operatorname{Funct}_{\operatorname{DGCat}}(\operatorname{AGCat},\operatorname{AGCat})
.

Let us explain what Proposition
4.5.3
says in concrete terms:

First, for any
\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}\in\operatorname{AGCat}

and
X\in\operatorname{Sch}
, the functor

\operatorname{Shv}(X)\otimes\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}(\operatorname{pt})\to\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}(X)

admits a right adjoint.

Second (see Proposition
2.7.2
), for every
{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Shv}(X^{\prime}\times X^{\prime\prime})
, the diagram

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Shv}(X^{\prime})\otimes\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}(\operatorname{pt})@>{}>{}>\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}(X^{\prime})\\
@V{{\mathcal{F}}\otimes\operatorname{Id}}V{}V@V{}V{\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}({\mathcal{F}})}V\\
\operatorname{Shv}(X^{\prime\prime})\otimes\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}(\operatorname{pt})@>{}>{}>\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}(X^{\prime\prime})\end{CD}

satisfies the right Beck-Chevalley condition.

And third (see Proposition
2.7.4
), for a map
\alpha:\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{1}\to\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{2}
and
X\in\operatorname{Sch}
, the
diagram

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Shv}(X)\otimes\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{1}(\operatorname{pt})@>{}>{}>\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{1}(X)\\
@V{\operatorname{Id}\otimes\alpha(\operatorname{pt})}V{}V@V{}V{\alpha(X)}V\\
\operatorname{Shv}(X)\otimes\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{2}(\operatorname{pt})@>{}>{}>\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{2}(X)\end{CD}

satisfies the right Beck-Chevalley condition.

4.5.4.
Proof of Proposition
4.5.3

The assignment

\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}\mapsto({\mathbf{i}}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}(\operatorname{pt}))\to\underline{{\mathbf{C}}})

can be viewed as a
\operatorname{DGCat}
-module functor

(4.20)

\operatorname{AGCat}\to\operatorname{Funct}([1],\operatorname{AGCat}).

The assertion of the proposition is equivalent to the assertion that the functor (
4.20
)
factors through
\operatorname{Funct}(\operatorname{Adj},\operatorname{AGCat})
.

By Proposition
A.5.6
, we have

(4.21)

\operatorname{Funct}_{\operatorname{DGCat}}(\operatorname{AGCat},\operatorname{Funct}([1],\operatorname{AGCat}))\simeq\\
\simeq\operatorname{Funct}^{\operatorname{DGCat}}(\operatorname{enh}(\operatorname{Corr}(\operatorname{Sch}),\operatorname{DGCat}),\operatorname{Funct}([1],\operatorname{AGCat})),

and we need to show that the object corresponding to
Equation

4.20
in the right-hand
side of (
4.21
)
lies in the 1-full subcategory

(4.22)

\operatorname{Funct}^{\operatorname{DGCat}}(\operatorname{enh}(\operatorname{Corr}(\operatorname{Sch}),\operatorname{DGCat}),\operatorname{Funct}(\operatorname{Adj},\operatorname{AGCat}))\subset\\
\subset\operatorname{Funct}^{\operatorname{DGCat}}(\operatorname{enh}(\operatorname{Corr}(\operatorname{Sch}),\operatorname{DGCat}),\operatorname{Funct}([1],\operatorname{AGCat})).

By
2.7.4
and
A.5.9
, we need to show the following:

(1)

For every scheme
Z
and another scheme
X
, the functor

(4.23)

\operatorname{Shv}(X)\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(Z)\overset{\boxtimes}{\to}\operatorname{Shv}(X\times Z)

admits a right adjoint.

(2)

For
{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Shv}(X^{\prime}\times X^{\prime\prime})
, the diagram

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Shv}(X^{\prime})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(Z)@>{}>{}>\operatorname{Shv}(X^{\prime}\times Z)\\
@V{[{\mathcal{F}}]\otimes\operatorname{Id}}V{}V@V{}V{[{\mathcal{F}}]}V\\
\operatorname{Shv}(X^{\prime\prime})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(Z)@>{}>{}>\operatorname{Shv}(X^{\prime\prime}\times Z)\end{CD}

satisfies the right Beck-Chevalley condition, where the notation
[-]
is as in Sect.
2.4.3
.

(3)

For
{\mathcal{G}}\in\operatorname{Shv}(Z_{1}\times Z_{2})
, the diagram

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Shv}(X)\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(Z_{1})@>{}>{}>\operatorname{Shv}(X\times Z_{1})\\
@V{\operatorname{Id}\otimes[{\mathcal{G}}]}V{}V@V{}V{[{\mathcal{G}}]}V\\
\operatorname{Shv}(X)\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(Z_{2})@>{}>{}>\operatorname{Shv}(X\times Z_{2})\end{CD}

satisfies the right Beck-Chevalley condition.

Note, however, that conditions (2) and (3) amount to the same, up to swapping

X
and
Z
.

Condition (1) follows from the fact that the functor (
4.23
)
maps compact objects to compact objects.

To prove condition (2), we can assume that
{\mathcal{F}}
is compact (i.e., constructible). In this case,
the functor
[{\mathcal{F}}]
admits a left adjoint, which is explicitly given by

{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime\prime}\mapsto(p^{\prime})_{!}({{\symAMSb D}}_{X^{\prime}\times X^{\prime\prime}}({\mathcal{F}})\otimes(p^{\prime\prime})^{*}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime\prime})),

where
p^{\prime},p^{\prime\prime}
are the projections from
X^{\prime}\times X^{\prime\prime}
to
X^{\prime}
and
X^{\prime\prime}
, respectively.

Now condition (2) follows from the fact that the rotated diagram

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Shv}(X^{\prime})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(Z)@>{[{\mathcal{F}}]\otimes\operatorname{Id}}>{}>\operatorname{Shv}(X^{\prime\prime})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(Z)\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
\operatorname{Shv}(X^{\prime}\times Z)@>{{\mathcal{F}}}>{}>\operatorname{Shv}(X^{\prime\prime}\times Z)\end{CD}

satisfies the
left
Beck-Chevalley condition.

∎[Proposition
4.5.3
]

4.5.5.

Recall from
4.1.5
that the adjunction
Equation

4.18
is canonically ambidexterous.
A consequence of
4.5.3
is a more precise description of the resulting

({\mathbf{e}},{\mathbf{i}})
-adjunction:

Corollary 4.5.6
.

The unit of the
({\mathbf{e}},{\mathbf{i}})
-adjunction

\operatorname{Id}_{\operatorname{AGCat}}\to{\mathbf{i}}\circ{\mathbf{e}}

is canonically isomorphic to the right adjoint of the counit of the adjunction
Equation

4.18
.

Proof.

Both the unit and the counit of
Equation

4.18
admit right adjoints: the unit because it’s
an isomorphism and the counit by
4.5.3
. Passing to right adjoints gives an adjunction
in the other direction.

By construction, the counit of this new
({\mathbf{e}},{\mathbf{i}})
-adjunction equals the counit from
4.1.5
:
both are given by the inverse of
\operatorname{Id}\to{\mathbf{e}}\circ{\mathbf{i}}
.

∎

4.6.
The notion of Verdier compatible algebraic stack

In this subsection we let
{\mathcal{Y}}
be an algebraic stack with a schematic diagonal.

4.6.1.

Note that we have an inclusion:

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})^{c}\subset\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})^{\operatorname{constr}}.

I.e., compact objects are constructible (but, in general, not vice versa). Recall from
[
AGKRRV1
, Sect. F.2.6]
that
{\mathcal{Y}}

is called
Verdier-compatible
if the subcategory
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})^{c}
is preserved by Verdier duality functor
8
8

8

We are insert the
superscript
\operatorname{Verdier}
in the notation in order to avoid conflating it with the functor
{{\symAMSb D}}_{\mathcal{Y}}
in (
4.16
). See
Remark
4.6.5
for the
relation between the two functors.

{{\symAMSb D}}^{\operatorname{Verdier}}_{\mathcal{Y}}:(\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})^{\operatorname{constr}})^{\operatorname{op}}\simeq\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})^{\operatorname{constr}}.

By
[
AGKRRV1
, Theorem F.2.8]
, all quasi-compact stacks of finite type that are locally quotient stacks are Verdier-compatible.
According to
[
AGKRRV1
, Conjecture F.2.7]
, all quasi-compact algebraic stack with a schematic diagonal are Verdier-compatible.

4.6.2.

By (
4.8
), we obtain that for a Verdier-compatible algebraic stack there is a canonical equivalence

(4.24)

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})^{\vee}\simeq\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})

4.6.3.

Since
{\mathcal{Y}}
has a schematic diagonal, we have a well-defined functor

(4.25)

\Omega_{\mathcal{Y}}:\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}}\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}),

see (
3.7
). We claim:

Proposition 4.6.4
.

Let
{\mathcal{Y}}
be an algebraic stack.
{\mathcal{Y}}
is Verdier-compatible if and only if it is tame.

Proof.

The transition functors in the colimit

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}}=\underset{S\in\operatorname{Sch}^{\operatorname{aff}}_{/{\mathcal{Y}}}}{\operatorname{colim}}\ \operatorname{Shv}(S)

preserve compact objects. In particular, the compact objects in the colimit
are generated by objects of the form

\operatorname{ins}_{f}(\mathcal{F})\in\underset{S\in\operatorname{Sch}^{\operatorname{aff}}_{/{\mathcal{Y}}}}{\operatorname{colim}}\ \operatorname{Shv}(S),

for a map
f:S\to{\mathcal{Y}}
,
{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Shv}(S)^{\operatorname{constr}}
, and where

\operatorname{ins}_{f}:\operatorname{Shv}(S)\to\underset{S\in\operatorname{Sch}^{\operatorname{aff}}_{/{\mathcal{Y}}}}{\operatorname{colim}}\ \operatorname{Shv}(S)

is the tautological functor.

By definition,
{\mathcal{Y}}
is Verdier-compatible if the Verdier dual of every compact object of
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})
is compact.
The compact objects in
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})
are generated by objects of the form

f_{!}(\mathcal{F})
for a map
f:S\to{\mathcal{Y}}
and
\mathcal{F}\in\operatorname{Shv}(S)^{\operatorname{constr}}
. The Verdier dual of
f_{!}(\mathcal{F})
is given by

f_{*}({\symAMSb D}_{S}(\mathcal{F}))\simeq\Omega_{\mathcal{Y}}\Bigl(\operatorname{ins}_{f}({\symAMSb D}_{\mathcal{Y}}(\mathcal{F}))\Bigr).

In particular, if
Equation

3.7
is an equivalence, the objects
f_{*}({\symAMSb D}(\mathcal{F}))

are compact and hence
{\mathcal{Y}}
is Verdier-compatible.

Now, suppose that
{\mathcal{Y}}
is Verdier-compatible. In order to show that (
3.7
) is an equivalence,
it suffices to show that its dual functor is an equivalence. However, it is easy to see that the composition

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\overset{\text{\eqref{e:Verdier as self-duality stack}}}{\simeq}\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})^{\vee}\overset{\Omega_{\mathcal{Y}}^{\vee}}{\to}\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})^{\vee}_{\operatorname{co}}\overset{\text{\eqref{e:dual of co}}}{\simeq}\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})

is the identity functor.

∎

Remark 4.6.5
.

Note that in the process of proof of Proposition
4.6.4
, we have shown that for
a Verider-compatible algebaric stack, the equivalence (
4.24
) equals

(4.26)

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})^{\vee}\overset{\text{\eqref{e:dual of co}}}{\simeq}\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})_{\operatorname{co}}\overset{\Omega_{\mathcal{Y}}}{\to}\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}).

In particular, the equivalence

(\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})^{c})^{\operatorname{op}}\simeq\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})^{c}.

induced by
{{\symAMSb D}}^{\operatorname{Verdier}}_{\mathcal{Y}}
equals
{{\symAMSb D}}_{\mathcal{Y}}
from (
4.16
).

Remark 4.6.6
.

Let
f:{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\to{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}
be a map between Verdier-compatible algebraic stacks. Recall the functor

f_{\blacktriangle}:\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2}),

see Sect.
3.7.3
.

It is not difficult to show that
f_{\blacktriangle}
is the ind-extension of the functor

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})^{c}\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2})^{c},

obtained by restriction from the
usual

f_{*}
, defined to be the (a priori, non-colimit preserving)
right adjoint of the functor
f^{*}
, see Remark
3.1.2
.

Thus,
f_{\blacktriangle}
has the same meaning as in
[
GaVa
, Sect. 1.2]
and
[
AGKRRV2
, Sect. A.2.3]
.

Remark 4.6.7
.

We note also that for a map
f:{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\to{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}
between Verdier-compatible algebraic stacks,
we have a natural transformation

(4.27)

f_{\blacktriangle}\to f_{*}.

Namely, (
4.27
) is obtained by adjunction from the natural transformation

(4.28)

f^{*}\circ f_{\blacktriangle}\to\operatorname{Id},

which in its turn described as follows:

For any
(X,g)\in\operatorname{Sch}_{/{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}}
, the precomposition of (
4.28
) with
g_{*}
is the
natural transformation

f^{*}\circ f_{\blacktriangle}\circ g_{*}\overset{g\text{ is schematic}}{\simeq}f^{*}\circ f_{\blacktriangle}\circ g_{\blacktriangle}\simeq f^{*}\circ(f\circ g)_{\blacktriangle}\overset{f\circ g\text{ is schematic}}{\simeq}f^{*}\circ(f\circ g)_{*}\overset{\text{unit}}{\to}\\
\to g_{*}\circ g^{*}\circ f^{*}\circ(f\circ g)_{*}\simeq g_{*}\circ(f\circ g)^{*}\circ(f\circ g)_{*}\overset{\text{counit}}{\to}g_{*}.

4.6.8.

We give the following definitions:

•

{\mathcal{Y}}
is
universally Verdier-compatible
if
{\mathcal{Y}}\underset{B}{\times}X
is Verdier-compatible for every scheme
X
;

•

{\mathcal{Y}}
is
AG Verdier-compatible
if both
{\mathcal{Y}}
and
{\mathcal{Y}}\underset{B}{\times}{\mathcal{Y}}
are universally Verdier-compatible.

From Proposition
4.6.4
we obtain:

Corollary 4.6.9
.

(a)
{\mathcal{Y}}
is universally Verdier-compatible if and only if it is universally tame.

(b)

{\mathcal{Y}}
is AG Verdier-compatible if and only if it is AG tame.

From
3.6.11
, we obtain:

Corollary 4.6.10
.

If
{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}
are AG Verdier compatible, then so is
{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\underset{B}{\times}{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}
.

Remark 4.6.11
.

Let
{\mathcal{Y}}
be AG Verdier-compatible. It follows from Remark
4.6.5
and Corollary
4.4.5

that the counit of the self-duality (
4.24
) is given by formula
(
4.14
).

However, one can show that this formula for the counit is valid for
any
Verdier compatible
prestack, see
[
DrGa
, Sect. 9]
.

5.
Computing trace in
\operatorname{AGCat}

In this subsection we perform the trace calculation, deriving formula (
0.7
),
and relate it to Grothendieck’s sheaves-functions correspondence.

5.1.
The Grothendieck sheaves-functions correspondence

In this subsection we let
{\mathcal{Y}}
be a Verdier-compatible algebraic stack over
\overline{{{\symAMSb F}}}_{q}
, defined over
{{\symAMSb F}}_{q}
.

5.1.1.

Let

\operatorname{Frob}_{\mathcal{Y}}:{\mathcal{Y}}\to{\mathcal{Y}}

denote the geometric Frobenius morphism. (We will simply write
{\operatorname{Frob}}
if no confusion is likely to occur.)

Suppose that
{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})^{c}\subset\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})^{\operatorname{constr}}
is an object equipped with a (weak) Weil structure, i.e. a map

{\operatorname{Frob}}^{*}({\mathcal{F}})\to{\mathcal{F}},

or, equivalently, a map

\alpha:{\mathcal{F}}\to\operatorname{Frob}_{*}({\mathcal{F}}).

Given this data, the sheaves-functions correspondence associates to
{\mathcal{F}}
a function

\mathsf{funct}({\mathcal{F}},\alpha)\in\mathsf{Funct}({\mathcal{Y}}({\symAMSb F}_{q}),\overline{{{\symAMSb Q}}}_{\ell})

on the set of
{{\symAMSb F}}_{q}
-points of
{\mathcal{Y}}
.

Given a point

y\in{\mathcal{Y}}({{\symAMSb F}}_{q}),

the value of
\mathsf{funct}({\mathcal{F}},\alpha)
at
y
is the trace of Frobenius acting on the *-stalk
{\mathcal{F}}_{y}
.

5.1.2.

Let us rewrite the above construction in a more categorical language. First, we note:

Lemma 5.1.3
.

There is a canonical isomorphism

{\operatorname{Frob}}_{*}\simeq{\operatorname{Frob}}_{\blacktriangle};

in particular, the functor
{\operatorname{Frob}}_{*}
is a map in
\operatorname{DGCat}
.

Proof.

Recall that for a map between (Verdier-compatible) algebraic stacks we have a natural transformation

(-)_{\blacktriangle}\to(-)_{*},

see (
4.27
). We claim that this map is an isomorphism for the geometric Frobenius map.

Recall that the two functors coincide on compact objects, while
(-)_{\blacktriangle}
preserves
colimits. Hence, the natural transformation between them is an isomorphism if and only if

(-)_{*}
also preserves colimits.

We claim that this is the case for the Frobenius map. In fact, we claim that
{\operatorname{Frob}}_{*}

is an equivalence. Since
{\operatorname{Frob}}_{*}
is by definition the right adjoint of
{\operatorname{Frob}}^{*}
,
it suffices to show that the latter is an equivalence.
9
9

9

Here is an alternative argument:
we can write
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})
as the limit of
\operatorname{Shv}(X)
under *-pullbacks for schemes
X
mapping to
{\mathcal{Y}}
.
Since for each
{\operatorname{Frob}}_{X}^{*}
is an equivalence, so is
{\operatorname{Frob}}_{\mathcal{Y}}^{*}
.

To prove this, it suffices to show that for any scheme
X
mapping to
{\mathcal{Y}}
, pullback with respect to the map

{\operatorname{Frob}}_{{\mathcal{Y}}/X}:X\underset{{\mathcal{Y}},{\operatorname{Frob}}_{\mathcal{Y}}}{\times}{\mathcal{Y}}\to X

induces an equivalence on
\operatorname{Shv}(-)
.

Note that the map
{\operatorname{Frob}}_{X}:X\to X
factors as

X\to X\underset{{\mathcal{Y}},{\operatorname{Frob}}}{\times}{\mathcal{Y}}\overset{{\operatorname{Frob}}_{{\mathcal{Y}}/X}}{\to}X,

with the first arrow being radicial, and hence pullback with respect to it
is an equivalence.

This implies that
{\operatorname{Frob}}_{{\mathcal{Y}}/X}^{*}
is an equivalence by the 2-out-3 property, since

{\operatorname{Frob}}_{X}^{*}
is an equivalence.

∎

5.1.4.

Thanks to Lemma
5.1.3
, it makes sense to consider the object

\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Frob}_{*},\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}))\in\operatorname{Vect}.

Furthermore, the pair
({\mathcal{F}},\alpha)
defines a class

\operatorname{cl}({\mathcal{F}},\alpha)\in\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Frob}_{*},\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})),

see
[
GKRV
, Sect. 3.4.3]
.

5.1.5.

We claim that there is a natural map, called “naive local term”

(5.1)

\operatorname{LT}^{\operatorname{naive}}:\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Frob}_{*},\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}))\to\mathsf{Funct}({\mathcal{Y}}({{\symAMSb F}}_{q}),\overline{{{\symAMSb Q}}}_{\ell})

that can be described as follows:

For every
y\in{\mathcal{Y}}(\overline{{{\symAMSb F}}}_{q})
, let

i_{y}:\operatorname{pt}\to{\mathcal{Y}}

denote the inclusion of
y
. Consider the functor

i_{y}^{*}:\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\to\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{pt})=\operatorname{Vect}.

If
y\in{\mathcal{Y}}({{\symAMSb F}}_{q})
, we have

(i_{y})^{*}\circ{\operatorname{Frob}}_{*}\simeq(i_{y})^{*}.

Hence, by the functoriality of the categorical trace (see
[
GKRV
, Sect. 3.4.1]
), we obtain a map

\operatorname{Tr}({\operatorname{Frob}},i_{y}^{*}):\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Frob}_{*},\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}))\to\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},\operatorname{Vect})\simeq\overline{{{\symAMSb Q}}}_{\ell}.

The map
\operatorname{LT}^{\operatorname{naive}}
is defined so that the composition

\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Frob}_{*},\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}))\to\mathsf{Funct}({\mathcal{Y}}({{\symAMSb F}}_{q}),\overline{{{\symAMSb Q}}}_{\ell})\overset{\operatorname{ev}_{y}}{\to}\overline{{{\symAMSb Q}}}_{\ell}

is the above map
\operatorname{Tr}({\operatorname{Frob}},i_{y}^{*})
.

Note, however, that the local term map
Equation

5.1
is typically
not
an isomorphism.

5.1.6.

Unwinding, we obtain that

(5.2)

\mathsf{funct}({\mathcal{F}},\alpha)=\operatorname{LT}^{\operatorname{naive}}(\operatorname{cl}({\mathcal{F}},\alpha)).

5.1.7.

In this section we will show that the Grothendieck sheaves-functions correspondence and the local term map have natural
interpretations via
\operatorname{AGCat}
.

Specifically, we will show that

(5.3)

\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Frob}_{*},\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}))\simeq\mathsf{Funct}({\mathcal{Y}}({{\symAMSb F}}_{q}),\overline{{{\symAMSb Q}}}_{\ell}),

where the trace in the left-hand side is taking place in the symmetric monoidal 2-category
\operatorname{AGCat}

and so is an object of

\operatorname{Maps}_{\operatorname{AGCat}}(\mathbb{1}_{\operatorname{AGCat}},\mathbb{1}_{\operatorname{AGCat}})\simeq\operatorname{Vect}.

The isomorphism (
5.3
), which we will denote by
\operatorname{LT}^{\operatorname{AG}}
, “corrects” the failure of

\operatorname{LT}^{\operatorname{naive}}
to be an isomorphism. That said, the map
\operatorname{LT}^{\operatorname{naive}}
also
has a natural interpretation via
\operatorname{AGCat}
, see Theorem
5.5.5
.

5.2.
Traces in
\operatorname{AGCat}

In this subsection, we let
{\mathcal{Y}}
be an AG tame prestack.

5.2.1.

Let
{\mathcal{F}}
be an object of
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times{\mathcal{Y}})
. We can think of
{\mathcal{F}}
as a map

\mathbb{1}_{\operatorname{AGCat}}\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}\times{\mathcal{Y}})\overset{\text{Corollary~\ref{c:hom verd comp}}}{\simeq}\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{AGCat}}(\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}),\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})),

i.e., an object in
\operatorname{Maps}_{\operatorname{AGCat}}(\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}),\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}))
, which we will denote by
\underline{[{\mathcal{F}}]}
.

Since
\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})
is dualizable, it makes sense to consider

(5.4)

\operatorname{Tr}(\underline{[{\mathcal{F}}]},\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}))\in\operatorname{Maps}_{\operatorname{AGCat}}(\mathbb{1}_{\operatorname{AGCat}},\mathbb{1}_{\operatorname{AGCat}})\simeq\operatorname{Vect}.

5.2.2.

We claim:

Proposition 5.2.3
.

The object (
5.4
) identifies canonically with

\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{\blacktriangle}({\mathcal{Y}},\Delta_{\mathcal{Y}}^{!}({\mathcal{F}})).

In the above formula and elsewhere,

\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{\blacktriangle}({\mathcal{Y}},-):=(p_{\mathcal{Y}})_{\blacktriangle}.

Proof of Proposition
5.2.3
.

By definition,
\operatorname{Tr}(\underline{[{\mathcal{F}}]},\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}))
is the composition

\mathbb{1}_{\operatorname{AGCat}}\overset{\operatorname{unit}}{\to}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})\overset{\operatorname{id}\otimes\underline{[{\mathcal{F}}]}}{\longrightarrow}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})\overset{\operatorname{counit}}{\to}\mathbb{1}_{\operatorname{AGCat}}.

Using Corollary
3.4.10
, we identify

(5.5)

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}),

and by definition, under this identification, the composition

\mathbb{1}_{\operatorname{AGCat}}\overset{\operatorname{unit}}{\to}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})\overset{\operatorname{id}\otimes\underline{[{\mathcal{F}}]}}{\longrightarrow}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}),

viewed as an object of

\operatorname{Maps}(\mathbb{1}_{\operatorname{AGCat}},\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}))\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}\times{\mathcal{Y}})(\operatorname{pt})\simeq\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}),

equals
{\mathcal{F}}
.

Thus, it suffices to show that the map

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}\times{\mathcal{Y}})\overset{\text{\eqref{e:sq Y}}}{\simeq}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})\overset{\operatorname{counit}}{\to}\mathbb{1}_{\operatorname{AGCat}},

evaluated on
\operatorname{pt}
equals the map

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times{\mathcal{Y}})\overset{\Delta_{\mathcal{Y}}^{!}}{\to}\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\overset{\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{\blacktriangle}({\mathcal{Y}},-)}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Vect}.

However, this is implied by Corollary
3.7.10
.

∎

5.2.4.

We will denote the isomorphism established in the above proposition by

\operatorname{LT}^{\operatorname{AG}}:\operatorname{Tr}(\underline{[{\mathcal{F}}]},\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}))\simeq\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{\blacktriangle}({\mathcal{Y}},\Delta_{\mathcal{Y}}^{!}({\mathcal{F}})).

5.2.5.

We now consider a particular case of the above situation:

Let

(5.6)

\textstyle{\mathcal{Y}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\mathcal{Z}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{c_{l}}
\scriptstyle{c_{r}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{Y}}

be a correspondence, where both
{\mathcal{Z}}
and

\operatorname{Fix}({\mathcal{Z}}):={\mathcal{Z}}\underset{{\mathcal{Y}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}}{\times}{\mathcal{Y}}

are also AG tame.

Consider the object

(c_{l},c_{r})_{\blacktriangle}(\omega_{\mathcal{Z}})\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{X}}\times{\mathcal{X}}).

defines a morphism. Denote by

\underline{[{\mathcal{Z}}]}:=[(c_{l},c_{r})_{\blacktriangle}(\omega_{\mathcal{Z}})]

the corresponding
object in
\operatorname{Maps}_{\operatorname{AGCat}}(\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}),\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}))
.

5.2.6.

As a special case of Proposition
5.2.3
, and using Lemma
3.7.4
,
we obtain:

Corollary 5.2.7
.

The object

\operatorname{Tr}(\underline{[{\mathcal{Z}}]},\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}))\in\operatorname{Maps}_{\operatorname{AGCat}}(\mathbb{1}_{\operatorname{AGCat}},\mathbb{1}_{\operatorname{AGCat}})\simeq\operatorname{Vect}

identifies canonically with

\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{\blacktriangle}(\operatorname{Fix}(C),\omega_{\operatorname{Fix}(C)}).

5.3.
Functoriality properties

5.3.1.

Let
{\mathcal{Y}}_{i}
,
i=1,2
be AG tame prestacks and
{\mathcal{F}}_{i}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{i}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{i})
.

Let now
{\mathcal{H}}
be an object in
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})
. Consider the corresponding map

\underline{[{\mathcal{H}}]}:\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2}).

Let us be given a map

\alpha:{\mathcal{F}}_{1}\star{\mathcal{H}}\to{\mathcal{H}}\star{\mathcal{F}}_{2}

in
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})
, where
(-)\star(-)
denote the usual convolution operation:

{\mathcal{F}}_{1,2}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}),\,\,{\mathcal{F}}_{2,3}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{3})\mapsto\\
{\mathcal{F}}_{1,2}\star{\mathcal{F}}_{2,3}:=(p_{1}\times p_{3})_{\blacktriangle}(\operatorname{id}\times\Delta_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}}\times\operatorname{id})^{!}({\mathcal{F}}_{1,2}\boxtimes{\mathcal{F}}_{2,3}).

5.3.2.

Assume now that the 1-morphism
\underline{[{\mathcal{H}}]}
admits a right adjoint in
\operatorname{AGCat}
. I.e., there exists

{\mathcal{H}}^{R}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{1})
, equipped with the maps

\Delta_{*}(\omega_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}})\overset{\operatorname{u}}{\longrightarrow}{\mathcal{H}}\star{\mathcal{H}}^{R}\text{ and }{\mathcal{H}}^{R}\star{\mathcal{H}}\overset{\operatorname{co-u}}{\longrightarrow}\Delta_{*}(\omega_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}}),

satisfying the usual adjunction axioms.

In this case, by
[
GKRV
, Sect. 3.3.4]
, the map
\alpha
induces a map

(5.7)

\operatorname{Tr}(\underline{[{\mathcal{F}}_{1}]},\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}))\to\operatorname{Tr}(\underline{[{\mathcal{F}}_{2}]},\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2})).

5.3.3.

On the other hand, we claim that the morphisms
\alpha
,
\operatorname{u}
and
\operatorname{co-u}
define a map

(5.8)

\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{\blacktriangle}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1},\Delta^{!}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}}({\mathcal{F}}_{1}))\to\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{\blacktriangle}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2},\Delta^{!}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}}({\mathcal{F}}_{2})).

Namely, (
5.8
) is the composition the following three morphisms:

(1)

The map

\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{\blacktriangle}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1},\Delta^{!}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}}({\mathcal{F}}_{1}))\to\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{\blacktriangle}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2},{}^{\prime}\Delta^{!}({\mathcal{F}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathcal{H}}\boxtimes{\mathcal{H}}^{R})),

induced by
\operatorname{u}
,
where
{}^{\prime}\Delta
is the morphism

{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}\to{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{1},\quad(y^{\prime}_{1},y^{\prime\prime}_{1},y_{2})\mapsto(y^{\prime}_{1},y^{\prime\prime}_{1},y^{\prime\prime}_{1},y_{2},y_{2},y^{\prime}_{1});

(2)

The map
