\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{\blacktriangle}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2},{}^{\prime}\Delta^{!}({\mathcal{F}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathcal{H}}\boxtimes{\mathcal{H}}^{R}))\to\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{\blacktriangle}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2},{}^{\prime\prime}\Delta^{!}({\mathcal{H}}\boxtimes{\mathcal{F}}_{2}\boxtimes{\mathcal{H}}^{R})),

induced by
\alpha
, where
{}^{\prime\prime}\Delta
is the morphism

{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}\to{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{1},\quad(y_{1},y^{\prime}_{2},y^{\prime\prime}_{2})\mapsto(y_{1},y^{\prime}_{2},y^{\prime}_{2},y^{\prime\prime}_{2},y^{\prime\prime}_{2},y_{1});

(3)

The map

\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{\blacktriangle}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2},{}^{\prime\prime}\Delta^{!}({\mathcal{H}}\boxtimes{\mathcal{F}}_{2}\boxtimes{\mathcal{H}}^{R}))\to\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{\blacktriangle}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2},\Delta^{!}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}}({\mathcal{F}}_{2})),

induced by
\operatorname{co-u}
.

5.3.4.

Unwinding, we obtain:

Lemma 5.3.5
.

The following diagram commutes:

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Tr}(\underline{[{\mathcal{F}}_{1}]},\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}))@>{\text{\eqref{e:UL alpha 1}}}>{}>\operatorname{Tr}(\underline{[{\mathcal{F}}_{2}]},\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2}))\\
@V{\text{Proposition~\ref{p:Tr sheaf}}}V{\sim}V@V{\sim}V{\text{Proposition~\ref{p:Tr sheaf}}}V\\
\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{\blacktriangle}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1},\Delta^{!}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}}({\mathcal{F}}_{1}))@>{\text{\eqref{e:UL alpha 2}}}>{}>\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{\blacktriangle}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2},\Delta^{!}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}}({\mathcal{F}}_{2})).\end{CD}

5.3.6.

Here is a particular case of how Lemma
5.3.5
can be applied:

Let
h:{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\to{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}
be a
proper
schematic map. Note that by Proposition
2.7.2
,
the corresponding 1-morphism

h_{*}:\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2})

admits a right adjoint, given by
h^{!}
.

Let us be given a map

\alpha:(\operatorname{id}\times f)_{*}({\mathcal{F}}_{1})\to(f\times\operatorname{id})^{!}({\mathcal{F}}_{2}).

Note that we have a canonical map

(5.9)

\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{\blacktriangle}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1},\Delta^{!}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}}({\mathcal{F}}_{1}))\to\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{\blacktriangle}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1},\Delta^{!}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}}({\mathcal{F}}_{2}))

defined as follows:

\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{\blacktriangle}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1},\Delta^{!}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}}({\mathcal{F}}_{1}))\to\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{\blacktriangle}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1},\Delta^{!}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}}\circ(\operatorname{id}\times f)^{!}\circ(\operatorname{id}\times f)_{*}({\mathcal{F}}_{1}))=\\
=\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{\blacktriangle}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1},\operatorname{Graph}_{f}^{!}\circ(\operatorname{id}\times f)_{*}({\mathcal{F}}_{1}))\overset{\alpha}{\to}\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{\blacktriangle}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1},\operatorname{Graph}_{f}^{!}\circ(f\times\operatorname{id})^{!}({\mathcal{F}}_{2}))\simeq\\
\simeq\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{\blacktriangle}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1},f^{!}\circ\Delta^{!}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}}({\mathcal{F}}_{2}))\simeq\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{\blacktriangle}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2},f_{*}\circ f^{!}\circ\Delta^{!}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}}({\mathcal{F}}_{2}))\to\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{\blacktriangle}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2},\Delta^{!}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}}({\mathcal{F}}_{2})).

We obtain:

Corollary 5.3.7
.

Under the identification of Proposition
5.2.3
, the morphism

\operatorname{Tr}(\underline{[{\mathcal{F}}_{1}]},\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}))\to\operatorname{Tr}(\underline{[{\mathcal{F}}_{2}]},\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2}))

induced by
f_{*}
and
\alpha
corresponds to the map (
5.9
).

5.3.8.

We now consider another case of Lemma
5.3.5
. Namely, given
{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times{\mathcal{Y}})
, we will
explain a geometric procedure that produces elements in

\operatorname{Tr}(\underline{[{\mathcal{F}}]},\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}))\simeq\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{\blacktriangle}({\mathcal{Y}},\Delta^{!}_{{\mathcal{Y}}}({\mathcal{F}})).

5.3.9.

Let
{\mathcal{G}}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})

be a compact object. We claim that the resulting 1-morphism

[{\mathcal{G}}]:\mathbb{1}_{\operatorname{AGCat}}\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})

admits a right adjoint, given by
[{{\symAMSb D}}_{\mathcal{Y}}({\mathcal{G}})]
, where
{{\symAMSb D}}_{\mathcal{Y}}
is an
in (
4.16
).

Indeed, this follows from Proposition
2.7.2
, using the fact that for

X\in\operatorname{Sch},\,\,{\mathcal{G}}^{\prime}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times X)\text{ and }{\mathcal{G}}_{X}\in\operatorname{Shv}(X),

we have

{\mathcal{H}}\operatorname{om}_{\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times X)}({\mathcal{G}}\boxtimes{\mathcal{G}}_{X},{\mathcal{G}}^{\prime})\simeq{\mathcal{H}}\operatorname{om}_{\operatorname{Shv}(X)}\left({\mathcal{G}}_{X},(p_{2})_{\blacktriangle}(p_{1}^{!}({{\symAMSb D}}_{\mathcal{Y}}({\mathcal{G}}))\overset{!}{\otimes}{\mathcal{G}}^{\prime})\right),

where
p_{1}
and
p_{2}
are the two projections from
{\mathcal{Y}}\times X
to
{\mathcal{Y}}
and
X
, respectively.

Indeed, we can assume that
{\mathcal{G}}_{X}
is compact, and both sides in the above formula
identify with

\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{\blacktriangle}\left({\mathcal{Y}}\times X,({{\symAMSb D}}_{\mathcal{Y}}({\mathcal{G}})\boxtimes{{\symAMSb D}}_{X}({\mathcal{G}}_{X}))\overset{!}{\otimes}{\mathcal{G}}^{\prime}\right)\overset{\text{\eqref{e:Verdier ext}}}{\simeq}\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{\blacktriangle}\left({\mathcal{Y}}\times X,{{\symAMSb D}}_{{\mathcal{Y}}\times X}({\mathcal{G}}\boxtimes{\mathcal{G}}_{X})\overset{!}{\otimes}{\mathcal{G}}^{\prime}\right).

5.3.10.

Let us now be given a map

\alpha:{\mathcal{G}}\to[{\mathcal{F}}]({\mathcal{G}})\simeq{\mathcal{G}}\star{\mathcal{F}}.

By Sect.
5.3.2
, to this data, we associate a map

\overline{{{\symAMSb Q}}}_{\ell}\to\operatorname{Tr}(\underline{[{\mathcal{F}}]},\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})),

i.e., an element of
\operatorname{Tr}(\underline{[{\mathcal{F}}]},\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}))
, which we will denote by

\operatorname{cl}(\underline{[{\mathcal{G}}]},\alpha)\in\operatorname{Tr}(\underline{[{\mathcal{F}}]},\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})).

5.3.11.

Let us compute this element in terms of the identification of Proposition
5.2.3
. From Lemma
5.3.5
, we obtain
that this element is the image of the canonical element

\operatorname{u}\in\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{\blacktriangle}({\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{G}}\overset{!}{\otimes}{{\symAMSb D}}_{\mathcal{Y}}({\mathcal{G}}))

(the unit of the adjunction) under the map

\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{\blacktriangle}({\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{G}}\overset{!}{\otimes}{{\symAMSb D}}_{\mathcal{Y}}({\mathcal{G}}))\simeq\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{\blacktriangle}({\mathcal{Y}},\Delta_{\mathcal{Y}}^{!}({\mathcal{G}}\boxtimes{{\symAMSb D}}_{\mathcal{Y}}({\mathcal{G}})))\to\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{\blacktriangle}({\mathcal{Y}},\Delta_{\mathcal{Y}}^{!}({\mathcal{F}})),

induced by a map

{\mathcal{G}}\boxtimes{{\symAMSb D}}_{\mathcal{Y}}({\mathcal{G}})\to{\mathcal{F}},

obtained in the following three steps:

(1)

We start with the counit of the adjunction, i.e., a map

\operatorname{co-u}:{\mathcal{G}}\boxtimes{{\symAMSb D}}_{\mathcal{Y}}({\mathcal{G}})\to\Delta_{*}(\omega_{\mathcal{Y}});

(2)

We apply to it
[{\mathcal{F}}]
along the first factor and thus obtain a map

[{\mathcal{F}}]({\mathcal{G}})\boxtimes{{\symAMSb D}}_{\mathcal{Y}}({\mathcal{G}})\to{\mathcal{F}};

(3)

We precompose the latter map with

{\mathcal{G}}\boxtimes{{\symAMSb D}}_{\mathcal{Y}}({\mathcal{G}})\overset{\alpha\boxtimes\operatorname{id}}{\longrightarrow}[{\mathcal{F}}]({\mathcal{G}})\boxtimes{{\symAMSb D}}_{\mathcal{Y}}({\mathcal{G}}).

5.4.
Comparing traces in
\operatorname{AGCat}
and
\operatorname{DGCat}

In this subsection we continue to assume that
{\mathcal{Y}}
be an AG tame prestack, and we let
{\mathcal{F}}

be an object in
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times{\mathcal{Y}})
.

5.4.1.

We now consider the induced action of
\underline{[{\mathcal{F}}]}
on
{\mathbf{e}}(\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}))\simeq\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})
; denote the
corresponding endofunctor
[{\mathcal{F}}]
. Explicitly, it is given by

[{\mathcal{F}}]({\mathcal{F}}_{1}):={\mathcal{F}}_{1}\mapsto(p_{2})_{\blacktriangle}(p_{1}^{!}({\mathcal{F}}_{1})\overset{!}{\otimes}{\mathcal{F}}).

We will now recall, following
[
GaVa
]
, the construction of a map

(5.10)

\operatorname{LT}^{\operatorname{true}}:\operatorname{Tr}([{\mathcal{F}}],\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}))\to\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{\blacktriangle}({\mathcal{Y}},\Delta_{\mathcal{Y}}^{!}({\mathcal{F}})).

5.4.2.

Consider the object

([{\mathcal{F}}]\otimes\operatorname{Id})(\operatorname{unit}_{\mathcal{Y}})\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}),

where
\operatorname{unit}_{\mathcal{Y}}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})
is the unit of the self-duality.

We claim:

Lemma 5.4.3
.

There is a canonical isomorphism

([{\mathcal{F}}]\otimes\operatorname{Id})(\operatorname{unit}_{\mathcal{Y}})\simeq(\boxtimes^{R})({\mathcal{F}}),

where
\boxtimes^{R}
is the right adjoint to

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\overset{\boxtimes}{\to}\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}).

Proof.

The assertion of the lemma can be reformulated as follows: for
{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times{\mathcal{Y}})
, the object

[{\mathcal{F}}]\in\operatorname{Funct}(\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}),\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}))\simeq\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})^{\vee}\otimes\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\simeq\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})

is given by
\boxtimes^{R}({\mathcal{F}})
.

In fact, this is true for
{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})
and
[{\mathcal{F}}]\in\operatorname{Funct}(\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}),\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2}))
. This is

[
GaVa
, Lemma 4.5 and footnote 4]
, which we reproduce here for completeness.

It suffices to show that for
{\mathcal{F}}_{1}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})^{c}
and
{\mathcal{F}}_{2}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2})^{c}
,

(5.11)

{\mathcal{H}}\operatorname{om}_{\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2})}({\mathcal{F}}_{1}\otimes{\mathcal{F}}_{2},[{\mathcal{F}}])\simeq{\mathcal{H}}\operatorname{om}_{\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})}({\mathcal{F}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathcal{F}}_{2},{\mathcal{F}}).

Unwinding, we obtain that the left-hand side in (
5.11
) is

{\mathcal{H}}\operatorname{om}_{\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})}({\mathcal{F}}_{1},[{\mathcal{F}}]({{\symAMSb D}}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}}({\mathcal{F}}_{2})))\simeq{\mathcal{H}}\operatorname{om}_{\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})}\left({\mathcal{F}}_{1},(p_{1})_{\blacktriangle}({\mathcal{F}}\overset{!}{\otimes}p_{2}^{!}({{\symAMSb D}}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}}({\mathcal{F}}_{2})))\right)\simeq\\
\simeq\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{\blacktriangle}\left({\mathcal{Y}}_{1},{{\symAMSb D}}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}}({\mathcal{F}}_{1})\overset{!}{\otimes}(p_{1})_{\blacktriangle}({\mathcal{F}}\overset{!}{\otimes}p_{2}^{!}({{\symAMSb D}}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}}({\mathcal{F}}_{2})))\right)\simeq\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{\blacktriangle}\left({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2},{\mathcal{F}}\overset{!}{\otimes}({{\symAMSb D}}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}}({\mathcal{F}}_{1})\boxtimes{{\symAMSb D}}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}}({\mathcal{F}}_{2}))\right)\simeq\\
\overset{\text{\eqref{e:Verdier ext}}}{\simeq}\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{\blacktriangle}\left({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2},{\mathcal{F}}\overset{!}{\otimes}{{\symAMSb D}}_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}}({\mathcal{F}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathcal{F}}_{2})\right),

which identifies with the right-hand side of (
5.11
), as desired.

∎

5.4.4.

From Lemma
5.4.3
we obtain a canonical map

(5.12)

\boxtimes(([{\mathcal{F}}]\otimes\operatorname{Id})(\operatorname{unit}_{\mathcal{Y}}))\simeq\boxtimes(\boxtimes^{R}({\mathcal{F}}))\to{\mathcal{F}}

By definition,

\operatorname{Tr}([{\mathcal{F}}],\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}))=\operatorname{counit}_{\mathcal{Y}}(([{\mathcal{F}}]\otimes\operatorname{Id})(\operatorname{unit}_{\mathcal{Y}})).

By Remark
4.6.11
, we obtain that

\operatorname{Tr}([{\mathcal{F}}],\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}))\simeq\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{\blacktriangle}\left({\mathcal{Y}},\Delta_{\mathcal{Y}}^{!}\left(\boxtimes(([{\mathcal{F}}]\otimes\operatorname{Id})(\operatorname{unit}_{\mathcal{Y}}))\right)\right).

Composing with (
5.12
) we obtain the desired map
\operatorname{LT}^{\operatorname{true}}
in
(
5.10
).

5.4.5.

Let
\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}\in\operatorname{AGCat}
be a dualizable object; in particular, by Corollary
4.4.3
,
{\mathbf{e}}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}})\in\operatorname{DGCat}
is also dualizable.
Since the functor

{\mathbf{i}}:\operatorname{DGCat}\to\operatorname{AGCat}

is symmetric monoidal, we have a map of dualizable objects

{\mathbf{i}}\circ{\mathbf{e}}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}})\to\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}.

By
4.5.3
, this 1-morphism admits a right adjoint.
Hence, by
[
GKRV
, Sect. 3.4.3]
for an endomorphism
\alpha
of
\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}
, we obtain a map

(5.13)

\operatorname{Tr}({\mathbf{i}}\circ{\mathbf{e}}(\alpha),{\mathbf{i}}\circ{\mathbf{e}}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}))\to\operatorname{Tr}(\alpha,\underline{{\mathbf{C}}})

in the category
\operatorname{Maps}_{\operatorname{AGCat}}(\mathbb{1}_{\operatorname{AGCat}},\mathbb{1}_{\operatorname{AGCat}})
.

5.4.6.

In addition, since
{\mathbf{i}}
is symmetric monoidal, we have

\operatorname{Tr}({\mathbf{i}}\circ{\mathbf{e}}(\alpha),{\mathbf{i}}\circ{\mathbf{e}}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}))\simeq{\mathbf{i}}(\operatorname{Tr}({\mathbf{e}}(\alpha),{\mathbf{e}}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}))),

where:

•

\operatorname{Tr}({\mathbf{e}}(\alpha),{\mathbf{e}}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}))\in\operatorname{Maps}_{\operatorname{DGCat}}(\mathbb{1}_{\operatorname{DGCat}},\mathbb{1}_{\operatorname{DGCat}})\simeq\operatorname{Vect}
;

•

The symbol
{\mathbf{i}}
in the right-hand side
indicates the functor

\operatorname{Vect}\simeq\operatorname{Maps}_{\operatorname{DGCat}}(\mathbb{1}_{\operatorname{DGCat}},\mathbb{1}_{\operatorname{DGCat}})\to\operatorname{Maps}_{\operatorname{AGCat}}(\mathbb{1}_{\operatorname{AGCat}},\mathbb{1}_{\operatorname{AGCat}})\simeq\operatorname{Vect},

induced by
{\mathbf{i}}
, which is (homotopic to) the identity.

5.4.7.

Summarizing, we obtain a map

(5.14)

\operatorname{Tr}({\mathbf{e}}(\alpha),{\mathbf{e}}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}))\to\operatorname{Tr}(\alpha,\underline{{\mathbf{C}}})

in
\operatorname{Vect}
.

5.4.8.

We apply the above discussion to
\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}=\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})
and
\alpha=\underline{[{\mathcal{F}}]}
, so that
{\mathbf{e}}(\alpha)=[{\mathcal{F}}]
.
Thus, (
5.14
) yields a map

(5.15)

\operatorname{Tr}([{\mathcal{F}}],\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}))\to\operatorname{Tr}(\underline{[{\mathcal{F}}]},\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})).

5.4.9.

Let
{\mathcal{G}}
be a compact object of
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})
equipped with a map

\alpha:{\mathcal{G}}\to[{\mathcal{F}}]({\mathcal{G}}).

By Sect.
5.3.10
, to this data we assign an element

\operatorname{cl}(\underline{[{\mathcal{G}}]},\alpha)\in\operatorname{Tr}(\underline{[{\mathcal{F}}]},\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})).

In addition, we can consider

\operatorname{cl}([{\mathcal{G}}],\alpha)\in\operatorname{Tr}(\underline{{\mathcal{F}}},\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})).

From the functoriality of the natural transformation (
5.14
), we obtain:

Lemma 5.4.10
.

The image image of
\operatorname{cl}([{\mathcal{G}}],\alpha)
under (
5.15
) equals

\operatorname{cl}(\underline{[{\mathcal{G}}]},\alpha)
.

5.4.11.

We are going to prove:

Theorem 5.4.12
.

The map

\operatorname{Tr}([{\mathcal{F}}],\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}))\overset{\text{\eqref{e:true again}}}{\to}\operatorname{Tr}(\underline{[{\mathcal{F}}]},\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}))\overset{\operatorname{LT}^{\operatorname{AG}}}{\simeq}\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{\blacktriangle}({\mathcal{Y}},\Delta_{\mathcal{Y}}^{!}({\mathcal{F}}))

equals the above map
\operatorname{LT}^{\operatorname{true}}
.

Proof.

We first consider the case when
{\mathcal{F}}
belongs to the full subcategory

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\subset\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}).

In this case, the assertion of the theorem is obtained by unraveling the definitions
(and the maps
\operatorname{LT}^{\operatorname{true}}
and (
5.15
) are isomorphisms).

Next we note that for a morphism
\alpha:{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\to{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime\prime}
in
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times{\mathcal{Y}})
, we have the commutative
diagrams

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Tr}([{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}],\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}))@>{\operatorname{LT}^{\operatorname{true}}}>{}>\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{\blacktriangle}({\mathcal{Y}},\Delta_{\mathcal{Y}}^{!}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}))\\
@V{\alpha}V{}V@V{}V{\alpha}V\\
\operatorname{Tr}([{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime\prime}],\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}))@>{\operatorname{LT}^{\operatorname{true}}}>{}>\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{\blacktriangle}({\mathcal{Y}},\Delta_{\mathcal{Y}}^{!}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime\prime}))\end{CD}

and

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Tr}([{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}],\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}))@>{\text{\eqref{e:true again}}}>{}>\operatorname{Tr}(\underline{[{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}]},\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}))@>{\operatorname{LT}^{\operatorname{AG}}}>{}>\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{\blacktriangle}({\mathcal{Y}},\Delta_{\mathcal{Y}}^{!}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}))\\
@V{\alpha}V{}V@V{\alpha}V{}V@V{}V{\alpha}V\\
\operatorname{Tr}([{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime\prime}],\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}))@>{\text{\eqref{e:true again}}}>{}>\operatorname{Tr}(\underline{[{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime\prime}]},\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}))@>{\operatorname{LT}^{\operatorname{AG}}}>{}>\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{\blacktriangle}({\mathcal{Y}},\Delta_{\mathcal{Y}}^{!}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime\prime})).\end{CD}

Hence, if the assertion of the theorem holds for
{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}
and the map

\operatorname{Tr}([{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}],\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}))\overset{\alpha}{\to}\operatorname{Tr}([{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime\prime}],\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}))

is an isomorphism, then it also holds for
{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime\prime}
.

Finally, we note that for
{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime\prime}:={\mathcal{F}}
and
{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}:=\boxtimes(\boxtimes^{R}({\mathcal{F}}))
, we have

{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\subset\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}),

and by Lemma
5.4.3
, the counit map

\boxtimes(\boxtimes^{R}({\mathcal{F}}))\to{\mathcal{F}}

induces an isomorphism

[\boxtimes(\boxtimes^{R}({\mathcal{F}}))]\to[{\mathcal{F}}]

(as endofunctors of
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})
), and hence also an isomorphism of traces.

∎

5.5.
The Frobenius correspondence

In this subsection we let
{\mathcal{Y}}
be an algebraic stack over
\overline{{{\symAMSb F}}}_{q}
, defined over
{{\symAMSb F}}_{q}
.
We will assume that
{\mathcal{Y}}
is AG Verdier compatible (and hence AG tame).

5.5.1.

Note that since
{\mathcal{Y}}
was assumed to be an algebraic stack, the stack

{\mathcal{Y}}^{\operatorname{Frob}}
is
algebro-geometrically discrete
, i.e., it is isomorphic to the groupoid

{\mathcal{Y}}({{\symAMSb F}}_{q})
with the trivial algebro-geometric structure (see
[
GaVa
, Lemma 6.4]
).

In particular,

\operatorname{C}_{\blacktriangle}({\mathcal{Y}}^{\operatorname{Frob}},\omega_{{\mathcal{Y}}^{\operatorname{Frob}}})\simeq\mathsf{Funct}({\mathcal{Y}}({\symAMSb F}_{q}),\overline{{{\symAMSb Q}}}_{\ell}).

5.5.2.

Taking (
5.6
) to be

\textstyle{\mathcal{Y}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\mathcal{Y}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{id}}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Frob}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{Y}},}

from Corollary
5.2.7
, we obtain:

Corollary 5.5.3
.

Let
{\mathcal{Y}}
be an AG Verdier-compatible algebraic stack defined over
{\symAMSb F}_{q}
.
Then

\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Frob}_{\blacktriangle};\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}))\simeq\mathsf{Funct}({\mathcal{Y}}({\symAMSb F}_{q}),\overline{{{\symAMSb Q}}}_{\ell}).

5.5.4.

Consider the composition

(5.16)

\operatorname{Tr}({\operatorname{Frob}}_{*},\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}))\overset{\text{Lemma~\ref{l:Frob triangle}}}{\simeq}\operatorname{Tr}({\operatorname{Frob}}_{\blacktriangle},\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}))\overset{\text{\eqref{e:true again}}}{\longrightarrow}\\
\to\operatorname{Tr}({\operatorname{Frob}}_{\blacktriangle},\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}))\overset{\text{Corollary~\ref{c:Tr Frob AG}}}{\simeq}\mathsf{Funct}({\mathcal{Y}}({\symAMSb F}_{q}),\overline{{{\symAMSb Q}}}_{\ell}).

We claim:

Theorem 5.5.5
.

The map (
5.16
) equals the map
\operatorname{LT}^{\operatorname{naive}}

of (
5.1
).

Proof.

By Theorem
5.4.12
, the composition (
5.16
) equals
\operatorname{LT}^{\operatorname{true}}
.
Now, in the particular case of the Frobenius correspondence,

\operatorname{LT}^{\operatorname{true}}=\operatorname{LT}^{\operatorname{naive}},

by one of the main results of
[
GaVa
]
, see Theorem 0.8 in
loc. cit.

∎

5.5.6.

Let now
{\mathcal{F}}
be an object in
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})
. We can view
{\mathcal{F}}
as a morphism

\underline{[{\mathcal{F}}]}:\mathbb{1}_{\operatorname{AGCat}}\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})

in
\operatorname{AGCat}
. Note that if
{\mathcal{F}}
is compact, then
[{\mathcal{F}}]
admits a right adjoint, see Sect.
5.3.9
.

Moreover, let
{\mathcal{F}}
be equipped with a weak Weil structure, i.e., a morphism

\alpha:{\mathcal{F}}\to{\operatorname{Frob}}_{*}({\mathcal{F}})\simeq{\operatorname{Frob}}_{\blacktriangle}({\mathcal{F}}).

Then by Sect.
5.3.10
, to this data we attach an element

\operatorname{cl}(\underline{[{\mathcal{F}}]},\alpha)\in\operatorname{Tr}({\operatorname{Frob}}_{\blacktriangle},\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})).

From Theorem
5.5.5
, Lemma
5.4.10
and (
5.2
), we obtain:

Corollary 5.5.7
.

The image of
\operatorname{cl}(\underline{[{\mathcal{F}}]},\alpha)
under

\operatorname{Tr}({\operatorname{Frob}}_{\blacktriangle},\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}))\overset{\text{Corollary~\ref{c:Tr Frob AG}}}{\simeq}\mathsf{Funct}({\mathcal{Y}}({\symAMSb F}_{q}),\overline{{{\symAMSb Q}}}_{\ell})

equals
\mathsf{funct}({\mathcal{F}},\alpha)
.

Appendix A
Recollections on enriched categories

This is a summary of the relevant parts of the theory of enriched
\infty
-categories developed in
[
GeHa
,
He
,
Hi1
,
Hi2
]
.

A.1.
The basics

A.1.1.

Recall that one of the (most convenient) frameworks for talking about
\infty
-categories
is via Segal spaces (see
[
GR1
, Chapter 10, Sect. 1]
for a review). In this approach, an
\infty
-category
{\mathcal{C}}

is encoded by a space
{\mathcal{C}}^{\operatorname{grpd}}
of objects of
{\mathbf{C}}
and a functor

{\mathcal{C}}^{\operatorname{grpd}}\times{\mathcal{C}}^{\operatorname{grpd}}\to{\operatorname{Spc}},\quad(c_{1},c_{2})\mapsto\operatorname{Maps}_{{\mathcal{C}}}(c_{1},c_{2}),

equipped with a multiplicative structure.

By contrast, when discussing enriched categories, it is often convenient to parameterize
its objects by another space.

A.1.2.

Let
{\mathcal{A}}
be a monoidal category and
{\mathcal{S}}
a space. To this pair, one associates the notion of

{\mathcal{S}}
-flagged
{\mathcal{A}}
-enriched category, see
[
GeHa
, Sect. 2.4]
.

An
{\mathcal{S}}
-flagged
{\mathcal{A}}
-enriched category

{\mathcal{C}}
consists of a functor

(A.1)

{\mathcal{S}}\times{\mathcal{S}}\to{\mathcal{A}},\quad(s_{1},s_{2})\mapsto\operatorname{Hom}^{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{S}},{\mathcal{C}}}(s_{1},s_{2}),\quad s_{1},s_{2}\in{\mathcal{S}},

endowed with a multiplicative structure

\operatorname{Hom}^{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{S}},{\mathcal{C}}}(s_{2},s_{3})\otimes\operatorname{Hom}^{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{S}},{\mathcal{C}}}(s_{1},s_{2})\to\operatorname{Hom}^{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{S}},{\mathcal{C}}}(s_{1},s_{3}),

equipped with a homotopy-coherent data of associativity.

A.1.3.
Example

One can take
{\mathcal{S}}=\{*\}
and
\operatorname{Hom}^{\mathcal{A}}(\{*\},\{*\})
to be any algebra object
A\in{\mathcal{A}}
.

A.1.4.

The totality of
{\mathcal{S}}
-flagged
{\mathcal{A}}
-enriched categories forms a category, denoted
\operatorname{Cat}^{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{S}})
.
Furthermore, we can form the “total space”
\operatorname{Cat}^{\mathcal{A}}({\operatorname{Spc}})
, equipped with a Cartesian fibration

(A.2)

\operatorname{Cat}^{\mathcal{A}}({\operatorname{Spc}})\to{\operatorname{Spc}}.

A.1.5.

Let
F:{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\to{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
be a
right-lax
monoidal functor. Given
({\mathcal{S}}_{1},{\mathcal{C}}_{1})\in\operatorname{Cat}^{{\mathcal{A}}_{1}}({\operatorname{Spc}})

and
({\mathcal{S}}_{2},{\mathcal{C}}_{2})\in\operatorname{Cat}^{{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}({\operatorname{Spc}})
, there is a natural notion of functor

({\mathcal{S}}_{1},{\mathcal{C}}_{1})\to({\mathcal{S}}_{2},{\mathcal{C}}_{2})

compatible with
F
. The collections of such functors forms a functor

(A.3)

(\operatorname{Cat}^{{\mathcal{A}}_{1}}({\operatorname{Spc}}))^{\operatorname{op}}\times\operatorname{Cat}^{{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}({\operatorname{Spc}})\to{\operatorname{Spc}}.

Keeping
({\mathcal{S}}_{1},{\mathcal{C}}_{1})
fixed and making
({\mathcal{S}}_{2},{\mathcal{C}}_{2})
variable, the functor (
A.3
) is corepresentable
by an object, denoted

{\mathbf{ind}}^{{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}_{{\mathcal{A}}_{1}}({\mathcal{S}}_{1},{\mathcal{C}}_{1})\in\operatorname{Cat}^{{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}({\operatorname{Spc}}).

The assignment

({\mathcal{S}}_{1},{\mathcal{C}}_{1})\mapsto{\mathbf{ind}}^{{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}_{{\mathcal{A}}_{1}}

is a functor

(A.4)

{\mathbf{ind}}^{{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}_{{\mathcal{A}}_{1}}:\operatorname{Cat}^{{\mathcal{A}}_{1}}({\operatorname{Spc}})\to\operatorname{Cat}^{{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}({\operatorname{Spc}}).

The object
{\mathbf{ind}}^{{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}_{{\mathcal{A}}_{1}}({\mathcal{S}}_{1},{\mathcal{C}}_{1})=:({\mathcal{S}}_{2},{\mathcal{C}}_{2})
can be described as follows:

{\mathcal{S}}_{2}={\mathcal{S}}_{1}=:{\mathcal{S}}
and for
s^{\prime},s^{\prime\prime}\in{\mathcal{S}}
,

\operatorname{Hom}^{{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}_{{\mathcal{S}},{\mathcal{C}}_{2}}(s^{\prime},s^{\prime\prime}):=F(\operatorname{Hom}^{{\mathcal{A}}_{1}}_{{\mathcal{S}},{\mathcal{C}}_{1}}(s^{\prime},s^{\prime\prime})).

The composition rule is induced by one on
{\mathcal{C}}_{1}
using the right-lax
monoidal structure on
F
.

A.1.6.

Take
{\mathcal{A}}={\operatorname{Spc}}
equipped with the Cartesian monoidal structure. We have a fully faithful functor

\mathsf{t}:\operatorname{Cat}\to\operatorname{Cat}^{\operatorname{Spc}}({\operatorname{Spc}})

that sends a category
{\mathcal{C}}
to the space
{\mathcal{S}}:={\mathcal{C}}^{\operatorname{grpd}}
and

\operatorname{Hom}^{\operatorname{Spc}}_{{\mathcal{S}},{\mathcal{C}}}(c_{1},c_{2}):=\operatorname{Maps}_{\mathcal{C}}(c_{1},c_{2}).

The functor
\mathsf{t}
admits a left adjoint, to be denoted
\mathsf{t}^{L}
. Thus, we can view

\operatorname{Cat}
as a localization of
\mathsf{t}^{L}
.

Remark A.1.7
.

One can give an explicit description of the arrows in
\operatorname{Cat}^{\operatorname{Spc}}({\operatorname{Spc}})
that get sent to isomorphisms
by
\mathsf{t}^{L}
:

Note that to an object
({\mathcal{S}},{\mathcal{C}})
one can naturally attach the
set

\pi_{0}({\mathcal{S}},{\mathcal{C}})
: the quotient of

\pi_{0}({\mathcal{S}})
by the equivalence relation generated by
\pi_{0}({\mathcal{C}})
.

A morphism
({\mathcal{S}}_{1},{\mathcal{C}}_{1})\to({\mathcal{S}}_{2},{\mathcal{C}}_{2})
becomes an isomorphism under
\mathsf{t}^{L}
if and only if:

•

It is Cartesian with respect to
\operatorname{Cat}^{\operatorname{Spc}}({\operatorname{Spc}})\to{\operatorname{Spc}}
;

•

The induced map
\pi_{0}({\mathcal{S}}_{1},{\mathcal{C}}_{1})\to\pi_{0}({\mathcal{S}}_{2},{\mathcal{C}}_{2})
is a surjection.

A.1.8.

Consider the (right-lax) monoidal functor

{\mathcal{A}}\to{\operatorname{Spc}},\quad a\mapsto\operatorname{Maps}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathbb{1}_{\mathcal{A}},a).

Applying the corresponding functor
{\mathbf{ind}}^{\operatorname{Spc}}_{\mathcal{A}}
, we obtain a functor

\operatorname{Cat}^{\mathcal{A}}({\operatorname{Spc}})\to\operatorname{Cat}^{\operatorname{Spc}}({\operatorname{Spc}}),

which we denote by
{\mathbf{oblv}}_{{\mathcal{A}}\operatorname{-Enr}}
(or simply
{\mathbf{oblv}}_{\operatorname{Enr}}
if the choice of
{\mathcal{A}}
is clear).

A.1.9.

A morphism in
\operatorname{Cat}^{\mathcal{A}}({\operatorname{Spc}})
is called an
enriched equivalence
if:

•

It is Cartesian with respect to (
A.2
);

•

Its image under

\operatorname{Cat}^{\mathcal{A}}({\operatorname{Spc}})\overset{{\mathbf{oblv}}_{{\mathcal{A}}\operatorname{-Enr}}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Cat}^{\operatorname{Spc}}({\operatorname{Spc}})\overset{\mathsf{t}^{L}}{\to}\operatorname{Cat}

is an isomorphism.

We define
\operatorname{Cat}^{\mathcal{A}}
to be the localization of
\operatorname{Cat}^{\mathcal{A}}({\operatorname{Spc}})
obtained by inverting
enriched equivalences.

Note that by definition

\operatorname{Cat}^{\operatorname{Spc}}\simeq\operatorname{Cat}.

A.1.10.

Let us be in the setting of Sect.
A.1.5
. One shows that the functor (
A.4
)
induces a functor on the corresponding localizations:

(A.5)

\operatorname{Cat}^{{\mathcal{A}}_{1}}\to\operatorname{Cat}^{{\mathcal{A}}_{2}},

which we denote by the same character
{\mathbf{ind}}^{{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}_{{\mathcal{A}}_{1}}
.

A.1.11.
The underlying category

In particular, applying this to the situation in Sect.
A.1.8
, we obtain a functor

\operatorname{Cat}^{\mathcal{A}}\to\operatorname{Cat}^{\operatorname{Spc}}\simeq\operatorname{Cat},

which we denote by
{\mathbf{oblv}}_{{\mathcal{A}}\operatorname{-Enr}}
(or simply
{\mathbf{oblv}}_{\operatorname{Enr}}
if the choice of
{\mathcal{A}}
is clear).

For
{\mathcal{C}}\in\operatorname{Cat}^{\mathcal{A}}
, we will refer to
{\mathbf{oblv}}_{{\mathcal{A}}\operatorname{-Enr}}({\mathcal{C}})
as the
underlying category
.

A.1.12.

Let
{\mathcal{C}}
be an object of
\operatorname{Cat}^{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{S}})
. Unwinding, we obtain that there is a naturally defined map

(A.6)

{\mathcal{S}}\to({\mathbf{oblv}}_{\operatorname{Enr}}({\mathcal{C}}))^{\operatorname{grpd}}.

Note, however, that this map is
not necessarily
an equivalence. For example, in the example of
Sect.
A.1.3
,

({\mathbf{oblv}}_{\operatorname{Enr}}({\mathcal{C}}))^{\operatorname{grpd}}=B(\operatorname{Maps}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathbb{1}_{\mathcal{A}},A)^{\times}),

where:

•

\operatorname{Maps}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathbb{1}_{\mathcal{A}},A)
is viewed as a monoid in
{\operatorname{Spc}}
;

•

(-)^{\times}
denotes the group of invertible points in a given monoid;

•

B(-)
denotes the classifying space of a given group.

Note also that the functor
{\mathbf{ind}}^{{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}_{{\mathcal{A}}_{1}}
changes the underlying groupoid of the underlying category,
which is one of the reasons to consider flaggings.

A.1.13.

That said, there is a canonical fully faithful embedding

(A.7)

\operatorname{Cat}^{\mathcal{A}}\to\operatorname{Cat}^{\mathcal{A}}({\operatorname{Spc}}),

whose essential image consists of those pairs
({\mathcal{S}},{\mathcal{C}})
for which the map (
A.6
)
is an isomorphism.

In other words, the functor (
A.7
) sends
{\mathcal{C}}\in\operatorname{Cat}^{\mathcal{A}}
to its canonical flagging by

({\mathbf{oblv}}_{\operatorname{Enr}}({\mathcal{C}}))^{\operatorname{grpd}}
.

Thus, in particular, we have a well-defined functor

\operatorname{Hom}^{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{C}}}:{\mathcal{C}}^{\operatorname{grpd}}\times{\mathcal{C}}^{\operatorname{grpd}}\to{\mathcal{A}},\quad(c_{1},c_{2})\mapsto\operatorname{Hom}^{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\mathcal{C}}(c_{1},c_{2})\in{\mathcal{A}}.

A.1.14.

The above procedure defines
\operatorname{Cat}^{\mathcal{A}}
as an
(\infty,1)
-category, in particular, for

{\mathcal{C}}_{1},{\mathcal{C}}_{2}\in\operatorname{Cat}^{\mathcal{A}}
we obtain the
space

\operatorname{Maps}_{\operatorname{Cat}^{\mathcal{A}}}({\mathcal{C}}_{1},{\mathcal{C}}_{2})

of
{\mathcal{A}}
-enriched functors
{\mathcal{C}}_{1}\to{\mathcal{C}}_{2}
.

Now, there is also a naturally defined notion of
natural transformation
between
{\mathcal{A}}
-enriched functors,
which upgrades
\operatorname{Maps}_{\operatorname{Cat}^{\mathcal{A}}}({\mathcal{C}}_{1},{\mathcal{C}}_{2})
to an
(\infty,1)
-category

\operatorname{Funct}^{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{C}}_{1},{\mathcal{C}}_{2}),\quad\operatorname{Maps}_{\operatorname{Cat}^{\mathcal{A}}}({\mathcal{C}}_{1},{\mathcal{C}}_{2})\simeq\left(\operatorname{Funct}^{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{C}}_{1},{\mathcal{C}}_{2})\right)^{\operatorname{grpd}}.

Furthermore, the assignment

{\mathcal{C}}_{1},{\mathcal{C}}_{2}\mapsto\operatorname{Funct}^{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{C}}_{1},{\mathcal{C}}_{2})

extends to a structure of
(\infty,2)
-category on
\operatorname{Cat}^{\mathcal{A}}
, in the sense of
[
GR1
, Chapter 10, Sect. 2]
.

In addition, for
F:{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\to{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
, the functor
{\mathbf{ind}}^{{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}_{{\mathcal{A}}_{1}}
extends to a functor of
(\infty,2)
-categories

\operatorname{Cat}^{{\mathcal{A}}_{1}}\to\operatorname{Cat}^{{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}.

A.2.
A set-theoretic digression

In this subsection, we explain the approach adopted in this paper for dealing
with set-theoretic issues.

A.2.1.

Given a cardinal
\mu
, we shall say that a category is of size
<\mu
if in Joyal’s model
of quasi-categories, it can be represented by a simplicial set of cardinality
<\mu
, see
[
Lu1
, Sect. 5.4.1]
.

We let
\operatorname{Cat}_{<\mu}
the category of categories of size
<\mu
.

A.2.2.

We fix a sequence of inaccessible cardinals

\lambda_{0}<\lambda_{1}<\lambda_{2}.

In particular, for
i=0,1
, categories generated under
\lambda_{i}
-small colimits by subcategories of size
<\lambda_{i}
are of
size
<\lambda_{i+1}
.

A.2.3.

By the definition of inaccessibility, we have:

•

The category
\operatorname{Sch}
of schemes of finite type over the ground field
k
is of size
<\lambda_{0}
;

•

For
X\in\operatorname{Sch}
, the category
\operatorname{Shv}(X)^{\operatorname{constr}}

(of
constructible

\ell
-adic sheaves on
X
) is of size
<\lambda_{0}
.

We will refer to categories of size
<\lambda_{0}
(resp.,
<\lambda_{1}
,
<\lambda_{2}
) as

small
(resp.,
large
,
huge
).

Note that the category
\operatorname{Cat}_{\operatorname{small}}
of small categories is itself large, while the
category
\operatorname{Cat}_{\operatorname{large}}
of large categories is huge.

A.2.4.

Recall, following
[
Lu1
, Sect. 5.5]
(see also
[
Lu2
, Sect. 5.3.2]
), that given a regular cardinal
\kappa<\lambda_{0}
, a category is said to be
\kappa
-presentable
if it contains small colimits, and is generated under small
\kappa
-filtered colimits by
a
small
subcategory of
\kappa
-compact objects.

Note that a
\kappa
-presentable category is automatically (not more than) large.

A.2.5.

We let
\operatorname{Cat}^{\operatorname{pres}}_{\kappa}
denote the category, whose objets are
\kappa
-presentable categories,
and whose morphisms are functors that commute with all colimits and preserve
\kappa
-compact
objects.

Note that
\operatorname{Cat}^{\operatorname{pres}}_{\kappa}
itself is
\kappa
-presentable; in particular,
it is (not more than) large.

We will regard
\operatorname{Cat}^{\operatorname{pres}}_{\kappa}
as a symmetric monoidal category with respect to the Lurie tensor product.

A.2.6.

Note that for
\kappa<\kappa^{\prime}
, the inclusion

\operatorname{Cat}^{\operatorname{pres}}_{\kappa}\to\operatorname{Cat}^{\operatorname{pres}}_{\kappa^{\prime}}

is symmetric monoidal and preserves all small colimits and limits of size
<\kappa
.

A.2.7.

We let
\operatorname{Cat}^{\operatorname{pres}}
denote the category, whose objects are
\kappa
-presentable categories
for some
\kappa<\lambda_{0}
, and whose morphisms are functors that preserve
\lambda_{0}
-small colimits.

We have

\operatorname{Cat}^{\operatorname{pres}}\simeq\underset{\kappa<\lambda_{0}}{\operatorname{colim}}\,\operatorname{Cat}^{\operatorname{pres}}_{\kappa},

where the colimit is taken in
\operatorname{Cat}_{\operatorname{large}}
.

We will regard
\operatorname{Cat}^{\operatorname{pres}}
as a symmetric monoidal category with respect to the Lurie tensor product.

A.2.8.

Repeating the contents of Sects.
A.2.4
-
A.2.7
, we obtain:

•

The notion of
\kappa
-presentable DG category;

•

The category
\operatorname{DGCat}_{\kappa}
of
\kappa
-presentable DG categories, equipped
with a symmetric monoidal structure;

•

The large category

\operatorname{DGCat}\simeq\underset{\kappa<\lambda_{0}}{\operatorname{colim}}\,\operatorname{DGCat}_{\kappa},

where the colimit is taken in
\operatorname{Cat}_{\operatorname{large}}
.

A.2.9.

The category
\operatorname{DGCat}
is the natural category of DG categories with which one works.

A.3.
Module categories–a recap

A.3.1.

For
{\mathcal{S}}\in\operatorname{Spc}
and
{\mathbf{C}}\in\operatorname{Cat}^{\operatorname{pres}}
, set

{\mathcal{S}}\otimes{\mathbf{C}}:=\underset{{\mathcal{S}}}{\operatorname{colim}}\,{\mathbf{C}}.

For a point
s\in S
, we have a natural insertion functor

\operatorname{ins}_{s}:{\mathbf{C}}\to{\mathcal{S}}\otimes{\mathbf{C}},

which admits a colimit-preserving right adjoint, to be denoted
\operatorname{ev}_{s}
.

The functors
\operatorname{ev}_{s}
assemble to a colimit-preserving functor

(A.8)

{\mathcal{S}}\otimes{\mathbf{C}}\to\underset{{\mathcal{S}}^{\operatorname{op}}}{\operatorname{lim}}\,{\mathbf{C}}\simeq\underset{{\mathcal{S}}}{\operatorname{lim}}\,{\mathbf{C}}\simeq\operatorname{Funct}({\mathcal{S}},{\mathbf{C}}).

The following is a particular case of
[
GR1
, Chapter 1, Proposition 2.5.7]
:

Lemma A.3.2
.

The functor (
A.8
) is an equivalence.

Corollary A.3.3
.

For an object
\Phi\in{\mathcal{S}}\otimes{\mathbf{C}}
, we have a canonical isomorphism

\Phi\simeq\underset{s\in{\mathcal{S}}}{\operatorname{colim}}\,\operatorname{ins}_{s}\circ\operatorname{ev}_{s}(\Phi).

A.3.4.

Let
{\mathcal{A}}
be an algebra object in
\operatorname{Cat}^{\operatorname{pres}}

(see Sect.
A.2.7
). We let
{\mathcal{A}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}
denote the category of
{\mathcal{A}}
-modules in
\operatorname{Cat}^{\operatorname{pres}}
.

Given
{\mathcal{M}},{\mathcal{N}}\in{\mathcal{A}}\mathbf{\mbox{-}mod}
, we denote by

\operatorname{Funct}_{{\mathcal{A}}}({\mathcal{M}},{\mathcal{N}})

the category of
{\mathcal{A}}
-linear functors.

A.3.5.

Applying Sect.
A.3.1
to
{\mathbf{C}}={\mathcal{A}}
, we obtain the object

{\mathcal{S}}\otimes{\mathcal{A}}\in{\mathcal{A}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}},

and an isomorphism

(A.9)

{\mathcal{S}}\otimes{\mathcal{A}}\simeq\operatorname{Funct}({\mathcal{S}},{\mathcal{A}})

in
{\mathcal{A}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}
.

A.3.6.

For
s\in{\mathcal{S}}
denote
\delta_{s}\in{\mathcal{S}}\otimes{\mathcal{A}}\simeq\operatorname{Funct}(S,{\mathcal{A}})
the object

\operatorname{ins}_{s}(\mathbb{1}_{\mathcal{A}}).

From Corollary
A.3.3
, we obtain a canonical isomorphism

(A.10)

\Phi\simeq\underset{s\in S}{\operatorname{colim}}\,\Phi(s)\otimes\delta_{s},\quad\Phi\in\operatorname{Funct}(S,{\mathcal{A}}),

where
(-)\otimes(-)
stands for the action of
{\mathcal{A}}
on
\operatorname{Funct}(S,{\mathcal{A}})
.

A.3.7.

Recall that given an object
{\mathcal{M}}\in{\mathcal{A}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}
, its dual (if it exists) is an object
{\mathcal{M}}^{\vee}\in{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{rev}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}
such that

\operatorname{Funct}_{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{M}},{\mathcal{N}})\simeq{\mathcal{M}}^{\vee}\underset{{\mathcal{A}}}{\otimes}{\mathcal{N}},\quad{\mathcal{N}}\in{\mathcal{A}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}.

In this case we shall say that
{\mathcal{M}}
is dualizable
10
10

10

Here and elsewhere, for an algebra
A
in a symmetric monoidal category,

A^{\operatorname{rev}}
denotes the algebra obtained by reversing the multiplication.
.

We claim that the object
{\mathcal{S}}\otimes{\mathcal{A}}\in{\mathcal{A}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}
is dualizable with dual
{\mathcal{S}}\otimes{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{rev}}
.
Indeed, for any

{\mathcal{N}}\in{\mathcal{A}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}
we have

\operatorname{Funct}_{{\mathcal{A}}}({\mathcal{S}}\otimes{\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{N}})=\operatorname{Funct}_{{\mathcal{A}}}(\underset{{\mathcal{S}}}{\operatorname{colim}}\,{\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{N}})\simeq\underset{{\mathcal{S}}}{\operatorname{lim}}\,\operatorname{Funct}_{{\mathcal{A}}}({\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{N}})\simeq\\
\simeq\underset{{\mathcal{S}}}{\operatorname{lim}}\,{\mathcal{N}}\overset{\text{Lemma \ref{l:limit and colimit}}}{\simeq}\underset{{\mathcal{S}}}{\operatorname{colim}}\,{\mathcal{N}}\simeq\underset{{\mathcal{S}}}{\operatorname{colim}}\,({\mathcal{A}}\underset{{\mathcal{A}}}{\otimes}{\mathcal{N}})\simeq(\underset{{\mathcal{S}}}{\operatorname{colim}}\,{\mathcal{A}})\underset{{\mathcal{A}}}{\otimes}{\mathcal{N}}=({\mathcal{S}}\otimes{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{rev}})\underset{{\mathcal{A}}}{\otimes}{\mathcal{N}},

as required.

In particular, we obtain that for
{\mathcal{S}}_{1},{\mathcal{S}}_{2}
we have a canonical equivalence of categories.

(A.11)

\operatorname{Funct}_{{\mathcal{A}}}({\mathcal{S}}_{1}\otimes{\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{S}}_{2}\otimes{\mathcal{A}})\simeq\operatorname{Funct}_{{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{rev}}}({\mathcal{S}}_{2}\otimes{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{rev}},{\mathcal{S}}_{1}\otimes{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{rev}}).

Remark A.3.8
.

Note, however, that when
{\mathcal{S}}_{1}={\mathcal{S}}_{2}={\mathcal{S}}
, both sides of (
A.11
) have natural
monoidal structures, while (
A.11
) reverses them.

A.4.
Enriched vs tensored

A.4.1.

In this subsection we let
{\mathcal{A}}
be as in Sect.
A.3.4
. We will distinguish two classes
of
{\mathcal{A}}
-enriched categories.

We will keep the notation
\operatorname{Cat}^{\mathcal{A}}
for the category of
{\mathcal{A}}
-enriched categories that can be flagged
by a
small
space. We will denote by
\operatorname{Cat}_{\operatorname{large}}^{\mathcal{A}}
the category of
{\mathcal{A}}
-enriched categories
that can be flagged by a large space.

We have the obvious inclusion

\operatorname{Cat}^{\mathcal{A}}\subset\operatorname{Cat}_{\operatorname{large}}^{\mathcal{A}}.

A.4.2.

Since
{\mathcal{A}}
was assumed presentable, for
{\mathcal{M}}\in{\mathcal{A}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}
and a pair of objects
m_{1},m_{2}\in{\mathcal{M}}
, we have a
well-defined
internal-relative to

{\mathcal{A}}
Hom

\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{{\mathcal{M}},{\mathcal{A}}}(m_{1},m_{2})\in{\mathcal{A}},\quad\operatorname{Maps}_{\mathcal{A}}(v,\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{{\mathcal{M}},{\mathcal{A}}}(m_{1},m_{2})):=\operatorname{Maps}_{\mathcal{M}}(v\otimes m_{1},m_{2}).

This allows us to view
{\mathcal{M}}
as
enriched
over
{\mathcal{A}}
:

\operatorname{Hom}^{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{M}}(m_{1},m_{2}):=\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{{\mathcal{M}},{\mathcal{A}}}(m_{1},m_{2}),

see
[
GeHa
, Corollary 7.4.9]
.

This way we obtain a functor

(A.12)

\operatorname{Mod-to-Enr}:{\mathcal{A}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}\to\operatorname{Cat}^{\mathcal{A}}_{\operatorname{large}}.

A.4.3.

In particular, for
{\mathcal{C}}\in\operatorname{Cat}^{\mathcal{A}}_{\operatorname{large}}
and
{\mathcal{M}}\in{\mathcal{A}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}
,
it makes sense to talk about the category

\operatorname{Funct}^{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{M}}):=\operatorname{Funct}^{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{C}},\operatorname{Mod-to-Enr}({\mathcal{M}}))

of
{\mathcal{A}}
-functors.

By definition, the datum of an object
\Phi\in\operatorname{Funct}^{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{M}})
consist of a functor

\Phi:{\mathbf{oblv}}_{{\mathcal{A}}\operatorname{-Enr}}({\mathcal{C}})\to{\mathcal{M}},

equipped with a compatible family of morphisms

\operatorname{Hom}^{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\mathcal{C}}(c_{1},c_{2})\otimes\Phi(c_{1})\to\Phi(c_{2}),

where
(-)\otimes(-)
refers to the action of
{\mathcal{A}}
on
{\mathcal{M}}
.

Remark A.4.4
.

In Sect.
A.8
we will explain a different point of view on
\operatorname{Funct}^{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{M}})
,
developed in
[
Hi1
,
Hi2
]
.

A.5.
Enriched presheaves

A.5.1.

We now claim that the functor
\operatorname{Mod-to-Enr}
admits a (partially defined) left adjoint, defined on
\operatorname{Cat}^{\mathcal{A}}\subset\operatorname{Cat}^{\mathcal{A}}_{\operatorname{large}}
,
to be denoted

(A.13)

{\mathcal{C}}\mapsto\mathbf{P}^{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{C}}).

Explicitly,

\mathbf{P}^{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{C}})=\operatorname{Funct}^{{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{rev}}}({\mathcal{C}}^{\operatorname{op}},{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{rev}}),

where:

•

{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{rev}}
denotes the monoidal category obtained from
{\mathcal{A}}
by reversing the monoidal operation;

•

{\mathcal{C}}^{\operatorname{op}}
is regarded as enriched over
{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{rev}}
;

•

\operatorname{Funct}^{{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{rev}}}({\mathcal{C}}^{\operatorname{op}},{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{rev}})
is regarded as an
{\mathcal{A}}
-module category,
via the
{\mathcal{A}}
-action on
{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{rev}}
“on the right” (which commutes with the natural left action of

{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{rev}}
on itself).

Remark A.5.2
.

The assumption that
{\mathcal{C}}\in\operatorname{Cat}^{\mathcal{A}}
is needed in order to make
\mathbf{P}^{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{C}})

a large (as opposed to huge) category.

A.5.3.

Concretely, an object of
\mathbf{P}^{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{C}})
is a functor

\Phi:{\mathcal{C}}^{\operatorname{op}}\to{\mathcal{A}},

equipped with a compatible data of maps

\Phi(c_{2})\otimes\operatorname{Hom}^{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\mathcal{C}}(c_{1},c_{2})\to\Phi(c_{1}),\quad c_{1},c_{2}\in{\mathcal{C}}.

A.5.4.

The unit of the
(\mathbf{P}^{\mathcal{A}}(-),\operatorname{Mod-to-Enr})
-adjunction is the
{\mathcal{A}}
-functor

(A.14)

{\mathcal{C}}\to\operatorname{Funct}^{{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{rev}}}({\mathcal{C}}^{\operatorname{op}},{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{rev}}),

that sends

c\mapsto\operatorname{Hom}^{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\mathcal{C}}(-,c).

We will refer to (
A.14
) as the
{\mathcal{A}}
-enriched Yoneda functor
, and denote it by

\operatorname{Yon}^{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{C}}\in\operatorname{Funct}^{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{C}},\mathbf{P}^{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{C}})).

A.5.5.

In Sect.
A.8
we will give a slightly different description of the category
\mathbf{P}^{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{C}})
, from
which we will deduce (see Sect.
A.8.8
):

Proposition A.5.6
.

For
{\mathcal{C}}\in\operatorname{Cat}^{\mathcal{A}}
and
{\mathcal{M}}\in{\mathcal{A}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}
, precomposition with
\operatorname{Yon}^{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{C}}
gives rise to an equivalence

\operatorname{Funct}_{{\mathcal{A}}}(\mathbf{P}^{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{C}}),{\mathcal{M}})\to\operatorname{Funct}^{{\mathcal{A}}}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{M}}).

Note that the assertion of Proposition
A.5.6

is equivalent to the statement that
\operatorname{Yon}^{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{C}}
indeed makes
\mathbf{P}^{\mathcal{A}}(-)
a left adjoint
of
\operatorname{Mod-to-Enr}
on
\operatorname{Cat}^{\mathcal{A}}
.

A.5.7.

Let
{\mathcal{A}}\to{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime}
be a monoidal functor. From Proposition
A.5.6
we obtain a
canonical identification:

\mathbf{P}^{{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime}}({\mathbf{ind}}_{{\mathcal{A}}}^{{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime}}({\mathcal{C}}))\simeq{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime}\underset{{\mathcal{A}}}{\otimes}\mathbf{P}^{{\mathcal{A}}}({\mathcal{C}}).

A.5.8.

Recall that a functor
\Phi:{\mathcal{M}}\to{\mathcal{N}}
between
\infty
-categories is said to be
1-full
if for every

m_{1},m_{2}\in{\mathcal{C}}
, the map

(A.15)

\operatorname{Maps}_{\mathcal{M}}(m_{1},m_{2})\to\operatorname{Maps}_{{\mathcal{N}}}(\Phi(m_{1}),\Phi(m_{2}))

is fully faithful (i.e., is an embedding of a union of connected components).

From Proposition
A.5.6
we deduce the following fact, which we will use later on:

Proposition A.5.9
.

Let
{\mathcal{C}}
be a
{\mathcal{A}}
-enriched category, and let
{\mathcal{M}}\to{\mathcal{N}}
be a 1-full functor of
{\mathcal{A}}
-module categories. Then:

(a) The induced functor

\operatorname{Funct}^{{\mathcal{A}}}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{M}})\to\operatorname{Funct}^{{\mathcal{A}}}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{N}})

is 1-full and the essential image consists of objects
\Phi\in\operatorname{Funct}^{{\mathcal{A}}}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{N}})
such that:

(i)
\Phi(x)\in{\mathcal{M}}
for all
x\in{\mathcal{C}}
;

(ii) For every
c_{1},c_{2}\in{\mathcal{C}}
, the morphism

\operatorname{Hom}^{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\mathcal{C}}(c_{1},c_{2})\otimes\Phi(c_{1})\to\Phi(c_{2})

given by (
A.15
) lies in
{\mathcal{M}}
.

(b) A map
\Phi^{\prime}\to\Phi^{\prime\prime}
between two such functors lies in
\operatorname{Funct}^{{\mathcal{A}}}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{M}})
iff
\Phi^{\prime}(c)\to\Phi^{\prime\prime}(c)

lies in
{\mathcal{M}}
for all
c\in{\mathcal{C}}
.

A.6.
The enriched Yoneda lemma

Given
{\mathcal{C}}\in\operatorname{Cat}^{\mathcal{A}}
, we continue to study the
{\mathcal{A}}
-functor

\operatorname{Yon}^{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{C}}:{\mathcal{C}}\to\mathbf{P}^{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{C}}).

A.6.1.

Note that for any
c,c^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}}
, we have a tautoloigical identification

(A.16)

\operatorname{Hom}^{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\mathcal{C}}(c^{\prime},c)\simeq\operatorname{Yon}^{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{C}}(c)(c^{\prime}),

where:

•

\operatorname{Yon}^{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{C}}(c)\in\mathbf{P}^{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{C}})
is viewed as an
{\mathcal{A}}
-functor
{\mathcal{C}}^{\operatorname{op}}\to{\mathcal{A}}
;

•

\operatorname{Yon}^{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{C}}(c)(c^{\prime})
denotes the value of the above functor on
c^{\prime}
.

In particular, we obtain a canonical map

(A.17)

\mathbb{1}_{\mathcal{A}}\to\operatorname{Yon}^{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{C}}(c)(c),

A.6.2.

Note also that for any
c\in{\mathcal{C}}
, evaluation on
c
gives rise to an
{\mathcal{A}}
-linear functor

\mathbf{P}^{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{C}}):=\operatorname{Funct}^{{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{rev}}}({\mathcal{C}}^{\operatorname{op}},{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{rev}})\to{\mathcal{A}},

to be denoted
\operatorname{ev}_{c}
.

As with the usual Yoneda embedding, the functor
\operatorname{Yon}^{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{C}}
has the following property:

Lemma A.6.3
.

For
c\in{\mathcal{C}}
and
\Phi\in\mathbf{P}^{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{C}}):=\operatorname{Funct}^{{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{rev}}}({\mathcal{C}}^{\operatorname{op}},{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{rev}})
, the map

\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathbf{P}^{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{C}}),{\mathcal{A}}}(\operatorname{Yon}^{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{C}}(c),\Phi)\overset{\operatorname{ev}_{c}}{\longrightarrow}\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathcal{A}}(\operatorname{Yon}^{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{C}}(c)(c),\Phi(c))\overset{\text{\eqref{e:precomposition with Yon id}}}{\longrightarrow}\Phi(c)

is an isomorphism.

The proof is the same as for the usual Yoneda lemma: one constructs explicitly the inverse map.

A.6.4.

As a particular case of Lemma
A.6.3
, we obtain:

Corollary A.6.5
.

For
c,c^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}}
, the map

\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathbf{P}^{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{C}}),{\mathcal{A}}}(\operatorname{Yon}^{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{C}}(c),\operatorname{Yon}^{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{C}}(c^{\prime}))\overset{\operatorname{ev}_{c}}{\longrightarrow}\\
\to\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathcal{A}}(\operatorname{Yon}^{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{C}}(c)(c),\operatorname{Yon}^{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{C}}(c^{\prime})(c))\overset{\text{\eqref{e:precomposition with Yon id}}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Yon}^{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{C}}(c^{\prime})(c)\simeq\operatorname{Hom}^{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\mathcal{C}}(c,c^{\prime})

is an isomorphism.

A.7.
More on the
(\mathbf{P}^{\mathcal{A}}(-),\operatorname{Mod-to-Enr})
-adjunction

This subsection is optional in that its contents are not used elsewhere in the paper.

A.7.1.

Fix a regular cardinal
\kappa<\lambda_{0}
, set

{\mathcal{A}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}_{\kappa}:={\mathcal{A}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}\underset{\operatorname{Cat}^{\operatorname{pres}}}{\times}\operatorname{Cat}^{\operatorname{pres}}_{\kappa}.

We define the functor

\operatorname{Mod-to-Enr}_{\kappa}:{\mathcal{A}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}_{\kappa}\to\operatorname{Cat}^{\mathcal{A}}

by sending
{\mathcal{M}}\in{\mathcal{A}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}_{\kappa}
to the (automatically
small
) category of
\kappa
-compact
objects
{\mathcal{M}}^{c}_{\kappa}\subset{\mathcal{M}}
, equipped with the
{\mathcal{A}}
-enrichment inherited from that on
\operatorname{Mod-to-Enr}({\mathcal{M}})
.

A.7.2.

Note that the functor
\mathbf{P}^{\mathcal{A}}(-)
sends
\operatorname{Cat}^{\mathcal{A}}
to
{\mathcal{A}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}_{\kappa}
for any
\kappa
such that

{\mathcal{A}}\in\operatorname{Cat}^{\operatorname{pres}}_{\kappa}
.

We claim that we have an adjunction

\mathbf{P}^{\mathcal{A}}(-):\operatorname{Cat}^{\mathcal{A}}\rightleftarrows{\mathcal{A}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}_{\kappa}:\operatorname{Mod-to-Enr}_{\kappa}.

A.7.3.

The unit of the adjunction is given by the enriched Yoneda functor

(A.18)

{\mathcal{C}}\to\mathbf{P}^{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{C}}),

whose image is easily seen to lie in the subcategory of
\kappa
-compact objects.

Let us describe the counit of the adjunction, which is by definition a map

\mathbf{P}^{\mathcal{A}}(\operatorname{Mod-to-Enr}({\mathcal{M}})_{\kappa})\to{\mathcal{M}}.

A.7.4.

For a given
{\mathcal{M}}
, we start with the enriched Yoneda functor

\operatorname{Yon}^{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{M}}^{c}_{\kappa}}:{\mathcal{M}}_{\kappa}^{c}\to\mathbf{P}^{\mathcal{A}}(\operatorname{Mod-to-Enr}({\mathcal{M}})_{\kappa}),

and we let

{}^{\prime}\!\operatorname{Yon}^{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{M}}}:{\mathcal{M}}\to\mathbf{P}^{\mathcal{A}}(\operatorname{Mod-to-Enr}({\mathcal{M}})_{\kappa})

be its extension to a colimit-preserving functor.

In Sect.
A.9.6
we will show that the functor
{}^{\prime}\operatorname{Yon}^{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{M}}
admits a
left
adjoint:

({}^{\prime}\!\operatorname{Yon}^{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{M}})^{L}:\mathbf{P}^{\mathcal{A}}(\operatorname{Mod-to-Enr}({\mathcal{M}})_{\kappa})\to{\mathcal{M}}.

The functor
{}^{\prime}\!\operatorname{Yon}^{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{M}}
is naturally right-lax compatible with the action of
{\mathcal{A}}
. Hence,
the functor
({}^{\prime}\!\operatorname{Yon}^{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{M}})^{L}
acquires a structure of left-lax compatibility with the action of
{\mathcal{A}}
.
However, in Sect.
A.9.6
, we will see that this left-lax compatibility is actually
strict, i.e.,
({}^{\prime}\!\operatorname{Yon}^{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{M}})^{L}
is a morphism in
{\mathcal{A}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}_{\kappa}
.

A.7.5.

We claim the above natural transformations indeed provide a unit and counit of the adjunction. Moreover,
for
{\mathcal{C}}\in\operatorname{Cat}^{\mathcal{A}}
and
{\mathcal{M}}\in{\mathcal{A}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}_{\kappa}
, the resulting isomorphism

\operatorname{Funct}^{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{C}},\operatorname{Mod-to-Enr}({\mathcal{M}})_{\kappa})\simeq\operatorname{Funct}_{{\mathcal{A}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}_{\kappa}}(\mathbf{P}^{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{C}}),{\mathcal{M}})

fits into the commutative diagram

(A.19)

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Funct}_{{\mathcal{A}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}_{\kappa}}(\mathbf{P}^{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{C}}),{\mathcal{M}})@>{\sim}>{}>\operatorname{Funct}^{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{C}},\operatorname{Mod-to-Enr}({\mathcal{M}})_{\kappa})\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
\operatorname{Funct}_{{\mathcal{A}}}(\mathbf{P}^{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{C}}),{\mathcal{M}})@>{\text{Proposition~\ref{p:env enh}}}>{\sim}>\operatorname{Funct}^{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{C}},\operatorname{Mod-to-Enr}({\mathcal{M}})).\end{CD}

A.7.6.

First, we claim that the composition

\operatorname{Funct}_{{\mathcal{A}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}_{\kappa}}(\mathbf{P}^{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{C}}),{\mathcal{M}})\overset{(-)\circ\text{\eqref{e:unit kappa}}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Funct}^{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{C}},\operatorname{Mod-to-Enr}({\mathcal{M}})_{\kappa})\hookrightarrow\\
\to\operatorname{Funct}^{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{C}},\operatorname{Mod-to-Enr}({\mathcal{M}}))

equals

\operatorname{Funct}_{{\mathcal{A}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}_{\kappa}}(\mathbf{P}^{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{C}}),{\mathcal{M}})\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Funct}_{{\mathcal{A}}}(\mathbf{P}^{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{C}}),{\mathcal{M}})\overset{\text{Proposition~\ref{p:env enh}}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Funct}^{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{C}},\operatorname{Mod-to-Enr}({\mathcal{M}})).

To check this, it is sufficient to consider the universal case, i.e., when we take
{\mathcal{M}}:=\mathbf{P}^{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{C}})
, and we start
with the identity functor. However, in this case, the assertion becomes tautological.

A.7.7.

It remains to show that for
{\mathcal{C}}\in\operatorname{Cat}^{\mathcal{A}}
and an
{\mathcal{A}}
-functor
{\mathcal{C}}\to{\mathcal{M}}^{c}_{\kappa}
, i.e.,
an
{\mathcal{A}}
-enriched functor
\Phi:{\mathcal{C}}\to\operatorname{Mod-to-Enr}({\mathcal{M}})_{\kappa}
, the composition

\mathbf{P}^{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{C}})\overset{\mathbf{P}^{\mathcal{A}}(\Phi)}{\to}\mathbf{P}^{\mathcal{A}}(\operatorname{Mod-to-Enr}({\mathcal{M}})_{\kappa})\overset{({}^{\prime}\!\operatorname{Yon}^{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{M}})^{L}}{\to}{\mathcal{M}}

corresponds under the isomorphism of Proposition
A.5.6
to the initial functor
\Phi
, followed by the embedding
{\mathcal{M}}^{c}_{\kappa}\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{M}}
.

I.e., we have to show that the composite

{\mathcal{C}}\overset{\operatorname{Yon}^{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{C}}}{\to}\mathbf{P}^{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{C}})\overset{\mathbf{P}^{\mathcal{A}}(\Phi)}{\to}\mathbf{P}^{\mathcal{A}}(\operatorname{Mod-to-Enr}({\mathcal{M}})_{\kappa})\overset{({}^{\prime}\!\operatorname{Yon}^{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{M}})^{L}}{\to}{\mathcal{M}},

viewed as an
{\mathcal{A}}
-functor, is isomorphic to
\Phi
, followed by the embedding
{\mathcal{M}}^{c}_{\kappa}\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{M}}
.

By the functoriality of
\operatorname{Yon}^{\mathcal{A}}_{-}
, the above composition identifies with

{\mathcal{C}}\overset{\Phi}{\to}\operatorname{Mod-to-Enr}({\mathcal{M}})_{\kappa}\overset{\operatorname{Yon}^{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{M}}^{c}_{\kappa}}}{\to}\mathbf{P}^{\mathcal{A}}(\operatorname{Mod-to-Enr}({\mathcal{M}})_{\kappa})\overset{({}^{\prime}\!\operatorname{Yon}^{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{M}})^{L}}{\to}{\mathcal{M}}.

Thus, it suffices to show that the composition

{\mathcal{M}}^{c}_{\kappa}=\operatorname{Mod-to-Enr}({\mathcal{M}})_{\kappa}\overset{\operatorname{Yon}^{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{M}}^{c}_{\kappa}}}{\to}\mathbf{P}^{\mathcal{A}}(\operatorname{Mod-to-Enr}({\mathcal{M}})_{\kappa})\overset{({}^{\prime}\!\operatorname{Yon}^{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{M}})^{L}}{\to}{\mathcal{M}},

viewed as an
{\mathcal{A}}
-functor, is isomorphic to the embedding
{\mathcal{M}}^{c}_{\kappa}\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{M}}
.

However, this follows from the fully faithfulness of
\operatorname{Yon}^{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{M}}
, see Corollary
A.6.5
.

A.8.
Enriched categories via quivers

In this subsection we will outline an approach to enriched categories developed in
[
Hi1
,
Hi2
]
.
It gives a complementary point of view on many notions discussed earlier in this section.

A comparison between the two approaches can be found in
[
He
]
.

A.8.1.

Let
{\mathcal{A}}
be an associative algebra in
\operatorname{Cat}^{\operatorname{pres}}
. Let
{\mathcal{S}}
be an object of

{\operatorname{Spc}}
. Consider the object

{\mathcal{S}}\otimes{\mathcal{A}}\in{\mathcal{A}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}

and the monoidal category

\operatorname{Quiv}_{\mathcal{S}}({\mathcal{A}}):=\operatorname{Funct}_{{\mathcal{A}}}({\mathcal{S}}\otimes{\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{S}}\otimes{\mathcal{A}})^{\operatorname{rev}}.

By Sect.
A.3.7
, we can identify
\operatorname{Quiv}_{\mathcal{S}}({\mathcal{A}})
as a plain category with

\operatorname{Funct}({\mathcal{S}}\times{\mathcal{S}},{\mathcal{A}}).

In terms of this description, the monoidal structure on
\operatorname{Quiv}_{\mathcal{S}}({\mathcal{A}})
corresponds to the

convolution
monoidal structure on
\operatorname{Quiv}_{\mathcal{S}}({\mathcal{A}})
, i.e., the monoidal operation is
pull-push along

({\mathcal{S}}\times{\mathcal{S}})\times({\mathcal{S}}\times{\mathcal{S}})\leftarrow{\mathcal{S}}\times{\mathcal{S}}\times{\mathcal{S}}\to{\mathcal{S}}\times{\mathcal{S}},

where “push” means left Kan extension.

We will regard
{\mathcal{S}}\otimes{\mathcal{A}}
as a
right
module over
\operatorname{Quiv}_{\mathcal{S}}({\mathcal{A}})
. This (right) action
of
\operatorname{Quiv}_{\mathcal{S}}({\mathcal{A}})
on
{\mathcal{S}}\otimes{\mathcal{A}}
commutes with the natural (left) action
of
{\mathcal{A}}
on
{\mathcal{S}}\otimes{\mathcal{A}}
.

A.8.2.

Note that there is a canonical isomorphism

(\operatorname{Quiv}_{\mathcal{S}}({\mathcal{A}}))^{\operatorname{rev}}\overset{\tau}{\simeq}\operatorname{Quiv}_{\mathcal{S}}({\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{rev}}),

namely

\operatorname{Funct}_{{\mathcal{A}}}({\mathcal{S}}\otimes{\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{S}}\otimes{\mathcal{A}})^{\operatorname{rev}}\simeq\operatorname{Funct}_{{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{rev}}}(({\mathcal{S}}\otimes{\mathcal{A}})^{\vee},({\mathcal{S}}\otimes{\mathcal{A}})^{\vee})\overset{\text{\eqref{e:duality funct}}}{\simeq}\\
\simeq\operatorname{Funct}_{{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{rev}}}({\mathcal{S}}\otimes{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{rev}},{\mathcal{S}}\otimes{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{rev}}).

In terms of the identifications

\operatorname{Quiv}_{\mathcal{S}}({\mathcal{A}})\simeq(\operatorname{Funct}({\mathcal{S}}\times{\mathcal{S}},{\mathcal{A}}))^{\operatorname{rev}}\text{ and }\operatorname{Quiv}_{\mathcal{S}}({\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{rev}})\simeq\operatorname{Funct}({\mathcal{S}}\times{\mathcal{S}},{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{rev}})^{\operatorname{rev}},

it corresponds to the swap of the factors in
{\mathcal{S}}\times{\mathcal{S}}
, followed by the identity functor

\operatorname{Funct}({\mathcal{S}}\times{\mathcal{S}},{\mathcal{A}})\simeq\operatorname{Funct}({\mathcal{S}}\times{\mathcal{S}},{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{rev}}).

For an algebra object
R\in\operatorname{Quiv}_{\mathcal{S}}({\mathcal{A}})
, we let
\tau(R^{\operatorname{rev}})
denote the corresponding algebra
object in
\operatorname{Quiv}_{\mathcal{S}}({\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{rev}})
.

A.8.3.

Let
{\mathcal{C}}
be an
{\mathcal{S}}
-flagged category enriched over
{\mathcal{A}}
.

To this data we associate an algebra object

R_{\mathcal{C}}\in\operatorname{Quiv}_{\mathcal{S}}({\mathcal{A}})

by letting the value of the corresponding functor
{\mathcal{S}}\times{\mathcal{S}}\to{\mathcal{A}}

on
(s_{1},s_{2})\in{\mathcal{S}}\times{\mathcal{S}}
be

\operatorname{Hom}^{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{S}},{\mathcal{C}}}(s_{1},s_{2})\in{\mathcal{A}}.

A.8.4.

Note that for the opposite category

{\mathcal{C}}^{\operatorname{op}}\in\operatorname{Cat}^{{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{rev}}},

we have

R_{{\mathcal{C}}^{\operatorname{op}}}\simeq\tau(R_{\mathcal{C}}^{\operatorname{rev}})\in\operatorname{Alg}(\operatorname{Quiv}_{\mathcal{S}}({\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{rev}})).

A.8.5.

Let
{\mathcal{M}}
be an
{\mathcal{A}}
-module category. Unwinding the definitions, we obtain that the category

\operatorname{Funct}^{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{M}})
identifies with

(A.20)

R_{\mathcal{C}}\operatorname{-mod}(\operatorname{Funct}_{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{S}}\otimes{\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{M}})),

where we consider
\operatorname{Funct}_{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{S}}\otimes{\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{M}})
as acted on the left by

\operatorname{Quiv}_{\mathcal{S}}({\mathcal{A}})
via its right action on the source.

A.8.6.

We obtain that

\mathbf{P}^{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{C}})=\tau(R_{\mathcal{C}}^{\operatorname{rev}})\operatorname{-mod}(\operatorname{Funct}_{{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{rev}}}({\mathcal{S}}\otimes{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{rev}},{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{rev}})).

By (
A.11
), we identify

\operatorname{Funct}_{{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{rev}}}({\mathcal{S}}\otimes{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{rev}},{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{rev}})\simeq{\mathcal{S}}\otimes{\mathcal{A}}.

Under this identification, the left action of
\operatorname{Quiv}_{\mathcal{S}}({\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{rev}})
on

\operatorname{Funct}_{{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{rev}}}({\mathcal{S}}\otimes{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{rev}},{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{rev}})
corresponds to the
right action of
\operatorname{Quiv}_{\mathcal{S}}({\mathcal{A}})
on
{\mathcal{S}}\otimes{\mathcal{A}}
via
\tau
.

In particular, we obtain an identification

(A.21)

\mathbf{P}^{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{C}})\simeq R_{\mathcal{C}}^{\operatorname{rev}}\operatorname{-mod}({\mathcal{S}}\otimes{\mathcal{A}}).

A.8.7.

Unwinding, we obtain that in terms of the identification (
A.21
), the Yoneda functor

\operatorname{Yon}^{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{C}}:{\mathcal{C}}\to\mathbf{P}^{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{C}})

corresponds to the tautological object in

R_{\mathcal{C}}\operatorname{-mod}(\operatorname{Funct}({\mathcal{S}}\otimes{\mathcal{A}},R_{\mathcal{C}}^{\operatorname{rev}}\operatorname{-mod}({\mathcal{S}}\otimes{\mathcal{A}})),

whose underlying object of
\operatorname{Funct}({\mathcal{S}}\otimes{\mathcal{A}},R_{\mathcal{C}}^{\operatorname{rev}}\operatorname{-mod}({\mathcal{S}}\otimes{\mathcal{A}})

is the induction functor

{\mathbf{ind}}_{R_{\mathcal{C}}^{\operatorname{rev}}}:{\mathcal{S}}\otimes{\mathcal{A}}\to R_{\mathcal{C}}^{\operatorname{rev}}\operatorname{-mod}({\mathcal{S}}\otimes{\mathcal{A}}).

A.8.8.

The latter observation makes the assertion of Proposition
A.5.6
manifest: for any
{\mathcal{M}}\in{\mathcal{A}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}
, the map

\operatorname{Funct}_{{\mathcal{A}}}(R_{\mathcal{C}}^{\operatorname{rev}}\operatorname{-mod}({\mathcal{S}}\otimes{\mathcal{A}}),{\mathcal{M}})\to R_{\mathcal{C}}\operatorname{-mod}(\operatorname{Funct}_{{\mathcal{A}}}({\mathcal{S}}\otimes{\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{M}}))

given by precomposition with
{\mathbf{ind}}_{R_{\mathcal{C}}^{\operatorname{rev}}}
is an isomorphism: indeed, both sides are monadic
over
\operatorname{Funct}_{{\mathcal{A}}}({\mathcal{S}}\otimes{\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{M}})
, and the above functor induces an isomorphism of monads.

A.8.9.

Note that the interpretation of
\operatorname{Funct}^{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{M}})
given by (
A.20
) implies that the canonical
functor

(A.22)

\operatorname{Funct}^{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{A}})\underset{{\mathcal{A}}}{\otimes}{\mathcal{M}}\to\operatorname{Funct}^{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{M}})

is an equivalence.

Indeed, the above functor identifies with

R_{\mathcal{C}}\operatorname{-mod}(\operatorname{Funct}_{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{S}}\otimes{\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{A}}))\underset{{\mathcal{A}}}{\otimes}{\mathcal{M}}\to R_{\mathcal{C}}\operatorname{-mod}(\operatorname{Funct}_{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{S}}\otimes{\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{A}})\underset{{\mathcal{A}}}{\otimes}{\mathcal{M}})\to\\
\to R_{\mathcal{C}}\operatorname{-mod}(\operatorname{Funct}_{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{S}}\otimes{\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{M}})),

and both above arrows are equivalences.

A.8.10.

In the sequel, we will make use of the following fact.

Proposition A.8.11
.

The
{\mathcal{A}}
-module category
\mathbf{P}^{{\mathcal{A}}}({\mathcal{C}})
is dualizable (as an
{\mathcal{A}}
-module category) with dual given by

\mathbf{P}^{{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{rev}}}({\mathcal{C}}^{\operatorname{op}})
.

Proof.

It suffices to establish an equivalence

\operatorname{Funct}_{{\mathcal{A}}}(\mathbf{P}^{{\mathcal{A}}}({\mathcal{C}}),{\mathcal{M}})\simeq\mathbf{P}^{{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{rev}}}({\mathcal{C}}^{\operatorname{op}})\underset{{\mathcal{A}}}{\otimes}{\mathcal{M}},\quad{\mathcal{M}}\in{\mathcal{A}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}.

We have

\operatorname{Funct}_{{\mathcal{A}}}(\mathbf{P}^{{\mathcal{A}}}({\mathcal{C}}),{\mathcal{M}})\simeq\operatorname{Funct}^{{\mathcal{A}}}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{M}})\overset{\text{\eqref{e:prshv and ten}}}{\simeq}\operatorname{Funct}^{{\mathcal{A}}}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{A}})\underset{{\mathcal{A}}}{\otimes}{\mathcal{M}}\simeq\mathbf{P}^{{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{rev}}}({\mathcal{C}}^{\operatorname{op}})\underset{{\mathcal{A}}}{\otimes}{\mathcal{M}},

as required.

∎

A.9.
Bousfield-Kan formula for enriched presheaves

In this subsection we continue to explore the point of view on enriched categories via quivers.
We use it to derive information about the category
\mathbf{P}^{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{C}})
.

A.9.1.

Our point of departure is formula (
A.21
). Hence, we obtain a monadic adjunction of
{\mathcal{A}}
-module categories

(A.23)

\operatorname{Funct}({\mathcal{S}},{\mathcal{A}})\simeq{\mathcal{S}}\otimes{\mathcal{A}}\rightleftarrows R_{\mathcal{C}}^{\operatorname{rev}}\operatorname{-mod}({\mathcal{S}}\otimes{\mathcal{A}})\simeq\mathbf{P}^{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{C}}).

The left adjoint in (
A.23
) is the
{\mathcal{A}}
-linear extension of the enriched
Yoneda embedding
\operatorname{Yon}^{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{C}}
. For the duration of this subsection, we denote it by
{\mathbf{i}}
.

In terms of the identification

{\mathcal{S}}\otimes{\mathcal{A}}\simeq\operatorname{Funct}({\mathcal{S}},{\mathcal{A}}),

the right adjoint in (
A.23
) is given by restriction along
\operatorname{Yon}^{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{C}}
.
For the duration of this subsection, we denote it by
{\mathbf{e}}
.

A.9.2.

From (
A.10
) we obtain that for
\Phi\in\operatorname{Funct}({\mathcal{S}},{\mathcal{A}})
,

(A.24)

{\mathbf{i}}(\Phi)\simeq\underset{s\in{\mathcal{S}}}{\operatorname{colim}}\,\Phi(s)\otimes\operatorname{Yon}^{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{C}}(s).

A.9.3.

Let now
\Psi
be an object
\mathbf{P}^{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{C}})
. From (
A.23
), we obtain that

\Psi
can be
canonically
written as a geometric realization of a simplicial object

\operatorname{BK}_{\bullet}(\Psi)
where

(A.25)

\operatorname{BK}_{n}(\Psi)={\mathbf{i}}((R_{\mathcal{C}}^{\operatorname{rev}})^{\otimes n}\otimes{\mathbf{e}}(\Psi)).

Combining with (
A.24
), we obtain:

Corollary A.9.4
.

Every object
\Psi\in\mathbf{P}^{{\mathcal{A}}}({\mathcal{C}})
is canonically a colimit of objects of the form

a\otimes\operatorname{Yon}^{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{C}}(c)\in\mathbf{P}^{{\mathcal{A}}}({\mathcal{C}}),

for
a\in{\mathcal{A}}
and
c\in{\mathcal{C}}
.

Remark A.9.5
.

The formula for
\operatorname{BK}_{n}(-)
can be made even more explicit. Namely, for any
\Phi\in\operatorname{Funct}({\mathcal{S}},{\mathcal{A}})\simeq{\mathcal{S}}\otimes{\mathcal{A}}

we have

(R_{\mathcal{C}}^{\operatorname{rev}})^{\otimes n}\otimes\Phi\simeq\underset{(s_{0},...,s_{n})\in({\mathcal{S}})^{n+1}}{\operatorname{colim}}\,\operatorname{Hom}^{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{C}}(s_{0},s_{1})\otimes...\otimes\operatorname{Hom}^{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{C}}(s_{n-1},s_{n})\otimes\Phi(s_{n})\otimes\delta_{s_{0}}.

Hence,

\operatorname{BK}_{n}(\Psi)\simeq\underset{(s_{0},...,s_{n})\in({\mathcal{S}})^{n+1}}{\operatorname{colim}}\,\operatorname{Hom}^{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{C}}(s_{0},s_{1})\otimes...\otimes\operatorname{Hom}^{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{C}}(s_{n-1},s_{n})\otimes\Psi(s_{n})\otimes\operatorname{Yon}^{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{C}}(s_{0}).

A.9.6.

We now return to the left adjoint of the functor
{}^{\prime}\operatorname{Yon}^{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{M}}
from Sect.
A.7.1
. We will now show
that the left-lax compatibility structure
({}^{\prime}\operatorname{Yon}^{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{M}})^{L}
with the action of
{\mathcal{A}}
is actually strict.

By Corollary
A.9.4
, in order to show both facts, it suffices to show
that
({}^{\prime}\operatorname{Yon}^{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{M}})^{L}
is defined and commutes with the action of
{\mathcal{A}}
on objects of the form
a\otimes\operatorname{Yon}^{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{M}}(m)
,
a\in{\mathcal{A}}
,
m\in{\mathcal{M}}
.

However, Corollary
A.6.5
implies that

({}^{\prime}\operatorname{Yon}^{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{M}})^{L}(a\otimes\operatorname{Yon}^{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{M}}(m))\simeq a\otimes m.

A.10.
Compatibility with the symmetric monoidal structure(s)

In this subsection we let
{\mathcal{A}}
be a
symmetric
monoidal category.

A.10.1.

When
{\mathcal{A}}
is
symmetric monoidal
(which is the case of interest in the present paper), the operation of Cartesian product of
the underlying plain categories extends to a symmetric monoidal structure on
\operatorname{Cat}^{{\mathcal{A}}}
:

For
{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime},{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime\prime}\in\operatorname{Cat}^{{\mathcal{A}}}
,
c^{\prime}_{1},c^{\prime}_{2}\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}
and
c^{\prime\prime}_{1},c^{\prime\prime}_{2}\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime\prime}
, we have

\operatorname{Hom}^{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}\times{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime\prime}}((c^{\prime}_{1},c^{\prime\prime}_{1}),(c^{\prime}_{2},c^{\prime\prime}_{2})):=\operatorname{Hom}^{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}}(c^{\prime}_{1},c^{\prime}_{2})\otimes\operatorname{Hom}^{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime\prime}}(c^{\prime\prime}_{1},c^{\prime\prime}_{2}).

Note, however, that this symmetric monoidal structure on
\operatorname{Cat}^{{\mathcal{A}}}
is
not
Cartesian.

Thus, we can talk about (symmetric) monoidal
{\mathcal{A}}
-enriched categories.

A.10.2.

let
F:{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\to{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
be a symmetric monoidal functor. Note that the functor
{\mathbf{ind}}^{{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}_{{\mathcal{A}}_{1}}

is (strictly) symmetric monoidal.

In particular, it sends (symmetric) monoidal
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}
-enriched categories to
(symmetric) monoidal
{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
-enriched categories.

Explicitly, if we lift
{\mathcal{C}}_{1}\in\operatorname{Cat}^{{\mathcal{A}}_{1}}
to a (commutative) algebra object in

({\mathcal{S}}_{1},{\mathcal{C}}_{1})\in\operatorname{Cat}^{{\mathcal{A}}_{1}}({\operatorname{Spc}})
, the corresponding (commutative) algebra object

({\mathcal{S}}_{2},{\mathcal{C}}_{2}):={\mathbf{ind}}^{{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}_{{\mathcal{A}}_{1}}({\mathcal{S}}_{1},{\mathcal{C}}_{1})
has the property that

{\mathcal{S}}_{2}={\mathcal{S}}_{1}={\mathcal{S}}
with the structure of (commutative) monoidal
coming from the (commutative) algebra structure on
({\mathcal{S}}_{1},{\mathcal{C}}_{1})
.

A.10.3.

Assume now that
{\mathcal{A}}
is presentable. Note that in this case
the functor
\operatorname{Mod-to-Enr}
has a natural right-lax symmetric monoidal structure.

By adjunction, we obtain that the functor

{\mathcal{C}}\mapsto\mathbf{P}^{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{C}})

acquires a left-lax symmetric monoidal structure.

However, from (
A.21
), we obtain that the above left-lax symmetric monoidal structure
is strict.

A.10.4.

Concretely, this means that for
{\mathcal{C}}_{1},{\mathcal{C}}_{2}
there is a naturally defined
{\mathcal{A}}
-bilinear functor

\mathbf{P}^{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{C}}_{1})\times\mathbf{P}^{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{C}}_{2})\to\mathbf{P}^{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{C}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{C}}_{2}),

such that the induced morphism in
{\mathcal{A}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}
:

\mathbf{P}^{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{C}}_{1})\underset{{\mathcal{A}}}{\otimes}\mathbf{P}^{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{C}}_{2})\to\mathbf{P}^{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{C}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{C}}_{2})

is an equivalence.

A.10.5.

In particular, we obtain that if
{\mathcal{C}}
is a symmetric monoidal
{\mathcal{A}}
-enriched category, then

\mathbf{P}^{{\mathcal{A}}}({\mathcal{C}})
acquires a natural symmetric monoidal structure as an object of
{\mathcal{A}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}
.

The Yoneda functor, viewed as a natural transformation

\operatorname{Id}\to\operatorname{Mod-to-Enr}\circ\mathbf{P}^{\mathcal{A}}(-)

between right-lax symmetric monoidal functors, itself has a natural symmetric monoidal structure.

A.10.6.

In particular, if
{\mathcal{C}}
is a symmetric monoidal
{\mathcal{A}}
-enriched category, the Yoneda functor

\operatorname{Yon}^{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{C}}:{\mathcal{C}}\to\mathbf{P}^{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{C}})

has a natural symmetric monoidal structure (as a functor between symmetric monoidal
{\mathcal{A}}
-enriched categories).

In particular, the plain functor underlying
\operatorname{Yon}^{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{C}}
has a natural symmetric monoidal structure.

Appendix B
Weighted limits and colimits

In this section, we let

{\mathcal{A}},\,\,\operatorname{Cat}^{\mathcal{A}},\,\,{\mathcal{A}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}

be as in Sect.
A.4
.

B.1.
Weighted limits

The usual notion of limits and colimits in not sufficient in the setting of enriched categories.
For this, we need the notion of weighted limits and colimits. We consider several variants of these.

B.1.1.

For
{\mathcal{C}}\in\operatorname{Cat}^{\mathcal{A}}
let

{\mathcal{W}}:{\mathcal{C}}\to{\mathcal{A}}

be an
{\mathcal{A}}
-functor. Given
{\mathcal{M}}\in{\mathcal{A}}{\operatorname{-}\!{\mathbf{mod}}}
and an
{\mathcal{A}}
-functor

\Phi:{\mathcal{C}}\to{\mathcal{M}},

we define the weighted limit of
F
with weight
{\mathcal{W}}

\underset{{\mathcal{C}}}{\operatorname{lim}}^{\mathcal{W}}\,\Phi\in{\mathcal{M}}

as follows.

B.1.2.

Using the functor
{\mathcal{W}}
, we define the functor

{\mathcal{W}}\otimes(-):{\mathcal{M}}\to\operatorname{Funct}^{{\mathcal{A}}}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{M}})

informally given by

m\mapsto(c\mapsto{\mathcal{W}}(c)\otimes m).

Formally,
{\mathcal{W}}\otimes(-)
is defined as the composite

(B.1)

{\mathcal{M}}\overset{{\mathcal{W}}\otimes\operatorname{id}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Funct}^{{\mathcal{A}}}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{A}})\underset{{\mathcal{A}}}{\otimes}{\mathcal{M}}\overset{\sim}{\to}\operatorname{Funct}^{{\mathcal{A}}}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{M}}).

Now given
\Phi\in\operatorname{Funct}^{{\mathcal{A}}}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{M}})
, the weighted limit
\underset{{\mathcal{C}}}{\operatorname{lim}}^{\mathcal{W}}

is defined as the value of the right adjoint to
Equation

B.1
, which exists by the Adjoint Functor
Theorem (
[
Lu1
, Corollary 5.5.2.9]
) (indeed, (
B.1
) is a colimit-preserving functor between
presentable categories).

B.1.3.

Explicitly, for
m\in{\mathcal{M}}

(B.2)

\operatorname{Maps}_{{\mathcal{M}}}(m,\underset{{\mathcal{C}}}{\operatorname{lim}}^{\mathcal{W}}\,\Phi)\simeq\operatorname{Maps}_{\operatorname{Funct}^{{\mathcal{A}}}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{M}})}({\mathcal{W}}(-)\otimes m,\Phi).

B.1.4.

It is clear that the formation of weighted limits is functorial in
F
. Moreover,
from
Equation

B.2
