, we obtain that the formation of weighted colimits is contravariantly functorial in
{\mathcal{W}}
.
Moreover, we have:

Lemma B.1.5
.

Let

{\mathcal{W}}_{I}:I\to\operatorname{Funct}^{{\mathcal{A}}}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{A}}),\quad i\mapsto{\mathcal{W}}_{i}

be a (small) diagram of weights, and let
{\mathcal{W}}=\underset{i\in I}{\operatorname{colim}}\ {\mathcal{W}}_{i}
.
Then for an
{\mathcal{A}}
-functor
\Phi:{\mathcal{C}}\to{\mathcal{M}}
there is a canonical isomorphism

\underset{{\mathcal{C}}}{\operatorname{lim}}^{\mathcal{W}}\,\Phi\simeq\underset{i\in I^{\operatorname{op}}}{\operatorname{lim}}\,\underset{{\mathcal{C}}}{\operatorname{lim}}^{{\mathcal{W}}_{i}}\,\Phi.

B.1.6.

Suppose that
{\mathcal{C}}=*
(i.e. it only has one object and the object of morphisms is
\mathbb{1}_{{\mathcal{A}}}
). In this case,
{\mathcal{W}}
is given
by an object
a\in{\mathcal{A}}
and
\Phi
is given by an object
m\in{\mathcal{M}}
. In this case, the weighted limit

\underset{{\mathcal{C}}}{\operatorname{lim}}^{\mathcal{W}}\,\Phi\simeq m^{a}

is the
cotensor
of
m
by
a
; i.e.

\operatorname{Maps}_{{\mathcal{M}}}(m^{\prime},m^{a})\simeq\operatorname{Maps}(a\otimes m^{\prime},m).

In particular, if
{\mathcal{M}}={\mathcal{A}}
and
m=a^{\prime}
, we have

\underset{{\mathcal{C}}}{\operatorname{lim}}^{\mathcal{W}}\,\Phi\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathcal{A}}(a,a^{\prime})

is the internal hom object.

B.1.7.

As in Lemma
B.1.5
we have

(B.3)

(\underset{{\mathcal{C}}}{\operatorname{lim}}^{\mathcal{W}}\,\Phi)^{a}\simeq\underset{{\mathcal{C}}}{\operatorname{lim}}^{a\otimes{\mathcal{W}}}\,\Phi,\quad a\in{\mathcal{A}},

where
a\otimes{\mathcal{W}}
is obtained from
{\mathcal{W}}
be tensoring with
a
on the target.

B.1.8.

Another key example of a weighted limit is when the weight is representable. Namely, let

{\mathcal{W}}\in\operatorname{Funct}^{{\mathcal{A}}}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{A}})

be of the form
\operatorname{Yon}^{{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{rev}}}_{{\mathcal{C}}^{\operatorname{op}}}(c)
for
c\in{\mathcal{C}}
.

Proposition B.1.9
.

In the above situation,

\underset{{\mathcal{C}}}{\operatorname{lim}}^{\mathcal{W}}\,\Phi\simeq\Phi(c)

for any
\Phi\in\operatorname{Funct}^{{\mathcal{A}}}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{M}})
.

Proof.

By Sect.
A.9.1
, we have an adjunction of
{\mathcal{A}}
-module categories

\textstyle{(-)\otimes\operatorname{Yon}^{{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{rev}}}_{{\mathcal{C}}^{\operatorname{op}}}(c):{\mathcal{A}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\operatorname{Funct}^{{\mathcal{A}}}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{A}}):{\mathbf{e}}_{c},}

where
{\mathbf{e}}_{c}
is the functor of evaluation at
c\in{\mathcal{C}}
functor, i.e.,
{\mathbf{e}}_{c}(\Phi)\simeq\Phi(c)
.
The desired result is obtained by applying the functor
(-)\underset{{\mathcal{A}}}{\otimes}{\mathcal{M}}
to this adjunction
and using (
A.22
).
∎

B.1.10.
Unenriched weighted limits

A useful class of examples of weighted limits is when the indexing category
{\mathcal{C}}
is of the form

{\mathcal{C}}=\operatorname{Enr}_{{\mathcal{A}}}(I)
for
I\in\operatorname{Cat}
.

In this case, we have

\operatorname{Funct}^{{\mathcal{A}}}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{M}})\simeq\operatorname{Funct}(I,{\mathcal{M}})

for any
{\mathcal{A}}
-module category
{\mathcal{M}}
. We will sometimes refer to weighted limits with such indexing category as unenriched weighted limits.

There are two important classes of unenriched weighted limits. When
I=\{\operatorname{pt}\}
, this gives the cotensor as in
Sect.
B.1.6
.

Another example is when the weight
{\mathcal{W}}:I\to{\mathcal{A}}
is the constant functor
\mathbb{1}_{{\mathcal{A}}}
.
In this case, the weighted limit of a functor
\Phi:I\to{\mathcal{M}}
is the ordinary limit

\underset{{\mathcal{C}}}{\operatorname{lim}}^{\mathcal{W}}\,\Phi\simeq\underset{I}{\operatorname{lim}}\,\Phi

in the category
{\mathcal{M}}
.

B.2.
Further properties of weighted limits

B.2.1.

An important aspect of weighted limits is that they are preserved by right adjoints. Namely, suppose we have
an
{\mathcal{A}}
-module functor

F:{\mathcal{M}}_{1}\to{\mathcal{M}}_{2}.

Then for any
{\mathcal{A}}
-enriched category
{\mathcal{C}}
, weight
{\mathcal{W}}:{\mathcal{C}}\to{\mathcal{A}}
, and an
{\mathcal{A}}
-functor

\Phi:{\mathcal{C}}\to{\mathcal{M}}_{1},

from the definition, we have a natural map

(B.4)

F(\underset{{\mathcal{C}}}{\operatorname{lim}}^{\mathcal{W}}\,\Phi)\to\underset{{\mathcal{C}}}{\operatorname{lim}}^{\mathcal{W}}\,(F\circ\Phi)

Lemma B.2.2
.

Suppose that in the above situation, the functor
F
admits a left adjoint which is a
strict
11
11

11

Recall that a left adjoint of an
{\mathcal{A}}
-module functor is automatically
{\mathcal{A}}
left-lax and being strict is then a condition, see
[
GR1
, Chapter 1, Lemma 3.2.4]

{\mathcal{A}}
-module functor. Then
F
preserves weighted limits; i.e. the map
Equation

B.4
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

The existence of an
{\mathcal{A}}
-module left adjoint
F^{L}
gives rise to the following commutative diagram

\textstyle{{\mathcal{M}}_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{F^{L}}
\scriptstyle{{\mathcal{W}}\otimes\operatorname{id}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{2}}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{M}}_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\mathcal{W}}\otimes\operatorname{id}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{1}}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Funct}^{{\mathcal{A}}}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{A}})\underset{{\mathcal{A}}}{\otimes}{\mathcal{M}}_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{id}\otimes F^{L}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Funct}^{{\mathcal{A}}}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{A}})\underset{{\mathcal{A}}}{\otimes}{\mathcal{M}}_{1}}

of
{\mathcal{A}}
-module categories.
Evaluating the corresponding commutative diagram obtained by passing to right adjoints on

\Phi\in\operatorname{Funct}^{{\mathcal{A}}}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{A}})\underset{{\mathcal{A}}}{\otimes}{\mathcal{M}}_{1}\simeq\operatorname{Funct}^{{\mathcal{A}}}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{M}}_{1})

gives the desired result.
∎

B.2.3.

We have the following basic result decomposing weighted limits into simpler ones.

Proposition B.2.4
.

An
{\mathcal{A}}
-module functor
F:{\mathcal{M}}\to{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}
preserves weighted limits if and only if it preserves all limits and cotensors.

Proof.

The “only if” direction is obvious as limits and cotensors are special cases of weighted limits.

Let
{\mathcal{C}}
be an
{\mathcal{A}}
-enriched category, and

{\mathcal{W}}\in\operatorname{Funct}^{{\mathcal{A}}}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{A}})\simeq\mathbf{P}^{{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{rev}}}({\mathcal{C}}^{\operatorname{op}})

be a weight. By Corollary
A.9.4
,
{\mathcal{W}}
is the colimit of objects in
\mathbf{P}^{{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{rev}}}({\mathcal{C}}^{\operatorname{op}})
of the form

a\otimes\operatorname{Yon}^{{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{rev}}}_{{\mathcal{C}}^{\operatorname{op}}}(c)

for
a\in{\mathcal{A}}
and
c\in{\mathcal{C}}
.

Hence, by Lemma
B.1.5
, it suffices to consider
{\mathcal{W}}
of this form. Further, by (
B.3
),
it suffices to consider
{\mathcal{W}}
of the form
\operatorname{Yon}^{{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{rev}}}_{{\mathcal{C}}^{\operatorname{op}}}(c)
.

Now the assertion follows from Proposition
B.1.9
.

∎

B.2.5.

We can make the decomposition of weighted limits into ordinary limits and cotensors explicit using Sect.
A.9.3
and
and Remark
A.9.5
:

Let

{\mathcal{W}}\in\operatorname{Funct}^{{\mathcal{A}}}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{A}})\simeq\mathbf{P}^{{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{rev}}}({\mathcal{C}}^{\operatorname{op}})

be a weight functor. By Sect.
A.9.3
,
{\mathcal{W}}
is the geometric realization of the simplicial object
\operatorname{BK}_{\bullet}({\mathcal{W}})
,

\operatorname{BK}_{n}({\mathcal{W}})\simeq{\mathbf{i}}((\tau(R_{\mathcal{C}}))^{\otimes n}\otimes{\mathbf{e}}({\mathcal{W}}))\simeq\\
\simeq\underset{(s_{0},...,s_{n})\in{\mathcal{S}}^{n+1}}{\operatorname{colim}}\,\operatorname{Hom}^{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{C}}(s_{0},s_{1})\otimes...\otimes\operatorname{Hom}^{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{C}}(s_{n-1},s_{n})\otimes{\mathcal{W}}(s_{0})\otimes\operatorname{Yon}^{{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{rev}}}_{{\mathcal{C}}^{\operatorname{op}}}(s_{n}).

Hence,

\underset{{\mathcal{C}}}{\operatorname{lim}}^{\mathcal{W}}\,\Phi\simeq\operatorname{Tot}(\underset{{\mathcal{C}}}{\operatorname{lim}}^{\operatorname{BK}_{\bullet}({\mathcal{W}})}\,\Phi),

where

\underset{{\mathcal{C}}}{\operatorname{lim}}^{\operatorname{BK}_{n}({\mathcal{W}})}\,\Phi\simeq\underset{(s_{0},...,s_{n})\in{\mathcal{S}}^{n+1}}{\operatorname{lim}}\,\Phi(s_{n})^{\operatorname{Hom}^{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{C}}(s_{0},s_{1})\otimes...\otimes\operatorname{Hom}^{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{C}}(s_{n-1},s_{n})\otimes{\mathcal{W}}(s_{0})}.

B.3.
Weighted colimits

The notion of a weighted colimit is dual to that of a weighted limit.
However, because our notion of
{\mathcal{A}}
-module categories is built on the symmetric monoidal category of
presentable categories, there is some asymmetry in the discussion that follows.

B.3.1.

Let
{\mathcal{C}}
be
{\mathcal{A}}
-enriched category and

\Phi:{\mathcal{C}}\to{\mathcal{M}}

be an
{\mathcal{A}}
-enriched functor, and an
{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{rev}}
-functor
{\mathcal{W}}:{\mathcal{C}}^{\operatorname{op}}\to{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{rev}}
, a “weight.”
We can think of
{\mathcal{W}}
as an object of
\mathbf{P}^{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{C}})
.

By the universal property of the Yoneda embedding,
the functor
\Phi
induces a functor of
{\mathcal{A}}
-module categories

\widetilde{\Phi}:\mathbf{P}^{{\mathcal{A}}}({\mathcal{C}})\to{\mathcal{M}}.

Following
[
Hi2
, Sect. 6]
, we define

\underset{{\mathcal{C}}}{\operatorname{colim}}^{\mathcal{W}}\,\Phi:=\widetilde{\Phi}({\mathcal{W}}).

B.3.2.

Let us describe the above construction more explicitly. Namely, we claim:

Proposition B.3.3
.

We have a canonical isomorphism

\operatorname{Maps}_{\mathcal{M}}(\underset{{\mathcal{C}}}{\operatorname{colim}}^{\mathcal{W}}\,\Phi,m)\simeq\operatorname{Maps}_{\operatorname{Funct}^{{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{rev}}}({\mathcal{C}}^{\operatorname{op}},{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{rev}})}({\mathcal{W}},\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{{\mathcal{M}},{\mathcal{A}}}(\Phi(-),m)).

Proof.

The statement of the proposition is equivalent to the assertion that the functor right adjoint to

\widetilde{\Phi}
sends
m\in{\mathcal{M}}
to the object

\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{{\mathcal{M}},{\mathcal{A}}}(\Phi(-),m)\in\operatorname{Funct}^{{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{rev}}}({\mathcal{C}}^{\operatorname{op}},{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{rev}})=\mathbf{P}^{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{C}}).

By the (Yoneda) Lemma
A.6.3
,
given
F\in\mathbf{P}^{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{C}})
, the
{\mathcal{A}}
-functor
{\mathcal{C}}^{\operatorname{op}}\to{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{rev}}
corresponding to it is given by

c\mapsto\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathbf{P}^{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{C}}),{\mathcal{A}}}(\operatorname{Yon}^{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{C}}(c),F).

Hence, for
F:=(\widetilde{\Phi})^{R}(m)
, the corresponding
{\mathcal{A}}
-functor
{\mathcal{C}}^{\operatorname{op}}\to{\mathcal{A}}^{\operatorname{rev}}
is

\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathbf{P}^{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{C}}),{\mathcal{A}}}(\operatorname{Yon}^{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{C}}(c),(\widetilde{\Phi})^{R}(m)),

which by adjunction identifies with

\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{{\mathcal{M}},{\mathcal{A}}}(\widetilde{\Phi}(\operatorname{Yon}^{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{C}}(c)),m).

However, by construction

\widetilde{\Phi}(\operatorname{Yon}^{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{C}}(-))=\Phi(-),

and the assertion follows.

∎

B.3.4.

By definition, weighted colimits commute with colimits in the weight. Namely, if we have a diagram

{\mathcal{W}}_{I}:I\to\mathbf{P}^{{\mathcal{A}}}({\mathcal{C}}),\quad i\mapsto{\mathcal{W}}_{i}

of weights, for any functor
\Phi:{\mathcal{C}}\to{\mathcal{M}}
, we have a natural isomorphism

\underset{i\in I}{\operatorname{colim}}\,\,\underset{{\mathcal{C}}}{\operatorname{colim}}^{{\mathcal{W}}_{i}}\,\Phi\simeq\underset{{\mathcal{C}}}{\operatorname{colim}}^{{\mathcal{W}}}\,\Phi

where
{\mathcal{W}}=\underset{i\in I}{\operatorname{colim}}\,{\mathcal{W}}_{i}
.

B.3.5.

In the case that
{\mathcal{C}}=*
with
{\mathcal{W}}
given by
a\in{\mathcal{A}}
and
\Phi
by
m\in{\mathcal{M}}
, we have:

\underset{{\mathcal{C}}}{\operatorname{colim}}^{\mathcal{W}}\,\Phi\simeq a\otimes m.

Moreover, for a general
{\mathcal{C}}
and
{\mathcal{W}}=\operatorname{Yon}^{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{C}}(c)
,
c\in{\mathcal{C}}
, we have

\underset{{\mathcal{C}}}{\operatorname{colim}}^{\mathcal{W}}\,\Phi\simeq\Phi(c).

B.3.6.

As in the case of weighted limits, we can describe weighted colimits explicitly in terms of ordinary limits and tensors
using Sect.
A.9.3
as a geometric realization:

\underset{{\mathcal{C}}}{\operatorname{colim}}^{\mathcal{W}}\,\Phi\simeq|\underset{{\mathcal{C}}}{\operatorname{colim}}^{\operatorname{BK}_{\bullet}({\mathcal{W}})}\,\Phi|,

where
\underset{{\mathcal{C}}}{\operatorname{colim}}^{\operatorname{BK}_{\bullet}({\mathcal{W}})}\,\Phi
is the simplicial object in
{\mathcal{M}}
with terms given by

\underset{(c_{0},...,c_{n})\in{\mathcal{S}}^{n+1}}{\operatorname{colim}}\,\operatorname{Hom}^{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\mathcal{C}}(c_{0},c_{1})\otimes...\otimes\operatorname{Hom}^{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\mathcal{C}}(c_{n-1},c_{n})\otimes{\mathcal{W}}(c_{n})\otimes\Phi(c_{0}).

B.3.7.

It follows immediately from the definition of weighted colimits that all
{\mathcal{A}}
-module functors

{\mathcal{M}}_{1}\to{\mathcal{M}}_{2}

preserve weighted colimits.

B.4.
Weighted colimits and enriched presheaves

B.4.1.

An key feature of weighted colimits is that every object in
\mathbf{P}^{{\mathcal{A}}}({\mathcal{C}})
is canonically a weighted colimit.
Namely, by definition, we have that for
F\in\mathbf{P}^{{\mathcal{A}}}({\mathcal{C}})
,

(B.5)

F\simeq\underset{{\mathcal{C}}}{\operatorname{colim}}^{F}\,\operatorname{Yon}^{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{C}},

where
\operatorname{Yon}^{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{C}}:{\mathcal{C}}\to\mathbf{P}^{{\mathcal{A}}}({\mathcal{C}})
is the enriched Yoneda embedding.

B.4.2.

We will say that an object
m\in{\mathcal{M}}
is
totally
{\mathcal{A}}
-compact
if the functor

\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{{\mathcal{M}},{\mathcal{A}}}(m,-):{\mathcal{M}}\to{\mathcal{A}}

preserves all weighted colimits.

B.4.3.

We have the following evident characterization of totally
{\mathcal{A}}
-compact objects:

Lemma B.4.4
.

Let
{\mathcal{M}}
be an
{\mathcal{A}}
-module category and
m\in{\mathcal{M}}
. The following are equivalent:

(i)
m
is totally
{\mathcal{A}}
-compact;

(ii) The functor
(-)\otimes m:{\mathcal{A}}\to{\mathcal{M}}

admits an
{\mathcal{A}}
-module right adjoint
12
12

12

I.e., we need that the right adjoint as a plain functor
preserves colimits, and that the natural stricture on it of right-lax compatibility with the action of
{\mathcal{A}}

be strict.
;

(iii)
\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{{\mathcal{M}},{\mathcal{A}}}(m,-):{\mathcal{M}}\to{\mathcal{A}}

is an
{\mathcal{A}}
-module functor.

B.4.5.

Note that the essential image of the enriched Yoneda functor

\operatorname{Yon}^{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{C}}:{\mathcal{C}}\to\mathbf{P}^{{\mathcal{A}}}({\mathcal{C}})

consists of totally
{\mathcal{A}}
-compact objects.

Indeed, this follows from (i)
\Leftrightarrow
(iii) in Lemma
B.4.4
and
Lemma
A.6.3
.

B.4.6.

The following assertion is useful:

Proposition B.4.7
.

Let
{\mathcal{C}}
be an
{\mathcal{A}}
-enriched category and let

\widetilde{\Phi}:\mathbf{P}^{{\mathcal{A}}}({\mathcal{C}})\to{\mathcal{M}}

be an
{\mathcal{A}}
-module functor such that:

(i)
\widetilde{\Phi}
is fully faithful in the enriched sense
13
13

13

I.e., it induces an isomorphism on
\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{-,{\mathcal{A}}}(-,-)
.

on the essential image of the enriched Yoneda functor;

(ii) For every
c\in{\mathcal{C}}
, the object
\Phi(c)\in{\mathcal{M}}
is totally compact, where

\Phi:=\widetilde{\Phi}\circ\operatorname{Yon}^{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{C}}
.

Then
\widetilde{\Phi}
is fully faithful.

Proof.

Since
\mathbf{P}^{{\mathcal{A}}}({\mathcal{C}})
is generated under weighted colimits by the objects
\operatorname{Yon}^{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{C}}(c)

for
c\in{\mathcal{C}}
, and
\widetilde{\Phi}
preserves weighted colimits, by Proposition
B.3.3
,
it suffices to show that
for
c\in{\mathcal{C}}
and
F\in\mathbf{P}^{{\mathcal{A}}}({\mathcal{C}})
, the map

(B.6)

\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathbf{P}^{{\mathcal{A}}}({\mathcal{C}}),{\mathcal{A}}}(\operatorname{Yon}^{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{C}}(c),F)\to\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{{\mathcal{M}},{\mathcal{A}}}(\widetilde{\Phi}\circ\operatorname{Yon}^{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{C}}(c),\widetilde{\Phi}(F))

is an isomorphism.

Now, by
Equation

B.5
, we have

F\simeq\underset{c^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}}}{\operatorname{colim}}^{F}\,\operatorname{Yon}^{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{C}}(c^{\prime}).

Since both sides in (
B.6
) preserve weighted colimits in
F
, we are reduced to showing that

\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathbf{P}^{{\mathcal{A}}}({\mathcal{C}}),{\mathcal{A}}}(\operatorname{Yon}^{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{C}}(c),\operatorname{Yon}^{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{C}}(c^{\prime}))\to\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{{\mathcal{M}},{\mathcal{A}}}(\widetilde{\Phi}\circ\operatorname{Yon}^{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{C}}(c),\widetilde{\Phi}\circ\operatorname{Yon}^{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{C}}(c^{\prime}))

is an isomorphism, which follows from the assumption.

∎

Corollary B.4.8
.

Let
{\mathcal{C}}_{1}\to{\mathcal{C}}_{2}
be a functor of
{\mathcal{A}}
-enriched categories that is fully faithful in
the enriched sense
14
14

14

I.e., it induces an isomorphism on
\operatorname{Hom}^{\mathcal{A}}(-,-)
.
.
Then the corresponding functor

\mathbf{P}^{{\mathcal{A}}}({\mathcal{C}}_{1})\to\mathbf{P}^{{\mathcal{A}}}({\mathcal{C}}_{2})

is fully faithful.

Corollary B.4.9
.

Let
{\mathcal{C}}
be an
{\mathcal{A}}
-enriched category. An
{\mathcal{A}}
-module functor

\widetilde{\Phi}:\mathbf{P}^{{\mathcal{A}}}({\mathcal{C}})\to{\mathcal{M}}

is an equivalence if and only if

(i)
\widetilde{\Phi}
is fully faithful in the enriched sense on essential image of the enriched Yoneda functor;

(ii) For every
c\in{\mathcal{C}}
, the object
\Phi(c)\in{\mathcal{M}}
is totally compact, where

\Phi:=\widetilde{\Phi}\circ\operatorname{Yon}^{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathcal{C}}
;

(iii) The image of
{\mathcal{C}}
generates
{\mathcal{M}}
under weighted colimits.

Appendix C
Adjunctions

C.1.
Adjunctions in 2-categories

C.1.1.

Let
{\mathbf{S}}
be a 2-category. Recall that a 1-morphism
f:x\to y
in
{\mathbf{S}}
admits a right (resp. left) adjoint if there exists another 1-morphism

f^{R}:y\to x\mbox{ (resp. }f^{L}:y\to x\mbox{)}

and 2-morphisms

f^{L}\circ f\to\operatorname{id}_{x}\ \ \mbox{(resp. }f\circ f^{R}\to\operatorname{id}_{y}\mbox{), and}

\operatorname{id}_{y}\to f\circ f^{L}\ \ \mbox{(resp. }\operatorname{id}_{x}\to f^{R}\circ f\mbox{)}

satisfying the standard relations.

C.1.2.

Let
\operatorname{Adj}
denote the universal adjunction, i.e., this is a 2-category such that the
space
of functors

\operatorname{Adj}\to{\mathbf{S}}

for any 2-category
{\mathbf{S}}
is the space of adjunctions in
{\mathbf{S}}
, i.e. the subspace of 1-morphisms of
{\mathbf{S}}
that admit a right adjoint.
There are natural functors

(C.1)

\rho,\lambda:[1]\to\operatorname{Adj}

selecting the right and left adjoint, repectively.

For a 2-category
{\mathbf{S}}
, we will be interested in a description of the 2-category of functors

\operatorname{Funct}(\operatorname{Adj},{\mathbf{S}}).

C.1.3.

Recall the following notion. Suppose that

(C.2)

\textstyle{x\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\scriptstyle{p}
\textstyle{y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{q}
\textstyle{z\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g}
\textstyle{w}

is a commutative square in
{\mathbf{S}}
. We say that
Equation

C.2
satisfies the right (resp. left) Beck-Chevalley condition if the 1-morphisms
f
and
g
admit right (resp. left) adjoints and the corresponding 2-morphism

g^{L}\circ q\to p\circ f^{L}\mbox{ (resp. }p\circ f^{R}\to g^{R}\circ q\mbox{)}

is an isomorphism.

C.1.4.

We have the following basic fact (see e.g.
[
AMR
, Lemma B.5.9]
and
[
AGH
, Theorem D]
):

Proposition C.1.5
.

Let
{\mathbf{S}}
be a 2-category and let

(C.3)

\operatorname{Funct}(\operatorname{Adj},{\mathbf{S}})\to\operatorname{Funct}([1],{\mathbf{S}})

denote the restriction along the right (resp. left) adjoint
Equation

C.1
. The functor
Equation

C.3

is 1-full:

(a) Its essential image consists of objects
x\to y
that admit a right (resp. left) adjoint.

(b) A 1-morphism

(C.4)

\textstyle{x\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{x^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{y^{\prime}}

between two such objects
x\to y
and
x^{\prime}\to y^{\prime}
lies in the image iff the square
Equation

C.4
satisfies the right (resp. left) Beck-Chevalley condition.

C.2.
Adjunctions in enriched presheaves

C.2.1.

Let
{\mathcal{A}}
be a presentable monoidal category.

We will be interested in the case when
{\mathcal{A}}
has the structure of 2-category (e.g.
{\mathcal{A}}=\operatorname{DGCat}_{\kappa}
) and the resulting
2-categorical aspects of
{\mathcal{A}}
-module categories.

Namely, suppose that
{\mathcal{A}}
is itself tensored over
\operatorname{Cat}^{\operatorname{pres}}_{\kappa}
(for some regular cardinal
\kappa
), i.e.,
{\mathcal{A}}
is a
\operatorname{Cat}^{\operatorname{pres}}_{\kappa}
-algebra
in categories
\operatorname{Cat}^{\operatorname{pres}}
. In particular, this endows every
{\mathcal{A}}
-module category with the structure of
2-category.
15
15

15

Since
\operatorname{Cat}^{\operatorname{pres}}_{\kappa}
is presentable, it follows that every
\operatorname{Cat}^{\operatorname{pres}}_{\kappa}
-module category in
\operatorname{Cat}^{\operatorname{pres}}

has
\operatorname{Cat}^{\operatorname{pres}}_{\kappa}
-valued homs.

Proposition C.2.2
.

Let
{\mathcal{C}}
be an
{\mathcal{A}}
-enriched category and let
{\mathcal{M}}
be an
{\mathcal{A}}
-module category. A 1-morphism
f:\Phi_{1}\to\Phi_{2}
in
\operatorname{Funct}^{{\mathcal{A}}}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{M}})

admits a right (resp. left) adjoint if and only if the following two conditions are satisfied:

(i)
For every
c\in{\mathcal{C}}
, the 1-morphism

f(c):\Phi_{1}(c)\to\Phi_{2}(c)

in
{\mathcal{M}}
admits a right (resp. left) adjoint;

(ii)
For all
c^{\prime},c^{\prime\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}}
, the square

\textstyle{\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{{\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{A}}}(c^{\prime},c^{\prime\prime})\otimes\Phi_{1}(c^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{{\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{A}}}(c^{\prime},c^{\prime\prime})\otimes\Phi_{2}(c^{\prime\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Phi_{1}(c^{\prime\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Phi_{2}(c^{\prime\prime})}

satisfies the right (resp. left) Beck-Chevalley condition.

Proof.

We will prove the assertion for right adjoints (the case of left adjoints is identical). The 1-morphism
f
is given by a functor

[1]\to\operatorname{Funct}^{{\mathcal{A}}}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{M}})\simeq\operatorname{Funct}^{{\mathcal{A}}}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{A}})\underset{{\mathcal{A}}}{\otimes}{\mathcal{M}}.

By
A.8.11
, such a functor is given by a functor of
{\mathcal{A}}
-module categories

(C.5)

\mathbf{P}^{{\mathcal{A}}}({\mathcal{C}})\to\operatorname{Funct}([1],{\mathcal{M}}).

The 1-morphism
f
admits a right adjoint iff the functor
Equation

C.5
factors through the functor

(C.6)

\operatorname{Funct}(\operatorname{Adj},{\mathcal{M}})\to\operatorname{Funct}([1],{\mathcal{M}}),

which is 1-full by
C.1.5
.

By Proposition
A.5.6
, we have

\operatorname{Funct}_{{\mathcal{A}}}(\mathbf{P}^{{\mathcal{A}}}({\mathcal{C}}),\operatorname{Funct}([1],{\mathcal{M}}))\simeq\operatorname{Funct}^{{\mathcal{A}}}({\mathcal{C}},\operatorname{Funct}([1],{\mathcal{M}})).

Now, the 1-morphism
f
admits a right adjoint if and only if the corresponding object in the category
\operatorname{Funct}^{{\mathcal{A}}}({\mathcal{C}},\operatorname{Funct}([1],{\mathcal{M}}))
lies in
the subcategory
\operatorname{Funct}^{{\mathcal{A}}}({\mathcal{C}},\operatorname{Funct}(\operatorname{Adj},{\mathcal{M}}))
.
By
A.5.9
and
C.1.5
, the functor

\operatorname{Funct}^{{\mathcal{A}}}({\mathcal{C}},\operatorname{Funct}(\operatorname{Adj},{\mathcal{M}}))\to\operatorname{Funct}^{{\mathcal{A}}}({\mathcal{C}},\operatorname{Funct}([1],{\mathcal{M}}))

is 1-full and the essential image is as claimed.

∎
